# Blister 2024-2025 Winter Buyer's Guide (Tenth Edition) -- Notes

Source: txt/2024-2025.clean (15,216 lines). Notes written incrementally while reading sequentially. Specs format: measured dims (length measured), stated radius, measured weight per ski, tip/camber/tail profile, measured length, mount point (cm from true center / cm from tail), lengths, core.

## 1. Structure, editorial framing

- Cover: Luke Koppa on the Fischer Curv GT 85 at Taos (photo Kristin Sinnott). Tenth edition.
- Ads: Elevation Resort (Mt. Crested Butte), Blister Summit 2025 (Feb 23-27, 2025, Mt. Crested Butte: 4 days of demos incl. next year's unreleased gear, guided BC tours, panels), Blister+ membership (12-month $0-deductible $25k/incident injury insurance, 1-on-1 gear recs, deals, buyer's guide, Flash Reviews, Deep Dive comparisons, new Resort Guides, Summit discount), Lone Pine Gear Exchange (SLC), Blister Recommended Shops page.
- "About this guide" (Jonathan Ellsworth, Founder & Editor): guide is a digestible shortcut to 1500-3000-word online reviews. Blister takes no ad money from manufacturers whose gear they review; reviewers spend multiple days in varied conditions on each product. Nearly every person pictured is a reviewer (some Blister Summit 2024 attendees). Blister Spectrums rank skis within each class relative to one another (close = similar; several spots apart = meaningful). Clickable READ MORE links, Gear 101, glossary. "Think before you buy": many older products still perform well (many Best Of picks unchanged for years); environmental impact of new gear -> keep using gear you're happy with. Skis in each section arranged narrowest to widest. No MSRP listed (misleading).
- "How to interpret our specs" (example: K2 Mindbender 99Ti 184 cm: 133.3-98.3-119.3 mm; 19.6 m; 2085 & 2120 g; 60/2.5/26 mm profile; mount -10.6 cm / 81.4 cm from tail; aspen + titanal "Y-Beam" + fiberglass). Measured dims = widest shovel, narrowest waist, widest tail; weights of both skis; profile = tip splay / camber underfoot / tail splay when decambered; measured length = straight tip-to-tail tape pull; mount point relative to true center (measured length / 2), and from tail (topsheet graphics can shift; measure from tail).
- Table of contents: Articles -- 6 About This Guide; 12 Letter from the Editor; 198 Ski-Quiver Selections; 271 'Best Of' Awards; 279 Index of Skis. Product reviews -- 14 50/50 Backcountry & Resort; 29 Backcountry Touring; 47 Women's All-Mountain More Stable; 62 Women's All-Mountain More Forgiving; 76 Women's Powder; 86 Frontside; 104 All-Mountain More Stable; 126 All-Mountain More Forgiving; 145 All-Mountain Chargers; 151 All-Mountain Freestyle; 165 Park; 173 Powder More Directional; 184 Powder More Playful; 202 Ski Boots; 224 Touring Bindings & Skins; 236 Snowboards & Splitboards; 259 Apparel; 265 Helmets & Goggles.
- Letter from the Editor, "Pushing Hard, Pulling Back" (Ellsworth): 10th annual guide, biggest yet with more products and more on-snow hours. Memorial to Blister reviewer Eric Freson, killed in a backcountry skiing accident in spring 2024; Freson loved pushing hard in many sports and pushing friends. Ellsworth argues for continuing to challenge ourselves physically/emotionally/spiritually/intellectually, but after a loss it is right to reassess risk tolerance. Proposal: at least once a year write down and discuss with friends safety commitments (retake Avy 1/2, WFR course, reread avalanche books) for accountability. Be smart, pick spots to push, be there for each other.
- Recommended Shops page: service-oriented shops Blister vouches for (boots, tuning, bikes); "We need great shops. Let's support them."

## 2. Product sections

### 50/50 Skis: Backcountry & Resort (p.14)
Class overview: for a single setup for lift skiing and touring. Dedicated setups still better (durability, stability, weight, safety), but advances in AT bindings/boots/skis make now the best time ever for one setup. Weight is the main tradeoff: lighter = harder/less fun in typical inbounds conditions but better on the uphill. These skis perform "fairly well" to "quite well" across resort conditions -- better than the lighter dedicated touring skis, not as heavy as All-Mountain/Powder skis (often less damp/stable). Decide what compromises you'll accept. Blister+ members can get personalized recs.

- **Volkl Blaze 94** (redesigned 24/25) -- 132.5-94-118 mm (179); radii 20/40/17/22 m; 1613 & 1631 g; 59/2.5/25; 178.7 cm; -13.3 / 76.1 from tail; 151-186 cm; poplar/paulownia/beech + recycled PET stringer + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Lightweight all-mountain ski now more resort-biased; more engaging and precise on piste, tweaked multi-radius sidecut for quicker initiation, impressive edge hold, respectable suspension for weight. Harder to release/pivot in tight, grabby off-piste than others. For more on/off balance see Blaze 104 or Bent 100.
- **Ferreol Explo 96** -- 128.7-96-118.5 (172); 15 m; 1533 & 1557 g; 75/9/34; 170 cm; -7.5 / 77.5; 156,162,172,182; poplar + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass & carbon. "3P camber" (extra camber zones fore/aft of binding) gives strong firm-snow grip but takes getting used to; tip/tail rocker, not-very-rearward mount, lively flex make it fairly playful off piste. Pionnier 104 / Norse Enduro looser and surfier; Explo 86 more intuitive on very firm snow. For quickness and energy over planted stability.
- **Norse Enduro** -- 125.1-99.4-113 (188); 25 m; 1933 & 1943 g; 65/4.5/24; 187.1; -8.1 / 85.5; 164-188; ash/poplar/balsa + 2 layers aluminum + fiberglass. Standout for slashing/hopping off piste; downhill-focused but tourable. Shines in moguls and tight trees (deep rocker, long radius, low swing weight); predictable in weird BC snow. Not forgiving when backseat; needs speed/effort to lay over on groomers; impressive edge hold. Less experienced -> Bent 100; tight carving -> Blaze 104. Great for steep technical terrain.
- **Atomic Bent 100** (first update since introduction) -- 129-99.5-119.5 (186); 19.5 m; 1886 & 1925 g; 56.5/11.5/33.5; 182.9; -7.5 / 84; 158-186; poplar + fiberglass. Keeps traits: drive shovels or ski upright (esp. mounted closer to center), surprisingly stable on edge for low weight. New: tweaked lengths, slightly softer more rockered tail (closer to wider Bents), but still by far the most precise/directional Bent. Playful, light, but can be pushed pretty hard.
- **Volkl Blaze 104** (new, replaces Blaze 106) -- 142-103.5-127.5 (186); 21/45/19/24 m; 1806 & 1864 g; 57.5/4/26.5; 186.2; -12.1 / 81; 158-186; poplar/paulownia/beech + recycled PET + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Snappier and more precise than predecessor; rewards aggressive edge angles on consistent snow, knocked around on variable surfaces; easy to flick (low swing weight) but needs directional stance and shovel pressure to release tails. Floats very well. Few as exciting for corduroy or spring corn.
- **Peak 104 by Dav** -- 134.3-103.7-122.6 (184); 25 m; 1807 & 1849 g; 63/7/22; 183.3; -9 / 82.7; 160,168,178,184; poplar/paulownia + PU stringers + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Lighter-material version of Peak 104 by Bode (Chris Davenport's version). Directional, rewards forward stance with strong edge hold but easy to skid/slide off piste. Gives up suspension/stability vs. Bode version (in All-Mountain More Stable) but respectable; good alternative to Blaze 104 for bigger, faster turns. If mostly lift-served, Bode's 104 (not that heavy) may be better.
- **Head Kore 105** -- 134.8-104.8-124.7 (184); 17.8 m; 1846 & 1873 g; 59/3/26; 183; -10.1 / 81.4; 163-191; karuba/poplar + graphene + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Rare combo of stiffness and low weight. For experienced directional skiers wanting light but aggressive-capable in soft/consistent snow; demanding for beginners/intermediates. Impressive stability-to-weight in soft snow; quickness/precision over damp ride. Kore 111 for deeper snow.
- **Ferreol Pionnier 104** -- 136.2-104.8-126.7 (188); 17 m; 1900 & 1917 g; 57/9.5/34; 188.1; -9.8 / 84.3; 164,172,180,188; poplar + cherry underfoot + fiberglass & carbon. Ferreol's original ski. Short radius + tapered shovels = easy to carve and slash in tight terrain; stiff midsection and less tapered tail = secure on edge even in bigger turns than radius suggests. Nice suspension for weight. Touring a lot and between sizes -> go shorter. More forgiving/fun on piste than Kore 105/Voss Z'N; Surfeur 112 for soft-snow maneuverability.
- **Wagner Summit 106** (semi-custom "Factory" series) -- 140.9-105.1-126.9 (186); 19.9 m; 2008 & 2065 g; 72/1.5/31.5; 184.4; -10.6 / 81.6; 165-186 (custom available); aspen/maple + "carbon fiber torsion box" + fiberglass. Exceptionally versatile, intuitive for all reviewers; deep low rocker lines with minimal taper -> loose in tight spaces but great carver for width. Vs. Kore 105: more damp, forgiving, better carver, a touch less stable in soft snow. Hard to unsettle. Centered/freestyle -> J Skis Slacker; Wagner can build bespoke.
- **Asnes Voss Z'N** -- 132.7-105.7-119.2 (187); 22 m; 1897 & 1917 g; 65/4/12; 185.7; mount points "Boot Center" -9.6 / 83.3, "All-Mountain" -12 / 80.9; 167,176,187; poplar + carbon. Looks like playful pow ski but is a powerful all-mountain ski; easy to pivot but stiff flat tail gives edge hold/support at speed and security on steep firm slopes; not engaging initiating carves at moderate speed. Not forgiving (see Goat, Citadel 107). For technically skilled skiers wanting low swing weight; floats better than Kore 105.
- **Meier Crosscut BC** -- 141.9-105.8-125.5 (185); 19 m; 1919 & 1928 g; 53/8/9; 184.8; -9.5 / 82.5; 178, 185; aspen/beetle-kill pine + fiberglass & innegra. Do-it-all touring ski, also resort. Tapered deeply rockered tips plane easily and aren't hooky; less rockered tail + cambered middle = directional feel like Voss Z'N but less punishing backseat. Tighter radius = more engaging on piste, though Summit 106 and Blaze 104 carve better. Excels in soft snow and tight terrain; ~1900 g.
- **Cardiff Goat 108 Enduro** -- 128.5-107.5-121 (186); 24 m; 1852 & 1883 g; 76.5/5.5/52; 183.9; -4.6 / 87.4; 174,180,186; poplar/paulownia + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Ski version of Cardiff's Goat snowboard: surfy, playful, freeride flavor; deep rocker, easy slashing from forward or centered stance; surprisingly strong grip and turn-shape versatility (blended multi-radius sidecut). Similar to WNDR Intention 108 -- Goat looser and lighter; Intention more composed at speed in rough snow.
- **Renoun Citadel 107** (new, replaces Citadel 106) -- 137-107.5-124.5 (184); 19 m; 1672 & 1740 g; 69.5/5/27.5; 182.2; -10.3 / 80.8; 163-191; aspen + titanal binding reinforcement + VibeStop + carbon. Light, powder-oriented; non-Newtonian VibeStop feels softer at low speed, stiffer/damper at high forces. Much more rearward mount and more tip rocker vs 106 -> floats better, more directional, responds best to forward stance. Not for on-piste precision or crud stability, but one of the lightest skis Blister still recommends for lift use.
- **WNDR Alpine Intention 108** (cambered version tested; reverse-camber also offered) -- 137.4-108.4-128.2 (188); 21.5 m; 1997 & 2003 g; 65/1/34; 186.8; -7.7 / 85.7; 164-194; aspen + algal-PU stringers + algal-PU sidewalls + "Spiral Plate" binding reinforcement + fiberglass. True do-it-all: carves well for size, easy to release/pivot/slash; accepts many stances (both directional and playful reviewers liked it). Excels in variable BC conditions that unsettle lighter skis; impressive suspension inbounds for weight. Vital 98 lighter/more precise; Reason 120 surfier.
- **J Skis Slacker** -- 135-109.2-127.1 (188); 20 m; 1905 & 1919 g; 80/2/39; 185.4; -7.7 / 85; 169,176,182,188; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. J Skis' easygoing, predictable, playful personality on a light ~110 platform; forgiving flex, deep rocker, progressive mount, instantly intuitive. Not as damp as J's heavier skis. Floats very well, not punishing backseat, easy on edge on firm. Next two skis better for high speed/big turns; Bent 110 for freestyle.
- **Majesty Havoc 110 Carbon** -- 142.8-109.3-126.5 (186); 23 m; 1820 & 1821 g; 70/5/18; 184.9; -9.9 / 82.6; 166-191; paulownia/poplar + carbon/aramid stringers + carbon & fiberglass. For aggressive directional skiers in a class of softer skis; light for long tours but surprisingly composed at moderate speed in crud / high speed in consistent snow. Not very forgiving; impressive flotation; releases easily when driving shovels; powerful high-angle carves on corn/soft groomers. Havoc Ti series (All-Mountain More Stable) for high-speed composure.
- **Norse Freeride** -- 132-109.4-122.1 (192); 26 m; 1973 & 2047 g; 67/3/33; 191; -7.2 / 88.3; 176,184,192; ash/poplar/balsa + 2 layers aluminum + fiberglass. Purpose-built for aggressive skiing on steep technical slopes, light enough to tour. Planes in low-density pow but narrow enough for firm snow; reasonably damp in chop with light swing weight. Looser (but strong) alternative to Kore 111. Not for carving priority; 4FRNT Hoji looser/more playful.
- **Atomic Bent 110** -- 133.2-109.5-124.6 (188); 19 m; 1863 & 1872 g; 68/5/64; 186.3; -3 / 90.1; 172,180,188; poplar + fiberglass. More a narrower Bent Chetler 120 than wider Bent 100: very surfy, nimble, slightly better on firm than the 120. Near-centered mount not ideal for directional skiers; great for tricks/jibbing resort and BC. Good edge hold for class but knocked around in crud; could be widest ski in a quiver. Too freestyle? -> Line Vision 114.
- **Folsom Giver** (built as a downhill-oriented touring ski for editor-in-chief) -- 137.5-109.5-128.5 (186); 25 m; 1947 & 1960 g; 69.5/9/25.5; 184.4; -9.9 / 82.3; 184,186,188,192,195 (custom); poplar/bamboo + 70/30 fiberglass/carbon. Giver shape minimizes deflection in variable snow; rocker/camber/rocker version still easy to feather/release. Reverse-camber Givers better in grabby/punchy snow, but camber adds bite/pop on firm. Good if Katana/Hoji appeal but you don't love fully rockered looseness. Folsom can make lighter or ultra-planted ("The Hammer", Chargers section) versions.
- **Head Kore 111** -- 139.7-110.8-126.5 (184); 21.2 m; 1833 & 1894 g; 66/4/27; 183.1; -8.1 / 83.5; 177,184,191; karuba/poplar + graphene + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. For dynamic skiers pushing limits in soft snow without heavy setups; very low swing weight and rare stability in pow/soft chop for weight. Light+stiff can feel harsh, needs attentive pilot in poor conditions. Performs well mounted a couple cm forward (more balanced). For advanced/expert directional skiers. Looser: next ski or Head Oblivion 116.
- **Ferreol Surfeur 112** -- 140-111.5-129.5 (184); 17 m; 1859 & 1864 g; 64/4/37; 183.4; -7.7 / 84; 176, 184; poplar + titanal binding plate + flax & carbon laminate (no fiberglass; see GEAR:30 Ep.248). Ferreol's widest/most playful; flax gives impressively damp ride for weight; tapered, deep rocker = maneuverable/playful; still predictable and stable in soft-ish conditions. Next two skis better for big fast open turns; Surfeur more engaging at moderate speeds; touring-friendly, easy to pilot, still fun skied dynamically.
- **4FRNT Hoji** (Eric Hjorleifson signature, middle of series) -- 128.2-111.6-119.3 (184); 30 m (all lengths); 2060 & 2075 g; 73/0/26; 182.5; -6.6 / 84.7; 170,177,184,191; aspen/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Reverse camber, long radius, fairly forward mount: agile and composed at speed, pivots/slashes, also big turns, centered or forward. Flatter middle = reliable edge hold on firm; not exciting carver but predictable. Inspires Hoji-style slashes, airs, big fast turns. 4FRNT Raven lighter/narrower; Volkl V-Werks Katana more directional/rearward.
- **Volkl V-Werks Katana** (constructionally unchanged longer than almost any ski in guide) -- 142.5-112-132 (184); 23.5 m; 1959 & 1975 g; 68/0/18; 182.2; -13.9 / 77.2; 177,184,191; poplar + ash underfoot + carbon laminate. Go-to for lightweight but strong, maneuverable ski; surprisingly damp for carbon; long-ish radius, traditional mount, no taper -> minimal deflection in chop. Great resort pow ski and powerful carver (not quickest edge to edge); with tech binding a very capable BC ski. Not forgiving, needs forward stance, don't bash into rocks. Excellent stability-to-weight. Havoc 110 Carbon if lower weight matters more.
- **Line Vision 114** (new, replaces Vision 118) -- 138.5-113-131 (183); 23 m; 1802 & 1819 g; 63/2.5/55.5; 181.9; -5.2 / 85.8; 159-189; aspen/paulownia + flax, carbon, fiberglass. Light playful pow ski blurring freeride/freestyle; shape/rocker/mount similar to Optic 114 but far more agile/lively and human-powered-friendly. Floats well, easy to slash/slarve, forward or centered stance. Much less composed in resort chop but impressive soft-snow stability for weight. For those between very directional and very freestyle.
- **Dynafit Tigard 114** -- 140.5-113.5-129.5 (188); 23.5 m; 2011 & 2023 g; 65.5/4.5/31.5; 186.7; -7.8 / 85.6; 180, 188; poplar + carbon stringers. Dynafit's widest/heaviest; floats well, easy to drift; surprisingly stiff and precise on firm base -> good driving through dense layers/crusts, but unforgiving backseat and harsh when very firm. Stable for weight centered or driving; comfortable in air. Easier-going: Surfeur 112 or Blaze 114.
- **Volkl Blaze 114** -- 153.4-114-136 (184); radii 39/18/29 m; 1938 & 2008 g; 68/3/22; 183.5; -11.8 / 80; 176,184,192; poplar/beech + recycled PET stringers + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Standout: easy and intuitive in pow (wide soft rockered tips, very rearward mount) yet versatile; doesn't need specific stance; carves clean on piste when over shovels. No charger but respectable suspension for weight. Better options for airs/balanced skiers; more intuitive for directional skiers; accessible alternative to Havoc 110 Carbon / Kore 111.
- **Salomon QST X** (new 24/25) -- 140-116-127 (184); 25 m; 1973 & 1997 g; 66.5/6/26; 183; -5.3 / 86.2; 178,184,192; karuba/poplar + cork tips/tails + titanal binding reinforcement + flax/carbon + fiberglass. Blend of old QST 118 and QST Echo 106: surfy and balanced, lighter. Extremely playful in soft snow; long carves from centered stance in pow; dynamic approach in chop. Less in element on firm (stiff midsection, short effective edge, long radius -> skid). QST Blank better for resort chop.
- **Atomic Bent Chetler 120** (slight 24/25 update to reduce environmental footprint) -- 143.5-119.5-134.5 (192); 20 m; 1941 & 1989 g; 62.5/8.5/57.5; 190.2; -2.6 / 92.5; 176,184,192; poplar + ash binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Feels largely same; reference model for playful pow; very surfy, maybe more so (tweaked "HRZN Tech" inserts); touch stiffer. Not for forward stance / big fast turns in variable. A first recommendation for centered, playful pow skiers.
- **WNDR Alpine Reason 120** (reverse-camber version measured; cambered also offered) -- 147.2-120.5-138.2 (191); 22 m; 2123 & 2140 g; 65/0/29; 189.4; -7 / 87.7; 170,177,184,191; aspen + algal-PU stringers/sidewalls + "Spiral Plate" + fiberglass. Adaptable pow ski: directional skiers can drive shovels; much more composed in chop than previous few skis; surfs from centered stance. Not lightest but better suspension than many touring/50/50 skis. Reverse camber = maneuverability; camber = firm-snow versatility. Intention 108 for wider range of conditions.
- **Armada ARG II UL** -- 134.5-132.3-133 (187); 50 m; 2277 & 2295 g; 61/0/52; 186.2; -5.5 / 88; 187 only; caruba + fiberglass. Does one thing exceptionally: reverse camber, near-reverse sidecut, 133 waist = absurdly surfy in soft snow, effortless to throw sideways, manageable in crust/sun-warmed snow. Not an all-rounder; learning curve and caution in variable/firm; light enough for BC where its conditions are more common. A special quiver ski for the best days.

**Blister Spectrums -- 50/50** (top -> bottom):
- Best in Variable -> Worst: Katana, Folsom Giver, Wagner Summit 106, WNDR Intention 108, WNDR Reason 120, Havoc 110 Carbon, Blaze 114, Norse Freeride, Hoji, Bent 100, Pionnier 104, QST X, J Slacker, Voss Z'N, Norse Enduro, Crosscut BC, Citadel 107, Tigard 114, Goat 108, Vision 114, Surfeur 112, Peak 104 Dav, Explo 96, Kore 111, Bent Chetler 120, Kore 105, Blaze 104, Bent 110, Blaze 94, ARG II UL.
- Most Inbounds-Oriented -> Most Touring-Oriented: Katana, Summit 106, Explo 96, Bent 100, Blaze 104, Blaze 94, Enduro, Reason 120, Blaze 114, Giver, Norse Freeride, Crosscut BC, Pionnier 104, Bent Chetler 120, Surfeur 112, QST X, Bent 110, Slacker, Intention 108, Citadel 107, Kore 111, Hoji, Goat 108, Tigard 114, Havoc 110 Carbon, Vision 114, Peak 104 Dav, Kore 105, Voss Z'N, ARG II UL.
- Most Playful -> Least Playful: Bent Chetler 120, Bent 110, ARG II UL, Surfeur 112, Vision 114, QST X, Slacker, Reason 120, Goat 108, Bent 100, Intention 108, Hoji, Citadel 107, Norse Freeride, Enduro, Pionnier 104, Tigard 114, Summit 106, Blaze 114, Crosscut BC, Explo 96, Giver, Kore 111, Blaze 104, Kore 105, Peak 104 Dav, Blaze 94, Voss Z'N, Havoc 110 Carbon, Katana.
(Interesting: Katana is simultaneously Best in Variable, Most Inbounds-Oriented and Least Playful; ARG II UL Worst in Variable and most touring-oriented.)

### Backcountry Touring Skis (p.29)
Class overview: choose the combination of uphill vs. downhill performance for your objectives; it's personal preference. Lots of good touring skis now (long big-vert days, quick laps, tricks). Don't fixate on ski weight; think about total weight on your feet (skis + bindings + boots + skins). If not doing long tours or racing uphill, heavier skis/boots/bindings give better stability, suspension/damping, and safety features on the descent. Blister+ personalized recs.
(Ad: a Crested Butte-area shop -- used + new gear, trade-in, bootfitting; garbled graphic.)

- **WNDR Alpine Nocturne 88** -- 117-88.5-108 (184); 20.5 m; 1667 & 1683 g; 48.5/6.5/16.5; 183.5; -7.8 / 83.9; 166-184; aspen + algal-PU stringers/sidewalls + Spiral Plate + fiberglass (reclaimed-waste inserts). WNDR's most "traditional" model but unusual for ~90 touring ski; not lightest, but notably more planted and predictable in rough snow than ultralights; slightly freeride shape/rocker/mount -> maneuverable, stance-adaptable. Stands out in inconsistent snow and higher speeds. Superwolf lighter and more directional.
- **Majesty Superwolf** -- 129.9-90.6-110.1 (178); 18 m; 1477 & 1481 g; 62/4/14; 176.3; -9.9 / 78.3; 148-184; paulownia + fiberglass. Reviewer favorite; much less harsh on firm and more stable at speed than its weight suggests; tapered, subtly rockered tip = predictable in soft/variable; handled steep icy lines with little drama. One of most stable/versatile touring skis at this width/weight -- fast fluid skiing and "survival skiing." For those who find skis in class twitchy/hooky/harsh.
- **La Sportiva Salto** (updated) -- 120-91.5-107 (178); 18 m; 1384 & 1434 g; 63.5/7/15.5; 177.2; -7.9 / 80.7; 164,178,188; poplar/paulownia + aluminum stringers + carbon. More downhill-oriented than expected; tapered tips/tails, lots of tip rocker, low tail -> maneuverable but solid edge hold. ~1400 g a sweet spot for bigger objectives without gram-counting. Tips can get twitchy in big turns in inconsistent snow (vs. Nocturne/Superwolf), but more maneuverable in crust/sastrugi than Superguide 95 / Crux 93. Preliminary -- more testing needed.
- **Head Crux 93 Pro** (new Head touring collection) -- 130-92.5-112.5 (177); 18.6 m; 1124 & 1260 g; 62.5/6.5/17; 175.5; -10.4 / 77.3; 163-184; karuba + graphene, carbon, fiberglass. Lots of precision, very light; strong flex, traditional directional design like Kore; good stability-to-weight in consistent snow (groomers, windbuff, corn), rewards driving. Low weight shows in rough snow -- needs attentive, conservative technique. Lighter on skin track than many. Kore 93 for more downhill orientation.
- **Majesty Superpatrol** -- 129-94.1-111 (177); 19 m; 1630 & 1635 g; 61/8/9; 175.8; -14.5 / 73.4; 161-185; paulownia + carbon/kevlar weave + fiberglass. "Cut-off tail technology": long effective edge, very set-back mount, lets you size down. Impressive stability and flotation for size when driving shovels; short tails snap around easily. Less intuitive centered. Stiffer/more stable than Superwolf, needs more speed. Mount divisive, but strong one-ski-quiver contender for directional skiers.
- **Scott Superguide 95** -- 129-95-115 (178); 21 m; 1362 & 1376 g; 65/1/23; 176.5; -12 / 76.3; 170,178,184; paulownia/beech + carbon, aramid, fiberglass. Top option for precision when edge hold is hard to find; fairly good suspension for weight; traditional shape/rocker, stout flex for grip and power. Superwolf easier to release, more forgiving, smoother. Composed for weight; few as confidence-inspiring for precise hop turns on steep technical lines; others more maneuverable in grabby snow.
- **Salomon MTN 96 Carbon** -- 130-95-116 (182); 19 m; 1451 & 1502 g; 68/6/13.5; 180.8; -11 / 79.3; 166,174,182,188; karuba + titanal binding reinforcement + cork tip inserts + carbon & basalt. Fills gap between QST and narrower MTN; preliminary take: similar to all-time favorite MTN Explore 95, but a bit lighter and more maneuverable (deeper tail rocker, softer flex); impressive suspension-to-weight, secure carving. Superpatrol more composed in variable at speed; Superguide/Crux lighter. Shaping up as very reliable all-rounder.
- **Line Vision 96** (new, replaces Vision 98) -- 131.5-95-120 (184); 17.0 m; 1634 & 1658 g; 64.5/6/31.5; 182.6; -6.3 / 85; 154-184; aspen/paulownia + flax, carbon, fiberglass. Playful and maneuverable light touring ski; design closer to Line's heavier Optic 96. Much easier to pivot/slash than most light touring skis, esp. soft/grabby snow; one of best for centered stance; smooths harsh snow better than lighter skis. More composed at speed than Vision 98, slightly less soft/engaging at very low speeds. Not for max edge hold.
- **Peak 98 by Dav** -- 128.5-97.7-116.2 (184); 25 m; 1805 & 1808 g; 60/6/21; 183.5; -10.3 / 82; 160,168,178,184; poplar/paulownia + PU stringers + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Lighter touring version of Peak 98 by Bode; brings resort qualities to BC: intuitive with forward driving stance, carves very well yet easier to skid than most precise options. Better suspension/stability than most dedicated touring skis (most lighter). Safe bet for directional skiers wanting versatile ~100 ski, fun to carve but not a firm-snow specialist.
- **WNDR Alpine Vital 98** (cambered version measured) -- 126-98-117 (183); 19.5 m; 1780 & 1782 g; 52.5/8.5/31.5; 182; -6.8 / 84.1; 159-189; aspen + algal-PU stringers/sidewalls + Spiral Plate + fiberglass. Excellent suspension and predictability for those willing to haul a bit more weight (<1800 g); not easily unsettled by unpredictable snow or aggressive skiing; adaptable to stances. Not best for hop-turning icy couloirs nor most engaging at slow speed.
- **Head Crux 99 Pro** (new) -- 132-98.5-116.5 (184); 22.7 m; 1254 & 1327 g; 60.5/7.5/17; 182.6; -11.5 / 79.8; 163-184; karuba + graphene, carbon, fiberglass. Kore lessons applied to a touring package; one of the lighter in class, nimble uphill, enough surface area. Shallow rocker + strong flex -> precision over playfulness; directional skiers with good technique can push it in consistent/soft snow. Shares shape with Kore 99 but much lighter, less planted; longer radius counters twitchiness. Rare precision and power for weight.
- **G3 SEEKr R3 100** -- 133-101-122 (178); 18.3 m; 1423 & 1437 g; 66/5/15; 176.5; -9.5 / 79.5; 170,178,186; poplar/paulownia + titanal mounting plate + carbon. "R3" skis reportedly 100% recyclable -- send back to G3 (GEAR:30 Ep.258). Light but not so light it needs extreme care; deep rocker and taper = maneuverable for a directional ski; stout flex, carves intuitively on firm. Well-rounded for weight-conscious directional skiers. G3 ROAMr (damper) and SLAYr (more playful) also use R3.
- **J Skis Escalator** -- 131.5-102-123 (182); 18.6 m; 1750 & 1761 g; 73/5/36; 180.2; -7.1 / 83; 162-188; aspen + carbon & fiberglass. Easygoing, predictable, fun; accessible flex, freestyle-friendly, moderately light; for park backgrounds or directional skiers wanting forgiving. Matches/exceeds stiffer skis in suspension; good carving + soft-snow maneuverability. Vs Vision 96: Vision looser/surfier, Escalator more stable/precise on firm. For those who dislike stiff touring skis but prioritize downhill.
- **ZAG Slap 104 Lite** (new) -- 136-103-125 (182); 19 m; 1741 & 1753 g; 70.5/7/47; 180.2; mounts Centered -3.7 / 86.3, Progressive -5.5 / 84.6, Recommended -7.2 / 82.8; 170-188; poplar/paulownia + rubber inserts + flax, carbon, fiberglass. Lighter freestyle-friendly Slap 104; maneuverable end of touring spectrum but surprisingly fun carver; works driving shovels, neutral, or switch. Not exceptionally light but not a haul; nice suspension in crust/refreeze/tracks. Fun at low speeds, keeps composure at higher speeds -- middle-road playful touring setup.
- **4FRNT Raven** -- 120.5-103.1-111.7 (184); 29 m (all lengths); 1736 & 1767 g; 64/0/25.5; 182.2; -5.7 / 85.4; 170,177,184,190; aspen + maple underfoot + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Spec outlier but all-time favorite touring ski; can be pushed hard yet one of easiest to maneuver (beginners to experts). No camber = easy smearing; long radius prevents twitchy/hooky feel. 4-Lock skin attachment system. Not for tight carving; Slap 104 Lite better for camber bite. One of the best in variable BC snow. Hoji for more stability/flotation.
- **Fauna Pioneer** -- 123.4-103.2-118.3 (184); 22 m; 1787 & 1793 g; 63.5/10/51; 184; -7.1 / 84.9; 175, 184; paulownia/ash + carbon & fiberglass. For max maneuverability/playfulness: very tapered tips/tails, progressive mount, twin tail, but lots of camber and very stiff cambered section. Extremely easy to pivot in soft, slices soft chop; decent edge hold once used to centered stance. Good suspension for weight; less composed than Raven in firm crud. For center-mounted resort skiers bringing playful style to BC.
- **Elan Ripstick Tour 104** -- 129.4-103.4-121.9 (187); 25.4 m; 1576 & 1592 g; 52.5/5.5/20.5; 184.3; -6.3 / 85.9; 173,180,187; paulownia/poplar + 1 carbon rod + carbon & fiberglass. Built from scratch for BC: softer, longer radius, more centered than Ripsticks. Energetic, easy to maneuver; more stance-adaptable and easier to release in grabby snow, but slower to initiate carves and less snappy. For varied stances, pow-focused touring, blending directional and playful.
- **Armada Locator 104** -- 130-103.5-121.5 (178); 22 m; 1442 & 1474 g; 49/6/19; 177; -10 / 78.7; 162-186; caruba + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Second-widest of touring Locator line; light, needs attentive pilot in rough snow but deep rocker/tapered tips keep it easy at slow speed; comfortable with big fast turns in pow/corn; good edge hold for width; rearward mount + soft shovels = very good flotation. Not freestyle (unlike ARV/ARW). Directional but fairly surfy. Locator 112 for deeper.
- **Line Vision 104** (middle of overhauled touring line, from Optic 104) -- 131.5-103-123 (185); 19 m; 1703 & 1709 g; 61.5/3.5/41; 183.9; -5.2 / 86.6; 158-185; aspen/paulownia + flax, carbon, fiberglass. Like Vision 108 before: surfy, playful, no need for speed, but more capable at speed; big sweet spot. Less composed at high speed than Raven; not for stiff precise directional skiers. Middle ground leaning playful.
- **Scott Superguide Freetour** -- 135.5-104.2-123.3 (185); 24 m; 1577 & 1605 g; 65/4/21; 183.3; -11.4 / 80.3; 178, 185; paulownia/beech + carbon, aramid, fiberglass. For technically skilled skiers valuing precision and stability; little rocker/taper; ~1600 g, mid-market weight. Very impressive composure at speed and in variable for weight. Prefers forward driving stance; not playful or easy, punishes backseat. Excellent stability-to-weight; big faces + steep firm hop turns.
- **Wagner Summit 105** (semi-custom) -- 134.7-104.4-122.6 (185); 23.8 m; 1705 & 1708 g; 72/5/31; 183.2; -9.8 / 81.8; 171,178,185 (custom); paulownia + carbon & fiberglass. Combines traits of favorite touring skis; very easy to slide (deep rocker), not very tapered so fun to carve with speed; big sweet spot but this version prefers forward stance. For directional skiers liking Raven but wanting camber and tighter, more engaging radius. Summit 106 (50/50) for lift use/more stability.
(Blister+ member testimonial ad: evacuation benefits.)
- **Head Crux 105 Pro** (new) -- 132.5-104.5-121.5 (184); 25.6 m; 1357 & 1380 g; 57/10/24.5; 182.7; -11.5 / 79.8; 170,177,184; karuba + graphene, carbon, fiberglass. Extremely impressive surface area for weight; traditional directional design (no radical rocker/center mount); long radius and slight taper boost soft-snow maneuverability vs narrower Crux. Encourages forward stance and technique; knocked around in variable. Alp Tracks 106 more playful (less stable); Superguide Freetour for suspension. Efficient pow-lap setup.
- **Weston Summit Carbon** -- 133.8-105.4-123.8 (186); 16.5 m; 1671 & 1675 g; 71/2/48; 184.2; -7.8 / 84.3; 156,166,176,186; paulownia + aluminum stringers + carbon. Playful, accessible for mellow glades; soft rockered tapered tips/tails, easy tight turns, forgiving, slashy; nice suspension for weight. Not for huge fast turns in difficult snow. Excellent for beginner/intermediate BC skiers and mid-winter tree laps; less one-dimensional than ultralights.
- **Faction Agent 3** -- 133.2-105.5-123.5 (180); 21 m; 1725 & 1774 g; 69.5/5/24; 178.1; -11.3 / 77.8; 164-188; caruba + carbon weave + titanal binding plate + fiberglass. Same shape/rocker as metal Dancer 3, lighter and slightly more accessible flex; big sweet spot. For aggressive directional skiers with forward stance: maneuverable in tight spots, holds edge really well, damp for weight, lively. Less punishing than Superguide Freetour. La Machine 3 looser/surfier.
- **Movement Alp Tracks 106** -- 137.1-105.5-125.3 (185); 20 m; 1337 & 1347 g; 61/4.5/15.5; 184.2; -8.8 / 83.3; 177, 185; caruba + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon. Lots of ski for little weight; excels in soft consistent snow, very soft tips plane well, easy slarves. Low weight shows as snow gets firm/rough -- needs attentive, proficient skier. Fantastic for max fun in good snow with min uphill effort. Locator 104 slightly heavier for variable.
- **Salomon QST Echo 106** -- 138.8-105.7-125.9 (181); 19 m; 1820 & 1824 g; 54/4/26; 180; mounts "MTN Binding" -8 / 82, "Alpine + Shift" -9 / 81; 157-189; caruba/poplar + cork tips/tails + basalt & fiberglass. Lighter QST 106 preserving most qualities; impressively calm/composed in unpredictable snow given several hundred grams less. Maneuverable, stance-adaptable, fun at varied speeds, capable in rough snow. For descent-focused BC skiers who find other options too soft/center-mounted.
- **Dynafit Radical 107** -- 135.5-107.5-124.5 (188); 22.5 m; 1708 & 1793 g; 65/8/17; 187.4; -8.8 / 84.9; 173,181,188; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. One of Dynafit's most downhill-oriented but competitively light "freeride touring" ski; excels in soft snow, easy drifts, composed in bigger arcs; holds edge well on firm; suspension less smooth than similar weights. In-between option for those drawn to Vision 104/Wildcat Tour 108 but worried they're too loose/centered. Tigard 114 more float/stability.
- **Moment Wildcat Tour 108** -- 134.5-107.7-127.1 (184); 22 m; 1692 & 1715 g; 72/5.5/65; 180.5; -7.1 / 83.2; 164-190; paulownia/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Retains regular Wildcat 108's "playful charger" personality, lighter. Directional-capable, stiff middle = stable for weight; also centered and balanced in air. More composed at speed than Weston Summit Carbon; slightly punishing for beginners/intermediates. Looser/surfier than most ~108s but reliable edge on smooth firm. Not for very aggressive forward stance or tight carving.
- **Ronin 108** (Chamonix brand; "LTE" version tested) -- 140.8-108.1-124 (185); 20.5 m (all lengths); 1637 & 1647 g; 59/6/22; 185.4; -11.3 / 81.4; 160-192; balsa/bamboo + carbon & flax. For directional skiers valuing versatility/reliability; impressive flotation (big shovels), more precise than similar widths; low weight, good suspension, lots of pop; moderately tapered/rockered, strong-not-excessive flex = predictable with forward stance. Up there with Agent 3 for support/stability/carving but more forgiving. Heavier "MFD" version for damper ride.
- **Atomic Backland 108 W** (overhauled 24/25; women's) -- 133-108.5-122 (176); 17.5 m; 1431 & 1503 g; 71/6.5/28; 174.5; -6.5 / 80.8; 158,167,176; poplar/karuba + ash binding reinforcement + fiberglass. More freeride focus, very light; impressive flotation and maneuverability in soft snow, tight terrain; carves fairly well on smooth firm but urges dialing back in refreeze/crust/sastrugi (not as unstable as Alp Tracks 106). Supportive flex lets you ski hard in soft snow. Less forward-stance-demanding than Backland 107 -> more forgiving.
- **Faction La Machine 3** -- 131.7-108.7-123.8 (184); 21 m; 1560 & 1594 g; 68/0/40; 181.9; mounts Newschool -8 / 83, Progressive -9.5 / 81.5, Classic -11 / 80; 165-190; paulownia/poplar + carbon & fiberglass. Derived from Faction's 126mm pow ski; surfy, maneuverable, impressively light, surprisingly predictable in BC conditions for weight; excellent pow ski for width. QST Echo 106 better at high speed in rough snow. Alternative to Raven (reverse camber) if worried about Raven's weight and refusal to carve tight turns.
- **Atomic Backland 109** (new) -- 134.5-109-123.5 (184); 19 m; 1526 & 1569 g; 56.5/10.5/24; 182.8; -6.6 / 84.8; 176,184,189; poplar/karuba + ash binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Like Backland 107: low weight, very surfy in soft snow; but less rearward mount, more tail rocker, softer flex -> more accessible, stance-versatile; less powerful/precise than 107. Just heavy enough to avoid getting bucked when skiing chop conservatively. Sweet spot of weight/composure/deep-snow maneuverability.
- **Moment Deathwish Tour** -- 136-111-128 (184); 25 m; 1660 & 1680 g; 66/3/64; 183.4; -5 / 86.7; 174-190; paulownia/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. "Triple Camber" = uncommon combo of maneuverability and edge hold; playful freestyle design + strong flex = intuitive but unconventional. Knocked around more than regular Deathwish (itself a great 50/50 option) but damp/stable for weight. Dual personality: edge hold when driving tips, easy slash/spin from neutral. Not for directional rearward-mount lovers.
- **Armada Locator 112** -- 135.7-111.6-127.8 (187); 26 m; 1608 & 1661 g; 58/6/23; 186; -10.4 / 83; 166-187; caruba + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Exceptionally versatile human-powered pow ski; does remarkably well even for bigger skiers in deep snow; balanced in drifted turns despite directional mount; very good stability-to-weight. Feels big on firm; long radius favors big turns. For most BC pow scenarios "impressively reliable and very, very fun"; regular deep blower -> wider options.
- **Black Diamond Helio Carbon 115** -- 143.5-114.5-125.5 (185); 25 m; 1650 & 1686 g; 65.5/8/19; 185.3; -9.3 / 83.4; 177, 185; paulownia + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon. Ultralight pow tool; specs nearly identical to all-time favorite old Helio 116; <1700 g, one of lightest pow skis tested; preliminary -- more predictable in crust/crud than Crux 105 Pro and Alp Tracks 106. Fairly niche.
- **Armada ARV 116 JJ UL** -- 139-116-134 (185); 18 m; 1820 & 1835 g; 71/6/64; 183.5; -5 / 87; 165-192; caruba + fiberglass. For playful/freestyle BC skiers; very rockered, tapered, soft tips/tails -> forgiving, easy butters/slashes, good flotation; great for BC booter sessions. Not good in firm/variable (prone to deflection). Moment Wildcat Tour much stiffer if you want rocker/center mount without slowing down.
- **Moment Wildcat Tour** -- 142.9-117.8-134.4 (190); 27 m; 1885 & 1914 g; 73/6.5/65; 186.5; -6.8 / 86.4; 174-190; paulownia/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Lighter version of beloved resort pow ski Wildcat: fun, surfy, adequate float, excellent stability for weight in chop. Balanced; great for playful BC approach (trees, big turns, jumps). Wildcat Tour 108 / Deathwish Tour more versatile; ARV 116 JJ UL softer. Very directional skiers needing deep-snow float -> next two skis.
- **Whitedot Ragnarok Carbonlite (118)** -- 143.1-118.1-130.1 (190); 30 m; 1854 & 1903 g; 81/2/42; 187.7; -8 / 85.9; 190 only; poplar/ash + carbon/aramid stringers + fiberglass. From Whitedot's big-mountain comp ski; powerful, stable in long sweeping turns, easy to slash in trees with forward stance. Heavier side for touring but composure in variable and landing support unmatched by most here; strong on edge but long radius needs speed. Centered/playful -> previous two or Ragnarok ASYM (Playful Pow). For those let down by high-speed composure of touring pow skis.
- **Moonlight Cruiser Carbon All-Terrain** -- 145-121-135 (186); 26 m; 1631 & 1662 g; 184.5 cm; -11.2 / 81; 177, 186; poplar/paulownia + carbon stringers + fiberglass (profile not given). Two versions share shape: "Carbon Superlight" (~1180 g @186; probably lightest ski relative to size ever reviewed, but twitchy and vibration-transmitting outside untouched pow) and "Carbon All-Terrain" (still extremely light for 120 mm, very good suspension in almost all conditions, remarkably composed, forgiving enough for <1200 g 2-buckle boots). Lightweight 120+ pow skis increasingly rare; one of best pow-touring options ever reviewed.

**Blister Spectrums -- Backcountry Touring** (best -> worst):
- Best for Steep, Firm Terrain: Superguide 95, Superwolf, Nocturne 88, Superguide Freetour, MTN 96 Carbon, Superpatrol, Peak 98 Dav, Agent 3, Crux 93, Crux 99, Salto, Vital 98, Crux 105, Ronin 108, Locator 104, Escalator, QST Echo 106, Summit 105, SEEKr R3 100, Ripstick Tour 104, La Machine 3, Slap 104 Lite, Vision 96, Raven, Deathwish Tour, Wildcat Tour 108, Radical 107, Backland 109, Backland 108 W, Vision 104, Weston Summit, Alp Tracks 106, Wildcat Tour, Pioneer, Ragnarok CL, Cruiser AT, Helio 115, Locator 112, ARV 116 JJ UL (worst).
- Best in Crusty / Punchy Snow: Wildcat Tour, Ragnarok CL, Raven, Helio 115, Cruiser AT, Deathwish Tour, ARV 116 JJ UL, Wildcat Tour 108, La Machine 3, Locator 112, QST Echo 106, Pioneer, Summit 105, Radical 107, Vision 104, Slap 104 Lite, Weston Summit, Locator 104, Ronin 108, Backland 109, Backland 108 W, Escalator, Vision 96, Ripstick Tour 104, Agent 3, Vital 98, SEEKr, Peak 98 Dav, Alp Tracks 106, MTN 96 Carbon, Superguide Freetour, Superpatrol, Crux 105, Superwolf, Salto, Nocturne 88, Crux 99, Superguide 95, Crux 93 (worst).
- Best in Heavy, Wet Snow: Wildcat Tour, Ragnarok CL, ARV 116 JJ UL, Cruiser AT, Locator 112, Helio 115, Raven, La Machine 3, Deathwish Tour, Wildcat Tour 108, QST Echo 106, Radical 107, Vision 104, Slap 104 Lite, Vital 98, Escalator, Backland 109, Locator 104, Backland 108 W, Weston Summit, Summit 105, Pioneer, Ronin 108, Agent 3, Vision 96, Ripstick Tour 104, SEEKr, Alp Tracks 106, Peak 98 Dav, Crux 105, MTN 96 Carbon, Superguide Freetour, Superpatrol, Superwolf, Salto, Nocturne 88, Crux 99, Superguide 95, Crux 93 (worst).
(Essentially narrow/stiff = steep firm; wide/rockered = crust and wet snow.)

### Women's All-Mountain Skis -- More Stable (p.47)
Class overview: brands vary in how they differentiate women's/men's/unisex; some skis here are women-specific, some not, but all tested by female reviewers in wide conditions. Women's all-mountain split into More Forgiving vs More Stable, plus Women's Powder. More Stable = generally stiffer, less tolerant of mistakes (esp. backseat), but damper and more stable at speed (easier fast through tracked snow, variable, roughed-up groomers); often heavier. Good edge hold, powerful carve finishes; good mogul skis for stronger skiers but punishing for less experienced; less appealing to playful/loose or very easy slow-speed initiation seekers. Read caveats -- some are fairly easy.

- **Blizzard Black Pearl 84** (Black Pearl series updated) -- 124-83.5-107 (170); 14 m; 1690 & 1700 g; 70/5/18.5; 168.4; -10.9 / 73.3; 146-176; poplar/beech + partial titanal layers + carbon & fiberglass. Well-rounded narrow all-mountain; excels on piste with easy, intuitive initiation without perfect technique; off piste needs shovel pressure to release tails, but not piste-only. Aggressive skiers may want more composure on firm/rough. Good for progressing skiers or advanced on moderate groomers. Explo 86 quicker in trees/moguls.
- **Head Kore 85 W** -- 126.7-84.7-106.9 (170); 14.8 m; 1496 & 1509 g; 57/4/17; 169.1; -9.4 / 75.6; 149-170; karuba/poplar + graphene + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Strong and light: push hard in smooth snow, low swing weight; less damp in chop. Needs forward pressure to bend; better than many 85s in bumps and slarves (not locked in). More consistent the snow, more impressive.
- **Ferreol Explo 86** (designed/made in Quebec) -- 123.5-86.5-111.5 (174); 15 m; 1393 & 1419 g; 54/10.5/18; 173.9; -8.4 / 78.5; 156-174; poplar + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. One of last season's surprises: light, design like wider all-mountain skis, uncommonly damp and stable for weight, very secure on edge. Initiates carves less readily than Black Pearl 84 but some found it more confidence-inspiring on firm/cut-up snow. Low swing weight, easy release off piste; appealing for uphill resort skinning. Intermediates to experts.
- **Blizzard Black Pearl 88** (24/25 new) -- 127-87.5-109 (170); 15 m; 1773 & 1785 g; 73.5/4.5/20.5; 168.3; -10.4 / 73.8; 146-176; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. One of world's best-selling skis; still versatile and very intuitive but a bit more easygoing than previous; aggressive skiers may want more rough-snow stability. Easy to get on edge for racers to beginners. Off piste can feel hooky, stay over shovels (less demanding than Mindbender 89Ti W). Santa Ana 92 / Black Pearl 94 for max stability. Safe bet for groomers + bumps.
- **K2 Mindbender 89Ti W** -- 129.5-88.5-113.5 (170); 14.2 m; 1762 & 1779 g; 49.5/4.5/13.5; 169.8; -10.1 / 74.9; 146-176; aspen + titanal "Y-Beam" + fiberglass. Strong, versatile; vs 99Ti W, tuned for firmer conditions, precise carver, less playful; snappy, secure, encourages tight high-angle turns; best when driven, punishing backseat. Black Pearl 88, Ranger 90, Stance 88 W more forgiving in big moguls; 89Ti more stable than latter two, quicker than BP88.
- **Fischer Ranger 90** -- 123.9-89.5-113.4 (170); 16 m; 1764 & 1794 g; 63/3/20; 168.6; -7.7 / 76.6; 156-184; poplar + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Strong directional but prioritizes maneuverability over max precision; stability comparable to Stance 88 / Mindbender 89Ti; softer shovels ease initiation (vague on very firm); looser off piste; strong tail unforgiving in moguls for beginners/intermediates. Less forgiving but more predictable in variable than BP88. Ranger 102 for softer snow.
- **Majesty Havoc 90 Ti** -- 122.5-89.5-107.5 (178); radii 17/20/21/18 m; 1711 & 1712 g; 71/7/17.5; 176.8; -9.6 / 78.8; 164-184; poplar/ash + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Narrowest of Majesty directional freeride line; quick, standout off-piste maneuverability (very rockered tapered tips, low swing weight); costs firm edge hold and high-speed stability (Stance 88 W, Ranger 90, BP88 better). Floats better, more nimble; strong supportive tail for forward-stance skiers. Havoc 100 Ti for deeper snow.
- **Salomon Stance 88 W** -- 126.2-90-108 (174); 18 m; stated weight 1660 g; 55/6/12; 174.1; -9.6 / 77.5; 154-174; poplar/karuba + 2 titanal layers + C/FX inserts + fiberglass. Well-rounded for broad range of directional skiers; respectable suspension and high-speed composure, less demanding than peers. Not for backseat steerers. Ranger 90 / Havoc 90 Ti easier to pivot; Stance more secure on edge, easier in moguls than Mindbender 89Ti. Safe bet for strong but not super precise/demanding.
- **Shaggy's Belle 90** -- 136-90-116 (174); 16.25-17.9 m; 1760 & 1771 g; 52/5/16; 173.7; -8.5 / 78; 156-174; ash/poplar + magnesium sheets + carbon & fiberglass. Resembles piste skis but wider = more versatile; rockered minimally tapered shovel = quick carve initiation, precise; stiff minimally rockered tail = powerful, poppy finishes but harder to release/skid. Not as quick edge-to-edge as narrower carvers but more composed on cut-up groomers. Santa Ana 92 / Reliance 92 Ti more intuitive off piste. Fun carver in many on-piste conditions.
(Photo: skier Angel Collinson at Blister Summit 2024.)
- **Head Kore 91 W** -- 129.7-90.7-112 (170); 14.7 m; 1572 & 1575 g; 54.5/4.5/13.5; 169.1; -9.1 / 75.5; 149-170; karuba/poplar + graphene + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Smooth and composed on groomers; handles chop well for weight under a dynamic skier; less lively than Santa Ana 92/Reliance 92 Ti but not dead. For stiff, drive-the-shovels skiers who go off piste and want nimble; best Kore for mostly on-piste with regular off-piste.
- **Armada Reliance 92 Ti** (slightly revised 24/25) -- 130-92-116 (172); 17 m; 1822 & 1843 g; 52.5/9/20; 171; -9.3 / 76.2; 156-180; poplar + titanal + "elastic compound" + fiberglass. More high-speed composure; rocker and early taper = comfortable in variable vs similarly narrow skis; needs more input/speed to get on edge, then solid hold and stable on roughed-up groomers; predictable centered, unforgiving backseat. Stance 94 W livelier; good if Santa Ana 92 / BP94 seem too much in tight off-piste.
- **Atomic Maven 93 C** -- 126.9-93.4-111 (172); 17.9 m; 1638 & 1639 g; 57/3/17; 171.1; -8.7 / 76.9; 156,164,172; poplar + carbon stringers + titanal underfoot + fiberglass. One of most precise, energetic, agile here at cost of some stability and off-piste maneuverability; loves carving; for aggressive edgers and also progressing carvers wanting more accessible than Secret 96 / BP94 / Santa Ana 92. Mostly on piste. Maven 103 CTI (new) more maneuverable in variable.
- **Nordica Santa Ana 92** (series overhauled; replaces Santa Ana 93) -- 128-93.5-116 (179); 18 m; 2045 & 2070 g; 65/5.5/19.5; 177.6; -7 / 81.8; 150-179; poplar/beech + partial titanal + elastomer layer + fiberglass. Excellent suspension and calm high-speed composure, among best in section; touch more energetic, stable, precise than predecessor without being much more demanding. Next two skis more accessible. Top pick for stability and precision on firm and in crud. Santa Ana 102 more maneuverable and even more stable in chop.
- **Blizzard Black Pearl 94** (all-new, effectively replaces Black Pearl 97) -- 132-93.5-114.5 (176); 17 m; 1933 & 1953 g; 76/6/20.5; 174.2; -10.4 / 76.7; 152-176; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Strong, damp, stable but more lively, slightly more maneuverable, more accessible initiation than BP97 (one of more demanding women's skis). Among most predictable/composed in rough snow; more accessible than Santa Ana 92; more precise but less playful than Mindbender 99Ti. Works for broader range.
- **Dynastar M-Pro 92 W** (new) -- 127.5-93.5-117.5 (178); 17 m; 1762 & 1781 g; 71.5/5.5/23.5; 177.1; -8.6 / 80; 146-178; poplar/PU + fiberglass. Strong directional, not one-dimensional; strong tail, lively flex -- more you put in, more rewarding. Heavier skis calmer in rough snow; M-Pro 92 more agile and suited to dynamic style; not most precise on very firm but happy to pivot/slash/hop off piste. M-Pro 98 W better in chop/soft. For experienced skiers who dislike sluggish skis.
- **ZAG Harfang 96** -- 131.4-95.3-115.7 (170); 17.5 m; 1788 & 1793 g; 50.5/5.5/11.5; 169.1; -10.9 / 73.6; 170-188; poplar + phenol binding reinforcement + fiberglass. A bit "old school": decidedly directional, needs traditional forward stance; then surprisingly agile in tight spots (short tails) and stable in open terrain. Less rebound than Dancer 2 / Santa Ana 102 -> less likely to kick you backseat. Vs Dancer 2: Harfang looser and slightly damper; Dancer livelier, lower swing weight. Safe bet for directional skiers with good-great technique.
- **Faction Dancer 2** -- 126.7-95.6-116.6 (171); 17 m; 1787 & 1798 g; 71/4.5/23.5; 169; mounts Newschool -7.2 / 77.3, Progressive -8.7 / 75.8, Classic -10.2 / 74.3; 155-177; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Metal ski with modern twists; less one-dimensional than BP94/Secret 96, more energetic and maneuverable in tight terrain; still best with forward stance, pretty stable. Middle-of-road; intermediate to expert; a touch more playful/agile. Harfang 96 smoother; Dancer 3 for pow.
- **Volkl Secret 96** (subtle tweaks to multi-radius sidecut and tip construction) -- 138.5-96-121 (170); radii 24/38/15/20 m; 1883 & 1925 g; 42/4.5/11.5; 170.6; -12.1 / 73.2; 149-177; poplar/beech + titanal layers + carbon stringers at tip + fiberglass. A reference ski for More Stable. A/B tested old vs new: very similar, still excellent stability, suspension, edge hold; new is a bit more precise, energetic, eager to initiate carves; more carving-biased. Easier to get on edge at slow speeds. BP94 more energetic/agile; Harfang 96 or QST Lumen 98 for off-piste maneuverability.
- **Salomon Stance 94 W** -- 130.1-96.1-112.1 (174); 19 m; 1812 & 1817 g; 58/7/13; 174; -9.6 / 77.4; 154-174; poplar/karuba + 2 titanal + CFX inserts + fiberglass. Lower swing weight, more maneuverable than other metal directional skis; strong, needs shovel driving, then easy to release in bumps/trees, carves well. Stability comparable to Secret 96 but easier to flick; vs Santa Ana 92: more forgiving, less energetic/precise on very firm. For strong, dynamic skiers in tight/technical terrain.
- **Head Kore 97 W** -- 135-96-117 (170); 15.3 m; 1627 & 1653 g; 54/2/13; 168.9; -9.3 / 75.2; 156-177; karuba/poplar + graphene + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. For strong skiers wanting precision/power with low swing weight; excels on groomers; easy to release/pivot off piste with good technique; fun in chalk/slush; stable for weight. Lacks energetic flex and crud-erasing suspension of Santa Ana / Secret 96. Strong 1-ski-quiver case in Kore line; Kore 103 for off-piste/float.
- **Salomon QST Lumen 98** -- 132-98-120 (176); 16 m; 1885 & 1907 g; 62.5/5.5/34.5; 176.6; -8.3 / 80; 152-176; poplar + cork tips/tails + titanal binding reinforcement + flax/carbon + fiberglass. Intuitive, accessible, adaptable to conditions and styles; directional (forward stance to release tails) but low input; quick and easy to skid in tight terrain. Less precise/stable than Secret 96/BP94 but better high-speed composure than most More Forgiving skis. For aggressive maneuverability-lovers or progressing skiers.
- **K2 Mindbender 99Ti W** -- 113.5[sic]-98.5-119.6 (172); 15.4 m; 1928 & 1945 g; 58.5/3.5/24; 171.8; -10 / 75.9; 154-172; aspen + titanal "Y-Beam" + fiberglass. Still big fans; broad balance of maneuverability and stability -- aggressive skiers get composure and edge hold, intermediates find it intuitive. One of most versatile here. Similar to 4FRNT MSP CC but easier to release/slash and floats better; vs Secret/BP94 needs less aggressive driving, more forgiving, gives up some precision/top-speed stability. One of safer bets.
- **4FRNT MSP CC** -- 132.5-98.4-121.1 (171); 16.75 m; 1957 & 1974 g; 61.5/5.5/12.5; 169.9; -7.8 / 77.2; 159,165,171; aspen/maple + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Impressive all-rounder for directional skiers; not quick to punish yet can be pushed hard. BP94 / Secret 102 more planted at very high speed but more demanding. Latest MSP CC more precise, game-on, energetic than first gen (which some found "dead"), needs slightly better technique, less forgiving backseat. QST Lumen 98 / Mindbender 99Ti W float/pivot better; MSP more precise/lively on piste.
- **Dynastar M-Pro 98 W** (replaces E-Pro 99 W; quite different) -- 133.5-99.5-123.5 (178); 17 m; 1817 & 1818 g; 67/8/23; 177.2; -8.5 / 80.1; 154-178; poplar/PU + fiberglass. Strong, directional, more stable than maneuverable but impressively manageable in moguls/tight terrain unless tail-gunning; more stance-versatile than rearward-mounted predecessor. Quicker to pivot than Secret 96/BP94; more planted/precise than Havoc 100 Ti / Kore 103 W. Vs Mindbender 99Ti W: MB more forgiving, M-Pro quicker/livelier. For those wanting stiff/precise/stable but lighter and livelier.
- **Majesty Havoc 100 Ti** -- 129.5-99.5-114.5 (176); 21 m; 1815 & 1824 g; 66/6.5/17.5; 174.9; -9.8 / 77.7; 164-188; poplar/ash + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Different take on directional metal ski: low swing weight, nimble in tight grabby terrain, stable/precise when driven. Very tapered rockered tips lack engagement initiating carves on firm. Floats well for width; stay off stiff tail; tips twitchy in very rough crud. Similar to Kore 103 but not as light or stiff in shovels -> smoother on firm.
- **Volkl Secret 102** (unchanged) -- 141-101.7-123.8 (170); radii 26/16/23 m; 2010 & 2063 g; 59/3/17; 170.5; -12 / 74; 156-177; poplar/beech + titanal layers + carbon stringers at tip + fiberglass. One of most damp, stable women-specific skis ever tested; current version (since 23/24) a tad more accessible than prior. Crud-erasing; for advanced/expert prioritizing suspension and stability; surprisingly manageable at slow speed. Secret 96 snappier on piste. Dancer 3 / Stance 102 if too much ski.
- **Salomon Stance 102** (female reviewers on shortest length; male reviewers loved it last season) -- 132-102-120 (176); 23 m; 1882 & 1938 g; 51.5/8/9.5; 175.3; -10.4 / 77.2; 176,183,190 (limited low-end sizing). Quick and lively vs section, not drastically less stable; excellent stability-to-weight; fairly forgiving, stance-adaptable (mount forward for centered). Tails easy to break free off piste. Not engaging at slow speed on piste; struggles on very firm smooth snow. For hard/fast skiers who hate lifeless skis.
- **Head Kore 103 W** -- 129-100-119 (170); 15.2 m; 1578 & 1583 g; profile listed 22/2/61 mm [as printed, likely transposed]; 169; -8 / 75.6; 163,170,177; karuba/poplar + graphene at tips/tails + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Widest women's Kore; powerful and agile for aggressive-yet-dynamic skiing in soft snow; more stable when driving stiff shovels than almost any similarly light ski; respectable float. Low weight apparent in crud; not damp. Santa Ana 102 / Stance 102 / Secret 102 for rough snow.
- **Atomic Maven 103 CTI** (new) -- 132-103.5-121 (178); 18 m; 1744 & 1921 g; 57/6.5/21.5; 177.1; -9.7 / 78.9; 156-178; poplar/ash + titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Rare feat: stands out on piste and in powder. Needs good technique, forward stance; much lower swing weight than comparable skis; lively and agile, not muting; one of more precise carvers yet above-average flotation, surfy in pow. Dancer 3 for chop-busting.
- **Nordica Santa Ana 102** (replaces Santa Ana 104 Free) -- 135-103.8-125.4 (179); 18.5 m; 2125 & 2139 g; 67/5/36; 177.4; -7.1 / 81.6; 155-179; poplar/beech + 1 partial titanal + elastomer layer + fiberglass. More precise, slightly less maneuverable, and considerably heavier, damper, more stable than 104 Free; needs good technique, rewards with excellent composure; energetic when bent. Vs Secret 102: less easy to pivot, more lively. 104 Free fans who didn't want more demanding may look elsewhere.
- **Folsom Cash 106** (stock "Signature Series"; custom available) -- 138-104.6-124 (176); 18 m; 1884 & 1894 g; 61/5/36.5; 174.4; -6.7 / 80.5; 164-189; poplar/bamboo + fiberglass & carbon. Can be pushed hard but not as sluggish/demanding as Secret 102 / Dancer 3; above-average soft-snow maneuverability plus impressive stability and suspension. Customizable lighter/softer or heavier/stiffer. Intuitive (not exhilarating) on piste, respectable float. Alternative to DPS Kaizen 105 (if suspension > low weight) or more forgiving than Dancer 3.
- **DPS Kaizen 105** -- 134-105-118.2 (179); 18 m; 1762 & 1830 g; 49/6/22; 178; -10 / 79; 155-189; ash/poplar (horizontally laminated) + carbon. One of more nimble here but handles more speed/aggression than More Forgiving skis; not exceptionally damp but impressive suspension for weight; dynamic approach in variable; more versatile than Kore 103 W there. Low swing weight, surfy; needs effort to bend on piste (less than older DPS carbon). Strong 50/50 case.
- **Faction Dancer 3** -- 133.7-105.6-123.5 (172); 18 m; 1958 & 1960 g; 66.5/4/26.5; 170.4; mounts Newschool -7.6 / 77.6, Progressive -9.1 / 76.1, Classic -10.6 / 74.6; 164,172,178; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Widest in section but adaptable; one of best for fast skiing in chopped pow; strong, stable, lively, quick for size; floats well; holds edge on very firm. Secret 102 / Santa Ana 102 more planted; Dancer 3 more energetic and playful. Great daily driver for advanced/expert in variable. Dancer 2 for firmer.

**Blister Spectrums -- Women's More Stable** (best -> worst):
- Best on Ice: Santa Ana 92, BP94, Secret 96, BP88, Mindbender 89Ti W, Secret 102, BP84, Belle 90, Maven 93 C, Reliance 92 Ti, MSP CC, Stance 88 W, Kore 85 W, Stance 94 W, Ranger 90, Kore 91 W, Explo 86, Dancer 2, Mindbender 99Ti W, M-Pro 92 W, Havoc 90 Ti, Santa Ana 102, Maven 103 CTI, Harfang 96, M-Pro 98 W, Stance 102, Kore 97 W, QST Lumen 98, Havoc 100 Ti, Kore 103 W, Dancer 3, Kaizen 105, Cash 106 (worst).
- Best Suspension/Damping: Secret 102, BP94, Santa Ana 102, Santa Ana 92, MSP CC, Stance 102, Secret 96, Mindbender 99Ti W, M-Pro 98 W, Dancer 3, Harfang 96, Stance 94 W, QST Lumen 98, Cash 106, M-Pro 92 W, Havoc 100 Ti, Dancer 2, Belle 90, Mindbender 89Ti W, Reliance 92 Ti, Ranger 90, Stance 88 W, Havoc 90 Ti, BP88, BP84, Maven 103 CTI, Kaizen 105, Explo 86, Kore 97 W, Kore 103 W, Maven 93 C, Kore 91 W, Kore 85 W (worst).
- Best in Powder: Kaizen 105, Maven 103 CTI, Cash 106, Dancer 3, Kore 103 W, Havoc 100 Ti, QST Lumen 98, Santa Ana 102, Mindbender 99Ti W, Stance 102, M-Pro 98 W, Secret 102, Harfang 96, MSP CC, Dancer 2, Kore 97 W, Secret 96, Reliance 92 Ti, Stance 94 W, M-Pro 92 W, Maven 93 C, BP94, Havoc 90 Ti, Santa Ana 92, Ranger 90, Kore 91 W, Belle 90, Mindbender 89Ti W, Explo 86, Stance 88 W, Kore 85 W, BP88, BP84 (worst).
(Ad: Willi's Ski & Snowboard, Pittsburgh/Seven Springs -- Recommended Shop; free kids' gear under 5, junior buyback, $350 beginner packages, Blister+ deals.)

### Women's All-Mountain Skis -- More Forgiving (p.62)
Class overview: same women's framing. "Forgiving"/"Stable" are broad and lines blur. More Forgiving skis generally (1) work well at slower speeds, (2) don't immediately punish backseat skiing, (3) need less input/power. More Stable skis work best fast/aggressive; these are accessible, intuitive, easier at slow speed. Many suit beginners/intermediates wanting a dependable everyday ski, but many work for aggressive skiing in soft/consistent snow; finesse-oriented advanced/experts preferring lighter/nimble skis should start here.

- **Coalition Snow Rebel** -- STATED 124-85-106 (168); 17 m; stated ~1678 g; rocker/camber/rocker (subtle); 162,168,175; birch + fiberglass. One of Coalition's narrowest, still all-mountain; easy, adaptable on firmer snow; snappy, lively on groomers; not best for icy carving but predictable; one of most accessible here. Good for spending time off piste on narrow ski without demands; good for learning to carve beyond groomers. BP88 for firm edge hold.
- **DPS Wailer 90** (Wailer name revived) -- 127-90.5-111 (184); 15 m (all lengths); 1917 & 1961 g; 78.5/8.5/22.5; 181.6; -12.1 / 78.7; 152-184; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Extremely agile/maneuverable in tight, tricky terrain; rearward mount, strong flex encourage forward stance but not quick to punish; easier off piste than most ~90s; good in soft/variable; costs turn initiation and edge hold on very firm groomers (Meier BNK, QST Lux 92, Ripstick 94, Blaze 94 better on hardpack). QST Lux 92 less directional.
- **Meier Skis BNK** (made in Colorado) -- 133.2-92-124.4 (173); 20 m; 1840 & 1850 g; 55/5/42; 171.5; -9 / 77; 147-173; poplar/maple/beetle-kill pine + carbon & fiberglass. Accessible, versatile for lower intermediates to experts; nice on/off-piste mix; one of more damp/stable here; little rocker/taper but easy to skid, more playful than More Stable skis; secure on edge. Ripstick 94 W more nimble/energetic; tapered skis better in soft/grabby. For a bit of everything at ~90.
- **Salomon QST Lux 92** -- 127-92-115 (168); 14 m; 1695 & 1704 g; 50/6.5/22; 166.9; -8.8 / 74.7; 152,160,168; poplar + carbon/flax stringers + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Loose, surfy, easy in tight off-piste and variable, still carves fairly firm groomers when driven. Approachable for less experienced; fun for playful dynamic experts. Ripstick 94 W more precise on piste; BNK less knocked around at speed. Not so light/soft that speed limit is low. QST Lumen 98 for chop.
- **Elan Ripstick 94 W** (all-new) -- 129.5-93.5-109 (168); 15.8 m; 1550 & 1603 g; 50/3/20; 167.2; -7 / 76.6; 147-175; poplar/paulownia + carbon rods + carbon strips + flax tip layer + fiberglass. Easygoing, nimble, lively; now more playful -- easier to release off piste and wider stance tolerance; doesn't initiate carves quite as eagerly as predecessor. Good for progressing skiers and quickness-prioritizing experts. Blaze 94 more piste-focused. Not fatiguing nor sketchy.
- **Volkl Blaze 94** (also in 50/50; same specs) -- 132.5-94-118 (179); 1613 & 1631 g. More precise on edge, quicker initiation; not most stable, but quick and low swing weight. Works as downhill touring, 50/50, or resort ski for firmer/shallower days. Vs Ripstick 94: stiffer, better edge, less easy in tight off-piste.
- **Black Crows Camox Birdie** (same construction as Camox; shorter sizes, different graphic) -- 128-96-117 (168.1); 19 m; 1685 & 1731 g; 54/5/38; 165.7; -5.4 / 77.5; 156.1-174.2; poplar + fiberglass. More camber, less rocker/taper than many here = precise on and off piste; not as loose in moguls as Ripstick 94 W/Sheeva 9 but playful; energetic flex, forward mount, twin tail suit air and centered style. Vs Moment Sierra: more stable/precise, less maneuverable. Freestyle-friendly but directional reviewers liked it too.
- **Blizzard Sheeva 9** (unchanged after last season's overhaul) -- 129-96-118 (174); 16 m; 1751 & 1752 g; 79/4/41; 174.3; -7.7 / 78; 150-174; poplar/paulownia/beech + titanal + fiberglass. Versatile, fairly easygoing yet can be skied hard; suits progressing skiers (little speed/input) but impressive composure at speed/rough snow for confident skiers wanting non-demanding ski. Damper, more stable, easier to pivot than previous two but less lively/forgiving backseat. Most precise on firm, least stable in variable of Sheeva line.
- **Renoun Endurance 98** -- 133.2-96.8-118.4 (184); 18 m; 1901 & 1902 g; 68/4/29; 182.2; -10.1 / 81; 163-184; aspen + 6 VibeStop channels + 2 partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Splits Forgiving and Stable via VibeStop: calm and supportive when pushed fast (one of most stable here), softer and accessible at slow speed. Versatile: respectable on piste, loose off piste. Next ski holds edge better on very firm; Sierra more freestyle. Few match low-speed accessibility + high-speed composure.
- **Nordica Unleashed 98** -- 132.4-97.5-121.3 (174); 17.4 m; 1741 & 1747 g; 70.5/3/54.5; 171.8; -7.2 / 78.7; 144-174; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. One of Nordica's most playful, but suits directional skiers too; quick in moguls (more than Santa Ana), more planted at speed than lighter skis here; lively, playful and carves very, very well; accepts stances. Sierra looser; Camox more balanced. Bridges playful vs stiff directional.
- **Moment Sierra** -- 124.2-98.4-115.6 (172); 21.5 m; 1693 & 1710 g; 57.5/3/59.5; 170.2; -4.9 / 80.4; 152,162,172,179; aspen/pine + carbon & fiberglass. One of most playful; for centered stance; mounted 1-2 cm back, directional reviewers liked it (more precise on firm). Lively, nimble, not very damp. Most freestyle-friendly here (switch, tricks). One of most maneuverable ~100 women's skis tested. Deathwish 104 for more support.
- **Line Pandora 99** (overhauled; now a truly unisex collection with wide size range) -- 130.5-98.5-120 (170); 16 m; 1711 & 1748 g; 57/3.5/39; 169.3; -4.6 / 80.1; 149-184; aspen + fiberglass. Playful, more agile than damp, good suspension for weight; less directional and a bit heavier than predecessors -> more stance-versatile and composed at speed, more freestyle-friendly. Completo 100, Wailer 100, Sheeva 10 easier to pivot; Pandora more precise on piste (detune for surfier).
(Photo: Kara Williard, Crested Butte.)
- **Folsom Completo 100** (Signature Series pre-built; custom available) -- 129.3-98.9-119.3 (174); 16 m; 1666 & 1668 g; 70/3/43; 171.6; -5.3 / 80.5; 156,174,186; poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass. Highly tapered, deep rocker = extremely maneuverable in tight terrain and all snow; fairly centered mount for centered or forward; supportive middle, soft ends. Downside: vague initiation on firm, less composure than less tapered Pandora 99. Not wildly light/soft; dynamic skiers can push it in soft snow.
- **DPS Wailer 100** (new Wailer collection) -- 133.5-100.5-118.5 (184); 15 m (all lengths); 1916 & 1963 g; 95/9/27; 181.4; -12.3 / 78.4; 153-189; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Distinctly maneuverable, excels in quick tight turns; loose for directional skiers (tapered, deep rocker, tight radius); respectably damp at speed if you drive shovels from rearward mount, but not its strength (Ranger 102, Maiden 102 more stable). Floats very, very well for width. Not for high-angle carving on very firm. Stiffer, more directional than Completo 100.
- **Fischer Ranger 102** -- 136.3-101.2-126.1 (176); 18 m; 1880 & 1916 g; 66.5/3.5/35; 174.4; -10.1 / 77.1; 155-190; beech/poplar + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Could fit either section; easy initiation, good float, respectable stability; soft shovels easy to carve, much stiffer tails give power (punishing in moguls if centered/backseat). Surprisingly loose/surfy off piste; edge hold mostly from short section near binding. ZAG Slap 104 / Maiden 102 more forgiving. For surfy ride with stronger tail and better suspension.
- **Blizzard Sheeva 10** (returns unchanged) -- 134-102-123 (180); 17.5 m; 1851 & 1856 g; 75/4/39; 178; -7 / 81.5; 156-180; poplar/paulownia/beech + titanal + fiberglass. Lively, maneuverable, surprisingly precise; well-rounded for wide ability/style range; not most forgiving backseat; supportive underfoot. Vs Unleashed 98: a bit more demanding in tight terrain but floats/turns better in fresh. Vs Ranger 102: more secure on edge, less punishing. Safe do-it-all ~100 if no strong priorities.
- **Icelantic Maiden 102** (Maiden and Nomad redesigned 24/25) -- STATED 137-102-129 (176); 18 m; 1830 & 1835 g; 69/5.5/57.5; 173.1; mounts PC -4.8 / 81.8, BC -6.8 / 79.8; 155-176; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Gray area between directional and freestyle: not ultra-stable nor most playful, appealing combo; predictable; deep rocker, soft tips/tails playful in tight/soft; minimal taper + moderate weight = more stable than most similarly maneuverable skis. New version more energetic and stiffer, still easygoing.
- **ZAG Slap 104** -- 133.7-102.4-122.5 (176); 18.5 m; 1761 & 1778 g; 67.5/6/49.5; 174.1; -7.3 / 79.8; 164-188; paulownia/poplar + phenol binding reinforcement + rubber strip + fiberglass. Playful, maneuverable but not a one-trick pony; tapered, rockered, middle-of-road flex/mount for many stances. Predictable but unexciting on piste; shines in dynamic off-piste turns in soft snow. Handles speed/rough snow pretty well. ARW 106 / Maiden 102 for freestyle. For playful skiers who dislike very centered mounts or ultra-loose skis.
- **Line Optic 104** (returns unchanged; dropped "Blade") -- 131.8-103.5-123.2 (178); 19 m; 2074 & 2088 g; 56.5/4/44.5; 177.6; -6.7 / 82.1; 159-190; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Conundrum: heaviest in section, smooth/damp in rough snow, yet one of most playful (skis switch, balanced in air, loose, surfy, poppy). Stiff middle = decent edge hold; soft tips/tails forgiving at slow speed. Aggressive directional skiers may find shovels soft. For playful skiers going faster or directional skiers wanting easygoing planted ride.
- **Coalition Snow SOS** -- 125.2-104.3-119.5 (173); radii 40/25/44 m; 1955 & 1990 g; 64/4/46.5; 171.8; -2.9 / 83; 157-180; birch + fiberglass. Freestyle-friendly but strong and damp; centered mount prefers upright stance (like Sierra); may not feel intuitive to directional skiers; smooth planted ride. ARW 106 livelier on piste (SOS needs speed to get on edge); SOS looser, lower swing weight. Rafiki for pow.
- **Armada ARW 106** (updated) -- 131.5-105.5-124.5 (180); 20 m; 2010 & 2031 g; 53.5/8/50.5; 178.4; mounts Freestyle -2.6 / 86.6, Factory -5.1 / 84.1; 164-188; poplar + ash binding insert + fiberglass. Freestyle-minded but better for non-spinners now; one of looser, more playful here; great for natural features/park; directional reviewers found it intuitive, very maneuverable, stable fairly fast. Pandora 106 / Mindbender 106C W for on-piste precision.
- **Line Pandora 106** (new unisex; replaces women's Pandora 104) -- 133.5-105.5-125 (176); 16.7 m; 1912 & 1931 g; 58/3/41; 175.3; -4.9 / 82.8; 155-189; aspen + fiberglass. One of more intuitive and versatile; damper/more stable than predecessor yet quick and lively, more playful (centered stance, balanced). Resists deflection better than lighter skis; floats well; carves firm better than Stella 106 / ARW 106. Strong 1-ski quiver in snowy areas. Optic 104 similar design but heavier core.
- **K2 Mindbender 106C W** -- 137-105.6-125.5 (176); 17.3 m; 1961 & 1985 g; 57/3/32; 175.7; -8.5 / 79; 155-176; aspen + "carbon spectral braid" + flax & fiberglass. Reviewers even bigger fans; directional, forward stance (Summit 106, Pandora 106 more forgiving) but super maneuverable and agile; current version a tad more maneuverable and stable than original. Excels in soft snow/pow, predictable on firm, carves surprisingly well. More precise than Stella 106, a bit more stable than Pandora 106 / ARW 106.
- **Salomon QST Stella 106** -- 138.2-105.9-124.9 (173); 18 m; 1878 & 1891 g; 59/4/29; 171.8; -9.2 / 76.7; 157,165,173; poplar + basalt/carbon stringers + cork tips/tails + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Extremely impressive maneuverability + suspension + stability; doesn't demand forward stance though can be skied hard; easy to release in tight/variable; smooths rough off-piste better than most here; carves groomers that aren't ultra firm. Surfy all-mountain you can still ski aggressively.
- **Wagner Summit 106** (women's test, 172) -- 140-106-126.9 (172); 17.4 m; 1847 & 1854 g; 70.5/1.5/29.5; 170.4; -10 / 75.2; 165-186 (custom). One of most intuitive skis ever tested; carves as easily as it skids; traditional tip shape and deep low rocker = predictable everywhere; "forget about it when on it" (compliment). Suspension comparable to Stella 106 with better on-piste and lower swing weight; alternative if Pandora 106's centered mount unappealing.

**Blister Spectrums -- Women's More Forgiving** (best -> worst):
- Best in Deep Snow: Mindbender 106C W, Summit 106, QST Stella 106, Pandora 106, Wailer 100, ARW 106, Maiden 102, Sheeva 10, Ranger 102, Optic 104, Completo 100, Slap 104, Pandora 99, SOS, Sierra, Unleashed 98, Wailer 90, Endurance 98, QST Lux 92, Sheeva 9, Blaze 94, Ripstick 94 W, Camox Birdie, Meier BNK, Coalition Rebel (worst).
- Most Stable in Crud: Optic 104, QST Stella 106, Endurance 98, Summit 106, Ranger 102, Mindbender 106C W, ARW 106, Pandora 106, Sheeva 10, Maiden 102, Sheeva 9, Unleashed 98, Pandora 99, Slap 104, BNK, Wailer 100, Completo 100, Wailer 90, Camox Birdie, SOS, QST Lux 92, Blaze 94, Ripstick 94 W, Sierra, Rebel (least).
- Easiest in Tight Terrain: Sierra, QST Lux 92, Completo 100, Wailer 90, Ripstick 94 W, Maiden 102, Wailer 100, Slap 104, ARW 106, Summit 106, Pandora 106, Pandora 99, Optic 104, Sheeva 10, Mindbender 106C W, SOS, Blaze 94, Endurance 98, Unleashed 98, Camox Birdie, Sheeva 9, BNK, QST Stella 106, Ranger 102, Rebel (most difficult).

### Women's Powder Skis (p.76)
Class overview: same women's framing. Skis for deep soft snow; wider, more tip/tail rocker than All-Mountain sections for float and easy turning in deep snow; range from directional to playful. Powder days also include chop/crud, so blurbs cover tracked-out performance. Some skis repeat from other sections due to versatility; some viable as all-mountain skis in snowy areas.

- **Folsom Cash 106** (repeat; specs above) -- Above-average soft-snow performance for width plus versatility; enough weight to avoid bouncing in cut-up pow but quick/agile; less loose than Stella 106 but more secure on edge (nice with tired legs). Wider Folsom shapes in More Directional pow section for deep days. Fun at 1" or 1'.
- **Prior Flute** (Whistler; customizable) -- 132.2-104.9-119.8 (163); 17 m; 1500 & 1504 g; 73/2/26; 162.3; -9.2 / 72; 163,169,175; maple + fiberglass (custom). Very maneuverable, agile, handles fresh snow far better than width suggests; deep rocker + tapered shovel easier in deep snow than some wider skis; holds edge with speed; impressive damping for weight. Prefers forward stance but playful-like swing weight. Stella 106 or heavier custom Flute for chop. Good lift/human-powered split.
- **Faction Dancer 3** (repeat) -- Viable pow ski for directional skiers; biased toward bigger faster turns but manageable in tight ones; far from loosest, but one of best for stability in chop, crud, firm snow. Bump mount forward for centered stance. Line/Armada/Coalition skis for max playfulness.
- **Line Pandora 106** (repeat) -- Effectively new line (not just between old 104 and 110): more balanced/freestyle-friendly than old 104, heavier and much more stable than 110. Highly adaptable; floats well, surfy; not as stable in chop as heaviest here but not consistent-snow-only. ARW 106 / Valhalla 107 looser/more freestyle; Maiden 108 / Unleashed 108 more composed at speed -- Pandora fills middle.
- **Armada ARW 106** (repeat) -- Best with some soft snow; ARW 112 chosen (and used last year in Alaska) for deep untracked; ARW 106 excellent for shallower/chopped resort conditions; surfy, balanced, directional reviewers impressed by composure in heavy snow/chop (more accepting of forward stance than previous). Valhalla 107 quicker; ARW 106 more versatile at speed.
- **K2 Mindbender 106C W** (repeat) -- Rare float + maneuverability + stability for directional skiers in soft snow; one of best ~106 skis for flotation; looser in pow than on firm; can ski aggressively in soft chop; for shovel drivers but allows centered stance in pow. Stella 106 more forgiving/surfy; MB106C more energetic, floats better. Mindbender 116C W for deep days.
- **Salomon QST Stella 106** (repeat) -- Easy to release/pivot/slash even in deep snow, stance-tolerant, more predictable in chop than lighter skis. ARW 112 / Sheeva 11 bog down less in very deep snow, but Stella practical as widest ski. One of most adaptable here. QST Blank / QST X for deep-day specialists.
- **Wagner Summit 106** (repeat; mid-light "Factory" series option) -- Floats well enough for most pow days; not as loose as Stella 106 or Valhalla 107 but carves notably better on firmer snow and more forgiving than MB106C; not demanding; reviewers confident immediately. Wagner Summit 107 (in Ski Index) more planted.
