- **RMU Valhalla 107** -- 134-106.5-123.5 (172); 18.5 m; 1783 & 1815 g; 59/3/37; 170.9; -6.5 / 79; 162, 172; poplar + beech mounting zone + fiberglass. Not widest but one of most playful/maneuverable in deep snow; short effective edge, deep rocker -- easy to release/pivot/slash in pow, slush, firm. Floats well for width; best for centered upright stance; viable all-mountain for those who click; one of most freestyle-friendly. ARW 106 / Unleashed 108 carve better and encourage bigger turns. For pow stashes in tight terrain without a super wide ski.
- **Nordica Unleashed 108** -- 140.6-107.5-129.5 (180); 19 m; 2035 & 2083 g; 73/3/56; 178; -7.5 / 81; 162-180; poplar/beech + titanal layer + carbon & fiberglass. One of Nordica's most playful but stiffer and more precise than other freestyle-friendly skis here; adaptable to upright or forward stance; for directional skiers prioritizing quickness/pop, or freestyle skiers wanting firm-snow precision. Closest comp Sheeva 11: Unleashed more practical daily driver; Sheeva better float and soft-chop stability. Maiden 108 more forgiving/playful, less composed. Hard to make feel out of place.
- **Icelantic Maiden 108** (widest of overhauled Maiden) -- STATED 143-108-135 (176); 18 m; 1821 & 1835 g; 66/2.5/59.5; 173.2; mounts PC -4.4 / 82.2, BC -6.4 / 80.2; 162,169,176; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Impressive combo of float, easygoing maneuverability, composure at speed; centered or forward; middle of stable/damp vs quick/playful. Big rockered shovels plane well; blasts through chop respectably. Sheeva 11 stiffer for aggressive directional; ARW 112 for max deep-snow maneuverability.
- **Blizzard Sheeva 11** -- 139.5-111.5-129 (180); 19 m; 1959 & 1986 g; 77/3/42; 179; -7.6 / 81.3; 162-180; poplar/paulownia/beech + titanal + fiberglass. More impressive for versatility than as pow tool; easy to slash in fresh and chop; one of better ~112 carvers. Doesn't match flotation/maneuverability of next three in untracked; shines in resort-pow-day soft chop, drive strong minimally tapered tips through piles. Rafiki / ARW 112 easier in tight low-angle; MB116C floats better. Maiden 108 softer/more playful.
(Blister+ testimonial ad: "Peace of mind.")
- **Armada ARW 112** (new; arguably successor to classic JJ) -- 134.5-111.5-129.5 (185); 21 m; 1908 & 1984 g; 55/7/54; 183.4; mounts Freestyle -2.7 / 89, Factory -5.2 / 86.5; 165,175,185; caruba + ash binding insert + fiberglass. Considerably more well-rounded than Armada's previous pow skis; low swing weight, deep rocker, very easy to flick/slash; supportive and damp enough in heavy pow/soft chop to ski fast; balanced, freestyle-friendly but lets you drive shovels in dense/firm; upright stance in light deep snow. Rafiki for high-speed chop composure. Playful without being niche.
- **Coalition Snow Rafiki** -- 137-114-131.5 (180); 21.4 m; 2166 & 2178 g; 95/3/61; 178; -4.5 / 84.5; 157-180; poplar/birch + fiberglass. Simultaneously one of loosest/surfiest and one of most damp/composed in soft chop; little effort to pivot; heaviest ski here, so not bounced around in cut-up pow. Centered mount, freestyle design, but directional reviewers found it intuitive. Sheeva 11 / Unleashed 108 much better on piste and more versatile between storms. Soft-snow ski, not powder-only; few match it in dense chop and blower alike.
- **K2 Mindbender 116C W** -- 142.5-115-131 (177); 19.5 m; 1927 & 2009 g; 53/4/30; 175.9; -8.5 / 79.5; 163,170,177; (core continues next chunk). Excellent pure pow option for directional skiers: float and tight-terrain maneuverability plus stability in open zones; works driving shovels but doesn't require it; doesn't need very deep snow to be fun; heavy/stiff enough to drive through soft chop; less practical on firm or dust-on-crust.
  - (MB116C W cont.) core: aspen + "carbon spectral braid" + flax & fiberglass. Quite adaptable across pow-day conditions; Rafiki surfier at slow speeds in deep snow and more planted in dense chop, but MB116C floats better with lower swing weight for directional skiers.

**Blister Spectrums -- Women's Powder** (best -> worst; footnotes note shorter lengths would rank lower, longer higher, re the length-dependent rankings):
- Most Stable in Chop: Rafiki, Sheeva 11, MB116C W, ARW 112, Dancer 3, QST Stella 106, Unleashed 108, ARW 106, Cash 106, Maiden 108, MB106C W, Pandora 106, Summit 106, Valhalla 107, Prior Flute (least).
- Most Surfy/Loose: Rafiki, Valhalla 107, ARW 112, ARW 106, Maiden 108, MB116C W, Pandora 106, Flute, Stella 106, MB106C W, Unleashed 108, Summit 106, Sheeva 11, Cash 106, Dancer 3 (least).
- Most Flotation: MB116C W, Rafiki, ARW 112, Unleashed 108, Sheeva 11, Maiden 108, MB106C W, Pandora 106, Summit 106, ARW 106, Valhalla 107, Stella 106, Cash 106, Dancer 3, Flute (least).

### Frontside Skis (p.86)
Class overview: "frontside" is nebulous; Blister uses it for three groups: (1) dedicated carvers for groomers only; (2) skis that carve well and work in moguls; (3) mostly groomers/moguls with a bit of off-piste (steeps, trees). Many don't realize the best carvers (skinny, flat tail, tight radius) are often terrible in moguls/off piste, esp. grabby variable snow; off-piste design traits compromise on-piste initiation, edge hold, power. Think hard about where you want your frontside ski to shine.

- **Fischer The Curv** (returns; Fischer added many "Curv" skis) -- 120-69.8-102.5 (185); 17.5 m; 2432 & 2445 g (w/ binding plates); 42/6/7; 185; -11.5 / 80; 157-185; beech + 2 titanal + carbon "bridge" underfoot + carbon & fiberglass. Strong, damp, versatile carver sharing World Cup tech; intuitive for experienced skiers yet more top-end than recreational carvers; smooth suspension, high-speed composure, turn-shape versatility; needs effort to bend at 185 but less punishing than a race ski. For advanced/expert wanting a GS-esque piste ski. The Curv GT series more accessible.
- **K2 Disruption MTi** -- 119.6-73.3-102.9 (175); 18.1 m; 2225 & 2248 g (w/ plates); 39/5/4; 175.2; -10.6 / 77; lengths listed 165,160,175,180 [as printed]; maple/aspen + titanal + "Dark Matter Damping" inserts + fiberglass. High-performance high-speed carver, not an ultra-planted tank; livelier/lighter than The Curv with impressive stability and suspension; harder to initiate, needs speed/input; great for big GS turns with lots of energy. Less aggressive -> Disruption 78Ti/82Ti.
- **Rossignol Forza 70D V-Ti** -- 133.1-76.5-109.5 (173); 14 m; 1701 & 1706 g; 44/9/7; 172.5; -10 / 76; 163,173,181; poplar + ~1.5 layers titanal + carbon, basalt, fiberglass. "70" refers to 70 deg edge angle; one of more expert-oriented Forzas but not expert-only; fat tips + tight radius = quick initiation yet easy to feather/release; best carving hard across fall line; damper than weight suggests, but not an ultra-damp cruiser. For high-edge-angle carving without a demanding slalom ski.
- **Folsom Spar 78** (custom options) -- 122-77-106 (177); 17 m; 1675 & 1732 g; 51/6/11.5; 176.3; -9.1 / 79; 158-184; poplar/maple/bamboo + 90/10 fiberglass/carbon. Engaging carver that handles some off-piste; narrower Spar 88 (a favorite ~90 all-mountain ski for bumps/trees). Quicker initiation, more precise and lively than Spar 88; needs precise approach off piste but more viable all-terrain than most narrow skis here. Energy/agility over suspension; Folsom can build more planted.
- **K2 Disruption 78Ti** -- 122.1-77.3-108.2 (177); 17.8 m; 1832 & 1841 g; 47/6/9; 177.3; -10.6 / 78; 156-184; aspen + titanal + Dark Matter Damping + fiberglass. Particularly adaptable piste ski; composed in big GS turns even on non-pristine groomers yet easy to bend into tighter turns for advanced; surprisingly nice suspension on very firm mornings despite lowish weight; less effort than MTi. Forza 70D V-Ti / Disruption 78C better for intermediates wanting tight turns. Supershape e-Titan more planted; Disruption 82Ti for roughed-up groomers.
- **Head Supershape e-Rally** (Supershapes updated 24/25: tips narrowed a few mm, carbon weave around binding area, slightly thinner metal) -- 127.5-77.5-111.5 (177); 15.3 m; 2199 & 2211 g (w/ plates); 51/11/11; 175.7; mount N/A (sold with plate); 156-184; wood + 2 titanal + graphene + "Energy Management Circuits" + carbon & fiberglass. Tight-turning mid-wide Supershape; strong, damp, precise; advanced/experts push into tight carves; more predictable in bigger GS turns than prior thanks to less bulbous tips. Not the most accessible on firm low-angle slopes (needs force).
- **Parlor Warbird** (Boston; standard build tested, custom available) -- 130.9-77.7-107.1 (178); 16 m; 1724 & 1735 g; 67/4/9; 176.6; -10.8 / 77.5; 155-192; maple/poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Precise, lively, agile carver with accessible flex vs very stiff narrow skis here; wide, fairly soft shovels + 16 m radius = very easy on edge; light but energetic, predictable in consistent snow; one of easier skis to carve on icy groomers without lots of speed. Parlor can build stiffer/heavier for high speed.
- **Volkl Peregrine 80** (Peregrine series replaces Deacon) -- 126-79.5-109 (177); radii 27/15/23 m; 1941 & 1968 g; 35.5/6.5/10.5; 178.1; mount N/A (plate); 162-182; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Stable carver not demanding tons of speed/commitment; happy with cruisy moderate-angle turns, digs in for tight arcs when driven; similar to Disruption 78Ti but easier to bend; carves ice, easy to feather/release. Intermediate through expert for very firm to mixed conditions. Peregrine 82 more suspension/top-end.
- **Renoun Atlas 80** -- 126.9-78.5-111.6 (177); 15 m; 1728 & 1750 g; 55/7/13; 173.1; -6.2 / 80.4; 163-184; maple + 2 titanal + VibeStop + carbon & fiberglass. Very easy on edge, soft at slow speed, stiffer/more stable the harder you push (VibeStop). Fun on mellow slopes, surprisingly stable; fight tighter sidecut in huge fast turns (ski more centered). Speed-sensitive flex takes getting used to; Endurance models for off piste.
- **Volkl Peregrine 82** (new) -- 128-81.5-111 (177); radii 27/15/23 m; 2000 & 2016 g; 37/6/12; 178; mount N/A (plate); 162-182; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Precise, stable carver covering many on-piste conditions; for more advanced/aggressive skiers than Peregrine 80; better edge, damper at speed, solid icy grip; edge hold more binary (holds or breaks free). Not a race ski; many turn shapes like Curv GT 85 Redefine. For good-to-great technique.
- **K2 Disruption 82Ti** -- 124.6-81.6-110.7 (177); 18.4 m; 1911 & 1917 g; 44/4/13; 177.6; -10.9 / 77.9; 163-184; aspen + titanal + Dark Matter Damping + fiberglass. Widest K2 piste ski; similar ride but more predictable on cut-up groomers; impressive firm edge hold; demand between MTi and 78Ti (closer to 78Ti); hard for beginners/intermediates to get on edge; good suspension and pop for advanced. Elan Wingman 86 CTi for tighter turns/bumps; Peregrine 80 more accessible.
- **Kastle MX84** -- 126.5-82.5-111.5 (176); 16.3 m; 1940 & 1949 g; 53/7.5/9; 174.1; -11.6 / 75.4; 155-183; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Blurs piste/all-mountain but best appreciated carving groomers; excellent icy grip and lots of energy; not most forgiving -- less intuitive transitioning between skidding and high-angle carving. Off piste a bit more manageable than e-Titan/82Ti; MX88 much more predictable in grabby snow and big moguls. More agile than weight suggests; next two more planted.
(Photo: Jonathan Ellsworth at Taos.)
- **Stockli Montero AR** -- 127.5-83-113 (180); 17.4 m; 2058 & 2079 g; 47/9/11.5; 178.7; -12.3 / 77; 165-185; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Above-average suspension with middle-of-road sidecut; shovel easy to bend without hooky lurching; stiffer precise middle/tail = solid edge hold and powerful finishes; doesn't pull you into turns on low-angle slopes; needs deliberate unweighting of wide stiff tails in moguls, but more viable off piste than the other two on its page. Steadfast 85 DC more engaging at slow speeds.
- **Head Supershape e-Titan** (same 24/25 updates) -- 128.5-83.5-112.5 (177); 17.2 m; 2121 & 2147 g (w/ plates); 62/9/12; 175.4; mount N/A (plate); 156-184; wood + 2 titanal + graphene + Energy Management Circuits + carbon & fiberglass. Strong, precise, damp; more predictable than prior in soft/variable. Not snappy/easy at slow speed; not an off-piste ski for most. Excellent for aggressive carving on icy-to-chewed groomers; less damp than e-Rally and more GS-biased; impressive high-speed composure. e-Rally for tighter turns/lower angles.
- **Fischer The Curv GT 85 Redefine** (Fischer's "all-conditions carving" line overhauled) -- 133.5-84.5-117 (175); 15 m; 1837 & 1854 g; 50/6.5/8; 174.3; -11.5 / 75.6; 161-182; beech/poplar + 2 titanal + flax & fiberglass. Same specs as The Curv GT 85 but recycled/alternative materials, reportedly 36% lower CO2-equivalent emissions. Both: impressively precise, pretty stable, versatile carvers; less speed needed than previous RC One 82 GT; comfortable in big GS turns. Redefine a bit damper and less energetic than standard. (Standard Curv GT 85 is the cover ski.)
- **Nordica Steadfast 85 DC** -- 136-84.5-115.5 (179); 15.8 m; 2096 & 2154 g; 51/7/11.5; 177.7; -7.3 / 81.6; 162-179; wood + 1 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Predictable at speed and in rough snow while one of more accessible in class; not quite Peregrine 82 / Disruption 82Ti precision/edge hold but close when driven; suspension matches/beats them; easier to feather carve vs skid; far more viable off piste. Bridges piste-specific and 50/50 on/off skis.
- **Elan Wingman 86 CTi** -- 130.4-85.2-114.3 (184); 17.4 m; 2235 & 2236 g (w/ plates); 50/3/15; 182.9; mount N/A (system binding); 160-184; poplar/maple + carbon tubes + titanal layer + fiberglass. Decent all-mountain option for precision lovers and fun to carve; not-super-stiff tips easy to bend, good grip, tons of energy; off piste much easier to pivot and more forgiving than narrower skis here, but needs technique in tight bumps. Stance 90 for more off-piste time; Blizzard Anomaly 88 damper/more stable.
- **Head Kore 87** -- 129.5-86.6-109.3 (177); 16 m; 1627 & 1640 g; 65/5.5/21; 175.9; -10.6 / 77.9; 156-191; caruba/poplar + graphene + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Narrow but freeride-inspired; very low swing weight, surprisingly composed fast in consistent conditions; aggressive skiers can push it, intermediates can ski casually -- noteworthy range on "aggression spectrum." Capable off piste but punishing tails for backseat moguls. Heavier skis plusher. Supershapes for dedicated carving.
- **Black Crows Mirus Cor** -- 133.2-86.7-122.8 (178); 13 m (all lengths); 1796 & 1838 g; 50/2/40; 176.5; -3.9 / 84.4; 168.3-184.2; poplar + partial titanal plate + fiberglass. One-of-a-kind: 13 m radius, centered mount, twin-tip rocker, split tail. Blast once adjusted; strong preference for tight turns, near-effortless initiation (fat shovels, symmetric accessible flex); deep rocker = easy to release/slash, skis switch. Centered stance takes adjustment for directional skiers; better off piste than most ~87s but can be hooky in variable. Phenomenal for low-angle groomers.
- **Rossignol Arcade 88** (new) -- 134.5-87-123.5 (178); 15 m; 2045 & 2057 g; 57.5/7/13.5; 175.7; -6.7 / 81.2; 162-186; poplar + ABS stringer + titanal layer + elastomer & AirTip inserts + fiberglass. Powerful, exciting carver with twists: not eager at slow speed, but pressure the front and the middle/tail give excellent grip and launch you into the next turn; engaging on low-angle, supports aggressive edges on steep/fast. Not for long turns; nice suspension, not dead. Rossignol claims it can "literally ski anywhere"; Blister says most will prefer it as a piste ski that handles most on-trail conditions.
- **Blizzard Anomaly 88** (Blizzard discontinued many perennial all-mountain skis; effectively replaces Brahma 88) -- 127.5-87.5-109 (182); 18 m; 2047 & 2056 g; 73/7/16; 180.4; -10.4 / 79.8; 164-188; poplar/beech + full-width & partial titanal layers + fiberglass. Minimal taper, shallow rocker = intuitive and precise on firm, but more versatile off piste than Brahma 88 (which some found too piste-oriented). Strong, not light, best for advanced/expert; stay off tails and fine in moguls/trees/chalky steeps. Not as lively on piste as Brahma 88 but as good or better edge hold and suspension.
- **Dynastar M-Cross 88** -- 134.5-87.5-116.5 (184); 15 m; 1906 & 1907 g; 48.5/10.5/15.5; 183.4; -12.7 / 79; 159-184; poplar/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Bridges M freeride and Speed piste skis with its own personality: snappy, dynamic on groomers; little force needed to initiate despite more tip rocker/taper; good firm edge hold, easy to feather turn styles. More pop than suspension (catch air between carves) but smoother than Kore 87 / Maverick 88 TI. Not nearly as maneuverable off piste as M-Pro/M-Free, but not piste-specific.
- **Kastle MX88** -- 129-87.5-113.5 (181); 18.8 m; 1960 & 2004 g; 54.5/8/10; 179; -8.9 / 80.6; 160-188; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Feels very different from MX84: true all-mountain for the right skier (groomers, moguls, trees, variable). Backseat skiers look elsewhere (Line Blade, More Forgiving skis). Slower carve initiation than MX84 but composed in GS turns with impressive edge hold. Stance 90 easier/more agile off piste; MX88 predictable on and off piste for aggressive skiers.
- **Atomic Maverick 88 TI** (returns; Atomic added wider Mavericks) -- 125.3-87.9-108.9 (184); 19.5 m; 1823 & 1853 g; 60/5/16; 183; -8.5 / 83; 161-184; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. One of most piste-oriented Mavericks; very easy carve initiation, very good edge hold, lively; on-piste excitement rivals narrower skis. Not as easy to pivot or forgiving as Stance 90 / Wingman 86 CTi, esp. moguls, but holds edge better. Upper-intermediate/advanced groomer skiers; Maverick 95 TI more versatile off piste.
(Photo: Luke Koppa, Taos.)
- **Meier Quickdraw** -- 137.5-88-113.5 (181); 19 m; 2025 & 2028 g; 55/11/25; 181; -11 / 79.8; 156-186; maple/aspen/beetle-kill pine + fiberglass. One of Meier's narrowest/most piste-oriented, less niche than shape suggests; subdued initiation minimizes hookiness in big fast turns in variable but less engaging on low angles; not out of place off piste if out of backseat; nice suspension in rough snow (better than next two); not for truly carving ice. GS cruiser that dabbles in bumps. Meier Wrangler for all-mountain.
- **K2 Mindbender 90C** -- 131.5-89.3-117 (178); 15.3 m; 1735 & 1754 g; 45/6/21; 178; -8 / 80; 160-178; aspen + "carbon spectral braid" + fiberglass. Wider Mindbender design on agile firm-snow platform; deeper rocker, lower swing weight = above-average soft-snow maneuverability but "precise" not "surfy"; surprisingly secure on edge, aggressive carves. Strong tail hard to release in firm big moguls/tight trees -> more piste-oriented, viable off piste with precise dynamic technique. Doesn't mute harsh snow; Mindbender 89Ti for suspension.
- **Salomon Stance 90** -- 128.3-90.2-109.6 (182); 19 m; 1854 & 1863 g; 56/8/9; 181; -10.5 / 80.5; 168-188; poplar/karuba + 2 titanal + carbon/basalt inserts + fiberglass. Family resemblance to wider Stances; lots of all-mountain versatility at ~90; less eager to pull into turns than less-tapered skis, but far better maneuverability in bumps/trees/variable. Not ideal for firm low-angle carving, secure with speed. Balance comparable to MX88 but quicker/livelier (MX88 more stable). One of better here for lots of ungroomed time. Anomaly 88 for near-ice carving.
- **Shaggy's Brockway 90** (custom available) -- 136-90.3-116.1 (180); 17.5-19.25 m; 1833 & 1849 g; 60/6.5/15; 179.5; -8.8 / 81; 162-186; ash/poplar + nanoMAG magnesium stringers + carbon & fiberglass. "Supersized carver": "camber pockets" fore/aft of bindings + minimal taper = excellent firm edge hold (potentially best of the ~90s); nanoMAG improves suspension beyond weight. Not as quick edge-to-edge as narrower skis but shines on groomers with loose/variable/soft snow where narrow skis hook. Shaggy's Ahmeek 95 for all-mountain.
- **Wagner Summit 91** (semi-custom) -- 134-90.5-115 (182); 17.7 m; 2072 & 2094 g; 76/1/26; 179.8; -10 / 80; 168,175,182 (custom); aspen/beech + titanal layers + fiberglass. Impressive narrower all-mountain; not quite as maneuverable/forgiving in tight bumps as Stance 90 but close, and wins on turn initiation and high-speed composure. Like Summit 97 but more precise/exciting on piste, less easy to skid. Less punishing/less aggression needed than MX88 or Commander 92. Viable for many skiers.
- **4FRNT MSP 91** -- 129-91-116 (181); 16.5 m; 2047 & 2082 g; 55/6/15; 180; -8.5 / 81.5; 165-187; aspen/maple + 2 titanal + neoprene tip insert + fiberglass. 4FRNT's narrowest, most precise; like damp MSP 99 but firm-snow biased: surprisingly snappy and lively, quick across fall line, impressive edge hold, pop. Capable off piste for good technique (precise, not loose); easier in bumps/trees than Anomaly 88. Commander 92 more composed in big turns.
- **Northland AM178** (made in Steamboat; "built to carve") -- 138.5-91-123 (178); 15 m; 1808 & 1834 g; 57/10/13; 177.7; -8.5 / 80.5; 158,168,178; hickory/ash/beetle-kill pine + kevlar, carbon, fiberglass. Unconventional by today's standards: nearly full camber, tight radius, strong flex -> deep trenches; doesn't pull you into turns -- rewards deliberate technique (learning curve). Rare precision, energy, direct feel; livelier than heavier metal carvers. Handful in moguls/trees (no rocker, strong flat tail) but wider platform avoids bogging down. Traditional responsive ride; not for everyone.
- **Moment Commander 92** (first Moment ski in Frontside section) -- 130-91.5-112.5 (182); 18 m; 2063 & 2094 g; 50.5/8/20.5; 180.6; -9.9 / 80.4; 170-188; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Unlike other Moments: stiff, decidedly directional, little rocker, long effective edge; needs effort to bend; impressive edge hold, rare combo of high-speed composure and lively rebound. Slower initiation than MSP 91 / Anomaly 88 but GS-to-super-G turn shapes. Capable off piste but no sloppy technique. For those finding stable skis too planted/dead.
- **Kastle Paragon 93** (new 24/25) -- 126.5-91.5-113.5 (177); 16.4 m; 1820 & 1867 g; 59.5/8/17.5; 174.8; -11.3 / 76; 163-184; poplar/beech/paulownia + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Lighter, more rocker, more tapered than MX; still very precise, excels firm/shallow. Less damp/composed than MX88 but lower swing weight, less speed needed, more maneuverable in soft snow. Commander 92 / MSP 91 for stability; next ski livelier out of carves. Wider Paragons in All-Mountain More Stable.
- **Atomic Maverick 95 TI** -- 128.4-93.9-112.6 (180); 19.3 m; 1900 & 1908 g; 62/4/17; 178.9; -8.6 / 80.9; 164-188; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Blurs Frontside/All-Mountain; fairly light, not taxing, but strong flex and precise ride = powerful on edge; exceptional carver for width (effortless initiation, very secure, energetic). Less backseat-tolerant than Summit 91 / Stance 90; Anomaly 88 / Commander 92 smoother in rough snow. Exhilarating carver that still dips into trees/bumps (precise, not slarvy).
- **Folsom Spar Turbo** (custom) -- STATED 148-94-120 (182); 15 m; 2112 & 2116 g; 51/7/15; -7 / 81; 152-186; maple/poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass. Niche "fun carver" class (tight radius + all-mountain design; with Mirus Cor and Line Blade); Spar Turbo the stiffest and most versatile off groomers of those three; few skis easier to tip up and crank small aggressive carves. Spar 88 better 1-ski quiver; Spar 78 better on ice. Makes mellow groomers fun.
- **Line Blade** -- 154-95.3-124 (181); radius "tight"; 1999 & 2060 g; 49.5/6/27; 178.8; -7 / 82.4; 169,176,181; aspen + titanal sheet w/ cutouts + fiberglass. Oddball that doesn't make sense on paper (slalom radius, fairly soft flex, progressive mount, slight twin tip) but beloved by some reviewers. All about tight turns, also off piste; beginners to experts can bend into slalom turns with rebound; tail easy to feather/release -> surprisingly versatile off piste; enough mass to not be harsh. Nearly all others better on ice; aggressive directional skiers must adjust to softer flex. Addictive; Mirus Cor for firm snow.

**Blister Spectrums -- Frontside** (top -> bottom):
- Most Versatile (carving + moguls + off-piste) -> Most Specialized: Wagner Summit 91, Anomaly 88, Maverick 95 TI, Commander 92, Spar Turbo, Stance 90, Line Blade, MSP 91, Wingman 86 CTi, Paragon 93, Kore 87, M-Cross 88, Mirus Cor, MX88, Mindbender 90C, Maverick 88 TI, Brockway 90, Spar 78, Quickdraw, Forza 70D V-Ti, Steadfast 85 DC, Montero AR, Northland AM178, Peregrine 80, MX84, Peregrine 82, Disruption 82Ti, Supershape e-Titan, Arcade 88, Curv GT 85 Redefine, Disruption 78Ti, Atlas 80, e-Rally, Parlor Warbird, Fischer The Curv, Disruption MTi.
- Most Accessible -> Most Demanding: Line Blade, Mirus Cor, Spar Turbo, Wingman 86 CTi, Forza 70D V-Ti, Mindbender 90C, Stance 90, Spar 78, Kore 87, Summit 91, M-Cross 88, Quickdraw, MSP 91, Maverick 95 TI, Maverick 88 TI, Anomaly 88, Commander 92, Steadfast 85 DC, Paragon 93, Montero AR, Peregrine 80, MX84, Brockway 90, MX88, Warbird, Atlas 80, Curv GT 85 Redefine, Disruption 78Ti, Arcade 88, e-Rally, AM178, Peregrine 82, Disruption 82Ti, e-Titan, Disruption MTi, The Curv.
- Best in Moguls -> Worst: Stance 90, Line Blade, Spar Turbo, Kore 87, Summit 91, Commander 92, Anomaly 88, Maverick 95 TI, Wingman 86 CTi, MSP 91, M-Cross 88, Paragon 93, Mirus Cor, Mindbender 90C, Spar 78, Quickdraw, Maverick 88 TI, Brockway 90, Steadfast 85 DC, Montero AR, MX88, Forza 70D V-Ti, AM178, MX84, Arcade 88, Peregrine 80, Curv GT 85 Redefine, Disruption 78Ti, Peregrine 82, Disruption 82Ti, Atlas 80, e-Titan, e-Rally, Warbird, Disruption MTi, The Curv.

### All-Mountain Skis -- More Stable (p.104)
Class overview: generally stiffer, less tolerant of mistakes (backseat/steering from tails) than More Forgiving, but damper/more stable at speed in tracked snow, variable, roughed-up groomers. Used to be heavier than More Forgiving skis but not for past few years -- Blister "not exactly thrilled" since mass still correlates strongly with stability, though some brands are getting better at strong, directional, lighter skis that aren't harsh. Good edge hold, powerful finishes; good mogul skis for strong skiers, punishing for less experienced; unappealing for loose/playful or easy slow-speed initiation. A few could fit More Forgiving; noted.

- **Renoun Endurance 88** -- 126.5-86.5-110.5 (184); 17 m; 1939 & 1959 g; 61/7.5/24; 180.4; -10 / 80.1; 156-184; maple + 2 titanal + VibeStop + fiberglass. VibeStop on a damp, stable, versatile design: soft and easy slow, stiffer/stable fast. Maneuverable off piste yet holds up in open terrain; easier to pivot and more forgiving than Spar 88 but just as stable; tapered tips = slower carve initiation but very good edge hold when driven. Off-piste-biased. Endurance 98 more agile.
- **Folsom Spar 88** (version tested a bit heavier than standard Signature Series; custom) -- 131.9-87.3-115.8 (182); 17 m; 2008 & 2015 g; 63/2/13; 181.2; -10.6 / 80; 172-186; maple/bamboo/poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. One of best narrow skis for whole mountain incl. tight moguls/techy off piste; carves well, very easy to pivot, rarely hooky in soft snow. More forgiving than Anomaly 94 / Enforcer 94, nice suspension; slightly vague initiation from tip shape. Spar Turbo for tight piste turns; Cash 93 for off-piste maneuverability.
- **K2 Mindbender 89Ti** -- 129.8-88.3-113.6 (182); 16.6 m; 1915 & 1937 g; 56/7/16; 181.7; -10.6 / 80.3; 164-188; aspen + titanal "Y-Beam" + fiberglass. Optimizes performance all over the mountain: easy carve initiation, very good firm edge hold, energy; easier to release/slash than most narrow Frontside skis. Heavier skis damper but more stable than most lighter ones. Minimizes on/off-piste compromises. Mindbender 99Ti for soft snow.
- **J Skis Fastforward** -- 125.6-92-110.8 (181); 18.2 m; 2053 & 2059 g; 66/4/36; 178.9; -7.6 / 81.8; 160-187; maple/aspen + 2 titanal strips + fiberglass. Damp and stable without being stiff/demanding/locked-in; very good suspension, on more maneuverable/forgiving end; notably smooth, calm in firm rough snow; easy to pivot off piste, not quick to punish (Masterblaster more easygoing). Most ~90s here better for truly carving very firm groomers; uncommonly playful for class.
- **Folsom Cash 93** (mid-light construction tested; custom) -- 128-92-118 (185); 19 m; 1952 & 1964 g; 65/5/18; 183.5; -8.8 / 83; 170-190; poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass. Standout for maneuverability/agility in firm-snow all-mountain ski; particularly nimble, better off piste than most narrow skis; tapered tips not hooky, but subdued initiation vs Spar 88; still carves firm groomers once used to it. Mid-class suspension. Anomaly 94 / Enforcer 94 for max precision/suspension.
- **Black Crows Serpo** -- 131.1-92.3-114.9 (180); 20 m (all lengths); 1824 & 1835 g; 53/3/20; 179.1; -7.8 / 81.8; 168.2-186.3; poplar + 2 H-shaped titanal plates + fiberglass. Like narrower Justis: very maneuverable, floats well, yet surprisingly powerful, exciting carver; not plush but can be skied hard; less punishing than Justis -- middle ground between stiff skis here and More Forgiving. Rare combo of playful maneuverability and edge hold. For dynamic, light-on-feet skiers who drive hard. Stormrider 95 more rearward mount, less rocker.
(Ad: blacksheepsports, Munich -- Recommended Shop; owner Sebastian Steinbach, MasterFit bootfitter; one of only EU shops carrying WNDR Alpine; GEAR:30 Ep.183.)
- **Blizzard Anomaly 94** (Blizzard overhauled directional all-mountain skis; replaces Bonafide 97 and Brahma 88 lineage) -- 133-93.5-114 (182); 18.5 m; 2138 & 2163 g; 65/6.5/15.5; 180.7; -10.5 / 79.8; 164-188; poplar/beech + full-width & partial titanal + fiberglass. Slightly softer, lighter, more rockered than Bonafide 97/Brahma 88, but still one of the most damp, precise, stable, demanding skis in class; a bit easier to bend and less punishing. For directional skiers with good technique wanting planted composure; calm and predictable except in deep snow.
- **Dynastar M-Pro 94 Ti** (new; shift in Dynastar's directional line) -- 127-93.5-117.5 (178); 17 m; 1762 & 1769 g; 70/8/21.5; 177.1; -8.6 / 80; 154-186; poplar/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Less directional than previous M-Pros (wider tail, less tapered tip, less rearward mount, more tail rocker); still best for forward stance but maneuverable, agile, even "playful." Not most damp but fun agility/energy with enough suspension for firm days; blast in tight terrain; carves well; not bogged in soft snow. Strong yet forgiving.
- **Nordica Enforcer 94** (updated; evolution not revolution) -- 128.5-93.5-116 (179); 17.5 m; 2024 & 2035 g; 63.5/7/17; 177.8; -6.9 / 82; 167-185; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Stands out for suspension, stability, precision; not forgiving but more well-rounded: livelier and less punishing off piste; quick carve initiation, good edge hold, rebound. Not quite as planted as Anomaly 94, but more energetic and lower swing weight.
- **Stockli Stormrider 95** -- 131-94-119 (182); 18.5 m; 1821 & 1838 g; 47/6.5/10.5; 180.4; -10.2 / 80; 170-188; poplar/beech/paulownia + titanal layers + "Freeride tip & tail" inserts + fiberglass. A surprise last season: rare blend of agility, suspension, versatility; fairly light for metal class but distinctly supple; knocked around more in crud than heavy skis but not harsh on firm. Easy on edge, holds carves, many turn shapes; nimble, lively off piste; forward stance but large sweet spot (directional and playful reviewers liked it). Agility/energy over max composure.
- **Head Kore 93** -- 135.5-94.6-116.7 (184); 17.7 m; 1783 & 1809 g; 60/5/21.5; 183; -11.5 / 80.5; 156-191; caruba/poplar + graphene at tips/tails + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. For stiff-flex lovers who dislike heavy swing weight; quick and powerful, can push hard on groomers, easy to flick off piste, can skid casually; not forgiving in tight trees/big moguls (backseat). Impressive high-speed composure for weight but not plush in deep chop. Aggressive in soft or smooth firm snow with quick adjustments.
- **ZAG Harfang 96** (182 tested) -- 132.7-95.2-116.9 (182); 20 m; 2008 & 2043 g; 55/5/11.5; 182.1; -12 / 79; 170-188; poplar + phenol binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Predictable, consistent across conditions; nice suspension for firm days; tapered shovels not hooky in soft/variable; agile in tight spots for proficient skiers. Very rearward mount, minimal tail rocker -> drive the shovels. Less engaging initiation on moderate groomers; Dancer 2 livelier. Middle ground between light and heavy skis here.
- **Volkl M7 Mantra** (7th iteration) -- 138.5-95.5-120 (184); radii 26/41/18/23 m; 2111 & 2144 g; 43/3.5/10.5; 183; -12.2 / 79.3; 163-191; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Quite similar to M6: for technically proficient skiers valuing precision, stability, support on firm groomers and in bumps/trees. Main difference: quicker, more eager carve initiation (more exciting on piste) but needs more focus/technique to pivot in variable/big moguls. Mantra 102 or Wagner Summit 97 for off-piste maneuverability.
- **Faction Dancer 2** (182 tested) -- 126.6-95.6-116.4 (182); 19 m; 1981 & 1991 g; 61/6/24.5; 180.5; mounts Newschool -7.8 / 82.5, Progressive -9.3 / 81, Classic -10.8 / 79.5; 163-187; poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Directional metal ski with new-school twist; not very forgiving/loose nor supremely planted; low swing weight + strong flex + precise = fun agility, energy, support; rewards aggressive-yet-dynamic style with airs. Very impressive initiation/edge hold on firm despite non-carving shape. Reviewer favorite for those who hate "dead" skis.
- **Salomon Stance 96** -- 131.9-96-113.9 (182); 20 m; 1946 & 1968 g; 56/9/12; 181; -10.5 / 80.5; 168-188; poplar/karuba + 2 titanal + carbon/flax inserts + fiberglass. Middle of bell curve: fairly stiff, damp, light metal ski balancing on/off piste; respectable carver (short of M7 Mantra / Anomaly 94) but much more accessible in bumps/trees; quicker, less fatiguing than heavier skis. "Jack of all trades" for directional skiers. Stance 102 for mixed conditions.
- **Wagner Summit 97** (semi-custom) -- 134.2-96.3-118.8 (182); 19 m; 2165 & 2186 g; 79/3.5/30; 179.6; -9.9 / 79.9; 161-182 (custom); maple/ash + titanal + fiberglass. Go-to recommendation: excellent suspension/stability without overly demanding feel; mutes rough snow on par with M7 Mantra / Enforcer 94 but considerably more forgiving and maneuverable; deep low rocker + minimal taper = easy off-piste pivoting plus impressive carving. Heavy swing weight but remarkably approachable. Stiffer/softer flex options or full custom.
- **Fischer Ranger 96** -- 128.5-96.6-119.6 (180); 18 m; 1935 & 1962 g; 66/3.5/21; 178.7; -7.8 / 81.6; 159-187; beech/poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. For off-piste maneuverability with more support than More Forgiving skis; more forgiving and easier to skid than older Ranger 99 Ti, but strong tail wants you out of the backseat. Easy initiation, fairly good edge hold (Dancer 2 / M7 Mantra for very firm). Balanced swing weight vs suspension. Ranger 102 for soft snow.
- **Peak 98 by Bode** -- 128.5-97.2-116.5 (184); 25 m; 2034 & 2060 g; 55/7/19; 183.7; -10.3 / 82; 160-190; paulownia/ash + 2 partial titanal + fiberglass. Peak's do-it-all resort ski; race-derived "Keyhole" construction and long radius yet immediately intuitive for directional reviewers; bends into many turn shapes, impressive edge hold, not engaging on low angles. Easier to pivot/more forgiving than M7 Mantra; rewards forward stance. Smooth suspension + manageable swing weight. Peak 104 by Bode more maneuverable.
(Photo: Luke Koppa, Crested Butte.)
- **Shaggy's Mohawk 98** -- 135.5-98-119.2 (186); 20.5-22.5 m; 2079 & 2089 g; 70/3/22; 185.5; -8.5 / 84.5; 162-186; ash/aspen + nanoMAG magnesium strips + carbon & fiberglass. Defies convention: very deep rocker -> easy release, admirable pow performance; but very directional flex with much stiffer tails (punishing backseat, supportive for experts). Surprisingly light swing weight for mass; below-average crud composure. For directional skiers wanting More Forgiving-like looseness but stiffer.
- **K2 Mindbender 99Ti** (benchmark ski; spec example in intro) -- 133.3-98.3-119.3 (184); 19.6 m; 2085 & 2120 g; 60/2.5/26; 184; -10.6 / 81.4; 166-190; aspen + titanal Y-Beam + fiberglass. Exceptional versatility, "well" to "excellently" everywhere; not best-in-class suspension/high-speed composure but more agile, maneuverable, forgiving than Mantra 102 while heavy/stiff enough for aggressive high-speed skiing; deep rocker easy at slow speed; carves really firm groomers, lively. One of easiest to recommend in category. Mindbender 89Ti more piste-oriented.
- **4FRNT MSP 99** -- 134-98.5-122 (181); 18 m; 2114 & 2130 g; 63.5/6/14; 179.6; -8.3 / 81.5; 171-187; aspen/maple + 2 titanal + fiberglass. 4FRNT's do-it-all directional ski with freeride traits; latest less easygoing than v1 (esp. backseat in moguls) but more agile than Enforcer 99. Snappy initiation, precise hold, lively exits; stay over shovels off piste; wide rockered shovels plane/blow through soft piles; moderate weight balances suspension and quickness. For high-speed composure + lively ride.
- **Black Crows Justis** -- 137.9-98.6-122.2 (183); 21 m; 2078 & 2138 g; 68/3/33; 181.7; -7.5 / 83.3; 171-189; poplar + 2 H-shaped titanal plates + fiberglass. Not middle-of-road: deep rocker = one of looser in class; almost no taper = very good initiation/edge hold; but can feel unpredictable/hooky in big fast turns in variable (detuning helps slightly); stiff tails punish backseat. More stable/precise alternative to Mohawk 98.
- **Nordica Enforcer 99** (replaces Enforcer 100) -- 133.5-99-121.5 (185); 18.5 m; 2199 & 2229 g; 63.5/6/26.5; 183.6; -7.1 / 84.7; 167-191; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. More than a 1 mm narrowing: more lively, a touch more forgiving, slightly more maneuverable, better balanced than heavy, backseat-intolerant Enforcer 100. Calm and predictable off piste with energy; precise initiation, great edge hold, rebound. One of better here for high-speed stability, suspension, precision. Mantra 102 more maneuverable.
- **Kastle Paragon 101** (middle of new freeride line; successor to FX concept) -- 134-99.5-120.5 (182); 18 m; 2032 & 2037 g; 59/9/17.5; 180; -11.5 / 78.5; 169-190; poplar/beech/paulownia + 2 titanal + Hollowtech tip insert + fiberglass. Directional, low swing weight, secure on edge; initiates and holds carves very well despite taper; lively. Suspension on really firm not great; drive hard in soft snow, conservative in rough. Enforcer 99 smoother; M-Pro 100 Ti / Declivity X 102 more forgiving/playful.
- **Dynastar M-Pro 100 Ti** (most directional freeride skis overhauled) -- 133.5-99.5-123.5 (186); 18 m; 1967 & 1972 g; 69.5/8.5/23; 185; -8.9 / 83.6; 162-186; poplar/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass. Fills gap between old M-Pro and playful M-Free; ride/stability like old M-Pro 99 but considerably more maneuverable, stance-versatile, less directional; better in air. Easy to bend, mid-class edge hold; easy to release/slash off piste. Knocked around more in crud than heavier skis but not sluggish/chattery. For aggressive directional skiers wanting some playfulness.
- **Majesty Havoc 100 Ti** (expanded freeride series; from 110 Havoc Ti) -- 130.5-99.5-115.5 (188); 23 m; 2032 & 2040 g; 68/7/16; 186.8; -10.2 / 83.2; 164-188; poplar/ash + partial titanal + fiberglass. Tapered rockered tips = above-average float, not hooky in grabby snow; but slow carve initiation (vague tips, long radius need speed/input). Agile off piste if off its tails. Mantra 102 blows through variable better. More maneuverable than Paragon 101.
- **Liberty Radian 100** (Radian replaces Evolv) -- 134.5-100-122.5 (179); 19 m; 1731 & 1750 g; 55.5/7/17.5; 179.1; -13.8 / 75.7; 167-185; poplar/ash + partial titanal + fiberglass & carbon. Very directional (mount), more tapered with slightly deeper rocker than Evolv. Light, far more agile than damp; easy in moguls if not steering from strong tails; carves well; most surprising: surfy and loose in soft snow, rivaling More Forgiving skis. Quick, fun carver that outperforms width in fresh snow.
- **Head Kore 99** -- 136.4-100.6-121.8 (184); 18.5 m; 1816 & 1819 g; 59/4.5/22; 183; -11.1 / 80.4; 156-191; caruba/poplar + graphene + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Powerful but light; for directional skiers who stay out of the backseat; quick through technical terrain; impressive damping for weight but heavier skis for crud. Push hard on smooth groomers/chalk; can skid casually. Dancer 2 livelier.
- **Armada Declivity X 102** (tweaked; same shape as Declivity 102 Ti, X 115-inspired) -- 134.5-101.5-124 (180); 18 m; 2026 & 2038 g; 55/7/21; 179.1; -8.5 / 81; 164-188; poplar + partial titanal + elastic compound + fiberglass. Very similar to predecessor: slightly damper/more stable, touch looser. Strong and just heavy enough to ski hard yet more playful than most metal skis; centered or load the shovels and launch. Vs M-Pro 100 Ti: M-Pro more precise, Declivity surfier.
- **Volkl Mantra 102** -- 141.3-101.8-124.1 (184); radii 30/19/27 m; 2166 & 2237 g; 60/2.5/21; 182.9; -11.3 / 80.1; 170-191; poplar/beech + titanal layers + carbon tip stringers + fiberglass. Go-to for experienced skiers wanting very smooth ride and excellent high-speed stability everywhere; current version less punishing in tight terrain than original. Not agile/easygoing but surprisingly accessible for class; excellent on piste for width; easier to release than M7 Mantra. Mantra 108 more chop composure; M7 snappier on piste.
(Photo: Dylan Wood.)
- **Stockli Stormrider 102** -- 134.5-102-124.5 (182); 19.8 m; 1998 & 2010 g; 64.5/5/13; 180; -11.7 / 78.3; 173,182,191; poplar/beech/paulownia + titanal layers + "Freeride tip & tail" inserts + fiberglass. Light, agile, uniquely supple; leans off piste, surprisingly easy to pivot/slash; less engaging/energetic on piste than SR95 but more composed at speed/rough snow; mutes small vibrations; floats well despite conservative rocker. Decidedly directional, strongly prefers forward stance. Stance 102 more balanced.
- **Salomon Stance 102** (183 tested) -- 134.3-102-120.8 (183); 23 m; 2019 & 2024 g; 58/6/11; 182.3; -10.2 / 81.2; 176,183,190; poplar/karuba + 2 titanal + carbon/basalt inserts + fiberglass. Immediately intuitive; first iteration demanded high input, current far more approachable. Decent carver (bigger turns, not icy train tracks); shines off piste with supportive-not-wildly-stiff flex and agility; excellent suspension-to-weight, energetic. Not drastically less stable than damper skis in soft snow; suits dynamic style in steep/tight terrain. Rarely out of place.
- **Parlor Cardinal Pro 102** (full custom available) -- 141.1-102.5-129.1 (182); 18.5 m; 1976 & 2028 g; 66/4/16; 180.8; -12.8 / 78; 154-196; maple/poplar + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Long-standing Parlor shape plus titanal; highly capable carver for width; handles mixed conditions well for a fairly light quick ski; adaptable off piste with weight forward; impressive damping without sluggishness. Narrower Cardinal Pro 93 via semi-custom program with many graphics.
- **Nordica Enforcer 104** (replaces reference ski Enforcer 104 Free) -- 136-104-126.5 (185); 19 m; 2273 & 2297 g; 60.5/6/27.5; 183.8; -7.4 / 84.6; 167-191; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + elastomer + fiberglass. Quite similar: impressive suspension, intuitive everywhere; touch stiffer/less forgiving in big tight moguls (less tail rocker/splay); slightly more stable in chop, more precise on firm, more energy. Most 104 Free fans will enjoy it. Mantra 102 looser.
- **Atomic Maverick 105 CTI** (new) -- 134-104-123 (185); 19 m; 2014 & 2038 g; 57/2.5/20; 184.2; -9.8 / 82.3; 170-191; ash/poplar + titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Much more true all-mountain than Maverick 88/95 TI; agile in tight terrain and surprisingly surfy in pow (Bent-like inspiration); decidedly directional, punishing backseat; not very damp but much better in variable than narrower Mavericks; standout carver for ~105.
- **Romp Oso 105** (stock "Romp Ready"; customizable) -- 140.5-104.5-130.5 (188); 20 m; 2215 & 2231 g; 57.5/6/40.5; 186.4; -7.2 / 86; 172,180,188; ash/aspen + carbon stringer + fiberglass. Newest Romp shape for steep technical terrain: better suspension than most lighter skis, moderately tapered/rockered for easy release and speed-shedding. Less precise on low-angle groomers than Enforcer 104/Mantra 102; more maneuverable in soft snow and accepting of centered stance. Romp Zorro for quick/playful.
- **DPS Kaizen 105** (184 tested) -- 135.4-105.2-119.1 (184); 18 m; 1867 & 1908 g; 52/7/26; 183.5; -9.7 / 82; 155-189; poplar/ash + carbon. Flagship lightweight carbon construction, very impressive damping-to-weight; not as harsh on firm as light competitors; tapered, moderately rockered = very maneuverable; easier to bend/engage than previous Pagoda skis without less stability. Strong 50/50 case for dynamic skiers.
- **Folsom Cash 106** (188 tested; lighter resort construction, "Directional Rocker") -- 138-105-124 (188); 21 m; 1989 & 2000 g; 73/4/32; 185.5; -8 / 84; 164-189 (custom). Very intuitive, adaptable; versatility is defining trait -- rarely out of place. More nimble than damp/precise; low swing weight, easy release, but keeps stability/edge hold. Folsom Primary and Giver for high-speed stability; Completo for max maneuverability.
- **ZAG Harfang 106** -- 139.5-105.1-124.1 (186); 21.5 m; 2118 & 2128 g; 53/3/16.5; 185.1; -13 / 79.5; 178,186,192; poplar + phenol binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Reliable composure and respectable suspension in soft/variable while agile; not twitchy in chop; lower swing weight and less hooky than more stable skis (tapered rockered tips). Rearward mount, strong flat tail -> for shovel drivers who like precise finishes. Damper/more stable alternative to Paragon 107.
- **4FRNT MSP 107** -- 139-106-128 (187); 21 m; 2366 & 2370 g; 59/6/17.5; 185.4; -8.8 / 83.8; 171-187; aspen/maple + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Substantial; impressive suspension and high-speed composure in chop/crud; current version "more ski" (heavier swing weight, less forgiving tail) and more energetic than v1; carves well for width. Conservative rocker hampers deep-snow float vs Mindbender 108Ti / Declivity X 108. Less easygoing but more accessible than Chargers while comparably capable at speed.
- **Wagner Summit 107** (semi-custom) -- 142.7-106.1-128 (186); 19.6 m; 2258 & 2303 g; 75/4/35; 183.8; -10.5 / 81.4; 165-186 (custom); aspen + titanal + fiberglass. Excellent at absorbing/muting harsh vibrations in rough snow but not demanding; very impressive carver yet easier to release off piste than many here. Lighter skis for quick dynamic turns. Excellent suspension in accessible package; Wagner can make stiffer charger version; Summit 106 (50/50) more agile.
- **Kastle Paragon 107** (new) -- 139.5-107.5-128.5 (184); 19.2 m; 2066 & 2083 g; 62/8/18; 182.5; -11.3 / 80; 168-192; poplar/beech/paulownia + 2 titanal + fiberglass. Narrower Paragon traits on soft-snow silhouette; still precise on firm though initiation not snappiest; looser and maneuverable in soft snow; low swing weight. Heavier skis glide through rough snow better; push hard in soft. "Lighter, more precise ZAG Harfang 106"; forward stance (tails punish backseat). MSP 107 for high-speed composure.
- **K2 Mindbender 108Ti** -- 135.9-107.4-124.5 (186); 22.8 m; 2220 & 2237 g; 64/4/19.5; 185.7; -9.5 / 83.3; 172-193; aspen + titanal Y-Beam + fiberglass. Smooth heavy metal ride in a soft-snow design; deep rocker = very easy to release/slash, excellent deep pow performance vs ~108s; feels like short platform on firm (prefer previous skis or MB99Ti for firm days). Float + maneuverability + high-speed stability in pow and chop; stiff tail, but less demanding than Chargers.
- **Armada Declivity X 108** (new) -- 129-107.5-120.5 (184); 24 m; 2161 & 2167 g; 55.5/5/30; 182.8; mounts Modern -5.7 / 85.7, Factory -8.7 / 82.7; 176,184,191; poplar + partial titanal + elastic compound + fiberglass. Between Declivity X 115 and X 102, closer to 115: impressive float/maneuverability in soft snow; surfier and more stable in big turns than 102, less engaging on piste and less lively. Strong narrower pow-oriented setup; middle weight/flex; prefers forward stance. Summit 107 / Countach 110 more practical in firm.
- **Black Crows Corvus** (original ski overhauled; very different) -- 136.5-109-119.5 (186.2); 25 m; 2008 & 2049 g; 53/3.5/34.5; 184.7; -6.1 / 86.2; 169.4-191.4; poplar/beech + partial titanal + fiberglass. Previous was directional, reverse-cambered, long effective edge; new has more forward mount, tapered tips/tails, symmetric rocker -- one of more agile/maneuverable here; still stiff cambered middle and long radius allow big fast soft-snow turns. Not as fun to carve or damp in crud as predecessor. For centered skiers wanting quickness without soft Freestyle-section flex/tight radii.
- **Moment Countach 110** -- 139.6-110-130 (188); 21 m; 2078 & 2131 g; 63/5/31; 186.7; -7.8 / 85.5; 176-194; poplar/beech + carbon & fiberglass. More than a gap-filler between Commander and Wildcat; worked for broad range of reviewers; lighter/quicker/more maneuverable than many here yet more supportive/damp/stable in chop than More Forgiving and Freestyle skis. Best in soft/variable; holds edge fine, floats well, blast in cut-up pow. For blurred directional/playful style. New narrower Countach 104 to be tested.
- **Heritage Lab FR110** (inspired by long-discontinued Rossignol Sickle) -- 134.5-110.5-129 (193); 27.2 m; 2534 & 2543 g; 62/0/32; (continues).
  - (FR110 cont.) 193.7 cm measured; -6.5 / 90.4; 180,186,193; bamboo/poplar + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. Significant mass = remarkably smooth in chop; but progressive mount and fully rockered profile make it very easy to pivot, hiding heft; favors neutral stance, fairly forgiving backseat. No other very damp, highly rockered, surfy 110 like it. Upcoming "R110" (same shape/construction, nearly flat profile) for directional skiers/firmer snow.
- **Fat-ypus D-Sender** -- 144.3-111.8-130.1 (184); 25 m; 1921 & 1927 g; 76/3/59; 180.9; -7.7 / 82.7; 164,174,184; poplar + fiberglass. Defies categories: almost fully cambered, little taper, long radius, but light, twin tail, mount allowing centered or forward. For directional skiers wanting edge hold + low weight, or playful skiers preferring precision. Not damp nor easiest to pivot; floats well without being sketchy on firm; compelling 50/50. Corvus looser.

**Blister Spectrums -- All-Mountain More Stable** (best -> worst):
- Best on Ice: M7 Mantra, Anomaly 94, Mindbender 89Ti, Enforcer 94, Serpo, Dancer 2, Enforcer 99, MSP 99, Mantra 102, Stormrider 95, Spar 88, Mindbender 99Ti, Kore 93, M-Pro 94 Ti, Paragon 101, Peak 98 Bode, Enforcer 104, Harfang 96, Radian 100, Summit 97, Endurance 88, Cash 93, Fastforward, Cardinal Pro 102, Justis, MSP 107, Maverick 105 CTI, M-Pro 100 Ti, Stance 96, Paragon 107, Stormrider 102, Stance 102, Ranger 96, Declivity X 102, Mohawk 98, Kore 99, Summit 107, Oso 105, Havoc 100 Ti, Harfang 106, Kaizen 105, Cash 106, D-Sender, Corvus, Countach 110, Declivity X 108, Mindbender 108Ti, Heritage FR110 (worst).
- Best Suspension/Damping: Heritage FR110, Summit 107, Mantra 102, MSP 107, Enforcer 104, Enforcer 99, Summit 97, Enforcer 94, Anomaly 94, Mindbender 108Ti, MSP 99, Oso 105, Countach 110, M7 Mantra, Stance 102, Endurance 88, Harfang 106, Spar 88, Mindbender 99Ti, Declivity X 108, Stormrider 102, Fastforward, Peak 98 Bode, M-Pro 100 Ti, Harfang 96, Cash 106, Declivity X 102, Stance 96, Maverick 105 CTI, Justis, Cardinal Pro 102, Paragon 107, Stormrider 95, Corvus, Dancer 2, Kaizen 105, Ranger 96, Havoc 100 Ti, M-Pro 94 Ti, Paragon 101, Mohawk 98, Cash 93, Mindbender 89Ti, D-Sender, Serpo, Kore 93, Kore 99, Radian 100 (worst).
- Best in Powder: Mindbender 108Ti, Declivity X 108, Countach 110, Summit 107, FR110, Corvus, Kaizen 105, Maverick 105 CTI, Paragon 107, D-Sender, Oso 105, Cash 106, Harfang 106, Justis, Enforcer 104, Mohawk 98, Mindbender 99Ti, Havoc 100 Ti, Paragon 101, Stormrider 102, Declivity X 102, Stance 102, Mantra 102, MSP 107, Peak 98 Bode, M-Pro 100 Ti, Radian 100, Cardinal Pro 102, Summit 97, Ranger 96, Kore 99, MSP 99, Enforcer 99, Harfang 96, Dancer 2, Stance 96, M-Pro 94 Ti, Stormrider 95, M7 Mantra, Serpo, Mindbender 89Ti, Cash 93, Enforcer 94, Endurance 88, Fastforward, Anomaly 94, Kore 93, Spar 88 (worst).

### All-Mountain Skis -- More Forgiving (p.126)
Class overview: diverse group that (1) works well at slower speeds, (2) won't immediately punish backseat skiing, (3) needs less input/power than Chargers and More Stable skis. Accessible, intuitive, easier at slow speeds, with important qualifiers per ski. Many suit beginners/intermediates wanting a dependable everyday ski, many also handle aggressive skiing in soft/consistent snow; finesse-oriented advanced/expert skiers preferring lighter/nimble skis should look here.

- **Line Optic 88** (new; replaces Blade Optic 92) -- 121-87.5-110.5 (184); 17.9 m; 1933 & 1977 g; 56.5/3.5/37.5; 183.4; -7.4 / 84.3; 149-184; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Notably better suspension and high-speed stability than predecessor; very maneuverable and playful for width but matches ride quality of similarly heavy More Stable skis; deep rocker, progressive mount = easy off piste. Great to progress with, fun for experts wanting nimble/surfy but not knocked around in shallow crud. Not for icy carving. Equal on/off piste.
- **ZAG Mata Ti** (new) -- 141.5-90-118 (178); 14.5 m; 1980 & 1981 g; 43.5/9.5/19.5; 177.1; -9.4 / 79.2; 163-178; poplar + partial titanal + quadraxial fiberglass + rubber inserts. Eye-catching asymmetrical shape but very approachable and versatile; short radius + soft shovels = easy tight carves on moderate slopes; tails forgiving and easy to free in moguls/trees. Not great on scraped-off groomers but predictable; blast on softer. Vs Line Blade: Mata quicker, more energetic; Blade holds edge slightly better and more composed at speed.
- **DPS Wailer 90** (revived Wailer: less complex, more affordable, made in Utah) -- specs as in women's section. Particularly nimble and maneuverable for width; tapered, rockered tips + tight radius = easy to slash and carve tight turns; doesn't encourage aggressive angles on very firm; outperforms dims in 3D snow. Forward stance but easy; advanced can push it. Optic 88 / QST 92 for centered stance.
- **Salomon QST 92** -- 129-92-117 (184); 16 m; 1893 & 1897 g; 55/6/19; 183; -9.3 / 82.2; 152-184; poplar + carbon/flax stringers + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. As versatile as wider QSTs, maybe more; easy to slide/skid/pivot for beginners, can be pushed hard by experienced quick/surfy skiers in tight terrain; performs above width in soft snow; more firm precision than QST 98; mutes harsh snow fairly well. Next ski / Frontside for eager carving. Optic 88 for more composure.
- **Meier Wrangler** -- 135.5-93-118.3 (185); 17 m; 1947 & 1962 g; 57/3/18; 185; -10 / 83; 170-185; poplar/maple/beetle-kill pine + carbon & fiberglass. Hard to categorize: damper, more stable, precise than many here but not as demanding as More Stable skis; minimal taper = reliable firm grip; turn-shape versatile. Not as easy in tight moguls/trees as other ~95s; keep pressure on shovels. Carves well, not harsh, doesn't need perfect technique. Optic 88 more playful.
- **Shaggy's Ahmeek 95** (made in Michigan; custom available) -- 131.5-94.9-116.1 (180); 20-22 m; 1808 & 1823 g; 57/1.5/45; 179.3; -6.6 / 83; 162-186; ash/poplar + carbon & fiberglass. "Camber pockets" fore/aft of bindings (like Ahmeek 105) = very good firm grip despite deep rocker for easy slashing. Less damp/stable than 105 but handles speed/big turns better than Season Aero / Mindbender 96C; centered or forward stance. Not for tight piste carving; quick off piste with better edge hold than similarly maneuverable skis.
(Ad: Spokane Alpine Haus -- Recommended Shop; "always say yes" motto, season leases, adding second location and bikes for spring 2025; 1350 Google reviews at 4.9.)
- **Season Aero** -- 123.6-95.2-119.8 (180); 17 m; 1883 & 1906 g; 45/8/40; 178.6; -2.8 / 86.5; 158-180; paulownia + 2 titanal + fiberglass + steel tail insert. Near-symmetric shape (park-like) but metal; much more centered mount -> upright balanced stance; nearly fully cambered with lots of camber underfoot yet easy to slash in shallow/firm snow; fairly light but really good suspension. Directional shovel-drivers need to adjust. Among best center-mounted skis at this width for smoothing rough snow; carves and slashes. Season Kin much softer (Freestyle).
- **Line Blade** (repeat) -- Excellent for very tight turns (short radius, big minimally tapered tips) but tail easy to release/feather -> forgiving and playful. Heavy swing weight and big shovels cumbersome in tight moguls; fine in widely spaced bumps/trees. Nice suspension, energetic. Makes you think about the whole mountain in a new way; reviewer favorite.
- **K2 Mindbender 96C** -- 131-95.5-119 (178); 16.7 m; 1831 & 1844 g; 55/5/27; 178; -9 / 80.5; 166-184; aspen + "carbon spectral braid" + flax & fiberglass. Lighter, more playful counterpart to MB99Ti: less suspension/high-speed stability but notably quicker on and off piste; directional, forward stance; low swing weight and deep rocker for quick slashes; tips pull into carves, impressive edge hold for class. Backseat-prone -> next three or K2 Reckoner. MB106C looser/more planted.
- **Elan Ripstick 96** (updated) -- 133-95.5-113.5 (182); 17.8 m; 1735 & 1741 g; 48/5/17.5; 181.3; -8.4 / 82.2; 161-189; poplar/paulownia + carbon rods + carbon strips + flax tip + fiberglass. Keeps nimble, lively ride, adds maneuverability/playfulness; not as composed in rough snow as heavier skis; very easy to flick/slash/carve. Vs previous: looser, slower carve initiation, more stance-adaptable. Great to progress with; experts with active light style. Next two for stability and freestyle.
- **RMU Apostle 96 Ti** -- 134-95.5-119 (184); 18.5 m; 1894 & 1919 g; 65/5/26; 182.5; mounts FR -5 / 86.5, TR -7 / 84.5; 176, 184; poplar + beech mount plate + 2 partial titanal sheets + fiberglass. Apostle 96 with two tapered titanal sheets: extremely playful, maneuverable, with unexpected precision/stability; easy to pivot/slash on snow and in air; stance-adaptable, forgiving backseat. Ripstick 96 / Camox better firm edge hold; impressive on piste for its off-piste maneuverability. Next ski similar with better suspension.
- **Line Optic 96** -- 129.1-95.6-118.7 (184); 18 m; 2077 & 2096 g; 59/3/42; 183.8; -7 / 84.9; 163-184; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Metal construction + freestyle-like design; more stable at speed than nearly all previous skis here yet engaging when not pushing; smooth on rough snow but lively and poppy without needing much speed; loose, surfy off piste. Not for lots of very firm carving. Rare suspension + maneuverability + playfulness for directional and freestyle skiers. Optic 88/104 for firmer/softer.
- **Black Crows Camox** -- 131.5-96.4-120 (186.5); 20 m; 1925 & 1934 g; 54/9/38; 184.6; -6.1 / 86.2; 168.1-186.5; poplar + fiberglass. Wide spectrum of skiers can appreciate it: drive the shovels, but energetic and balanced in air. Beginners better on softer QST 98/Optic 96; great for improving intermediates and dynamic experts (precision + energy). Heavier skis smoother; others looser off piste. Precise mostly cambered ski without set-back mount or punishing tail. Black Crows Atris for soft snow.
- **Renoun Endurance 98** (repeat; 184) -- VibeStop: forgiving when easy, damper/more supportive when pushed. Versatile, intuitive; intermediates to advanced. QST 98 looser/playful; Rustler 9 more precise on very firm.
- **Blizzard Rustler 9** -- 131-97-121 (180); 17 m; 1880 & 1887 g; 78/3/41; 178; -8 / 81; 162-186; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. For quickness, precision, energy; despite deep rocker, not as loose as many here, but initiates/holds/finishes carves with more precision and power than most; with detuning more maneuverable than More Stable skis. Next few smoother/surfier. Camox if worried it's too light; Ripstick 96 even more nimble.
- **ZAG Slap 98** -- 130-97.5-116.5 (180); 20 m; 1830 & 1860 g; 58.5/7.5/35; 178.5; -7.3 / 82; 160-187; paulownia/poplar + phenol binding reinforcement + rubber strip + fiberglass. Freestyle-friendly but directional skiers could love it; accessible, maneuverable, easygoing; easy to carve but stands out for releasing/pivoting in moguls, trees, soft snow; intuitive with forward stance unlike many freestyle skis. Practical swing weight/suspension balance. Rustler 9 better edge; Pandora 99 more composed at speed. Good for dipping into freestyle.
- **Salomon QST 98** -- 133.1-97.7-120.9 (189); 18 m; 2054 & 2063 g; 67/3/42; 187.7; -8.5 / 85.4; 169-189; poplar + carbon & flax stringers + cork tips/tails + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Highly maneuverable yet well-rounded; deeper rocker and more taper than most -> great soft snow, loose surfy ride; weaker edge hold/initiation than Rustler 9 / Pandora 99; handles tight, steep, technical terrain very well; impressive suspension for agility. Broad range: first mogul line to experts preferring agile/playful. QST 92 for groomers; QST 106 for soft snow.
- **Line Pandora 99** (184 tested; unisex overhaul) -- 130.5-98.5-120 (184); 19.5 m; 1976 & 2000 g; 61.5/3/38; 183.5; -5 / 86.8; 149-184; aspen + fiberglass. Shapes/rocker/mounts like Line Optic; playful, balanced, surfy, but carves surprisingly well, works with directional stance, nice suspension (much more stable than prior Pandora 94). Quicker, less damp than Optic 96. Versatile middle ground: "a more damp and composed Rustler 9, or a more agile J Skis Masterblaster."
- **J Skis Masterblaster** -- 132.5-98.6-117.9 (181); 18 m; 2112 & 2149 g; 71/4.5/33; 178.6; -7.6 / 81.7; 160-187; maple/aspen + 2 titanal strips + fiberglass. Easy at moderate speeds, yet one of most damp and stable in category; smooths chop; heavier swing weight but easy to release, doesn't need speed. Not energetic/precise on scraped groomers but less one-dimensional than similarly damp skis. For intermediates wanting smooth predictable ride, or advanced wanting damp + accessible + playful. J Hotshot wider; J Fastforward for firm.
- **Folsom Completo 100** (186 tested; Signature Series; custom) -- 129.3-99-119.6 (186); 18 m; 1851 & 1868 g; 60/4/42; 186.3; -6.8 / 85; 156,174,186. Maneuverability and playfulness standout: very tapered, rockered, fairly soft ends -> easy to pivot/slash in firm moguls or powder; costs firm precision and high-speed stability but impressively stable in soft-ish snow for weight. Completo 105 and 113 for softer conditions.
- **Romp Zorro 100** ("Romp Ready" stock or custom) -- 136.7-99.3-129.7 (183); 17 m; 1800 & 1824 g; 58/4/50; 181.4; -6 / 84.7; 155-190; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Particularly adaptable, easy, rarely out of place; easy on edge, enough taper for tight terrain; tested with twinned tail and progressive mount -> fun all-mountain-freestyle ski. Stock construction light, not very damp, extremely poppy. Few weaknesses for nimble-ski lovers. Romp Oso 105 for stability.
- **Icelantic Nomad 100** (overhauled best-seller) -- STATED 135-100-127 (188); 20 m; 1893 & 1925 g; 65/7/61; 185.1; mounts "Progressive Center" -4.9 / 87.7, "Boot Center" -6.9 / 85.7; 171-188; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Similar shape/rocker/flex but notably lighter and livelier without much loss of high-speed stability. Pretty soft, surfy, agile, easygoing; holds up at speed surprisingly well for playful advanced skiers. Not for very firm carving or stiff-flex fans. Nomad 106 more supportive.
- **DPS Wailer 100** (repeat; middle of revived family) -- Dramatically tapered, deep rocker, tight radius: extremely easy to slash off piste, engaging on piste (not for really firm carving). Familiar Wailer/"RP" shape now in simpler, less expensive, damper construction than old carbon versions; made in DPS's Utah factory. Great for tight terrain and soft snow without a wide ski, esp. for those who like stronger skis with rearward mounts.
- **Rossignol Sender Soul 102** ("Soul" name revived; very different from Soul 7) -- 134-102-124.5 (180); 17 m; 2097 & 2098 g; (continues). Very accessible, doesn't punish sloppy technique; far more forgiving than previous Sender 106 and ...
  - (Sender Soul 102 cont.) 57/7.5/27.5; 180.1; -7.1 / 83; 156-188; poplar + partial titanal + ABS stringer + rubber layers + "Air Tip" insert + fiberglass. ...Sender Free 104. Unlike tapered, rockered Soul 7 it uses a traditional shape/rocker; easy to bend and slash yet a very fun carver; big shovels not knocked around much in chop. Not very stable relative to whole market but respectable suspension/composure within this section. Broad demographic -- easy for intermediates, reviewers pushed it hard in soft steeps/moguls/groomers.
(Blister+ testimonial ad.)
- **Fischer Ranger 102** (183 tested) -- 137.5-102.2-127.8 (183); 19 m; 2057 & 2061 g; 70/4/34; 181.2; -10 / 80.6; 155-190; beech/poplar + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Could fit More Stable: easygoing/surfy sometimes, powerful/precise others; soft shovels ease carving; middle of ski grips firm snow; low swing weight, loose off piste; stiff tails punish backseat more than most here (beginners off piste -> next two or Optic 104). Impressive stability for nimbleness. Ranger 108 for pow.
- **Liberty Scope 104** (new; follows Origin series) -- 133-103-123 (188); stated 22.5 m [printed "cm"]; 1778 & 1792 g; 57/8/47.5; 186.4; -6.7 / 86.5; 164-188; poplar/ash + phenol binding plate + fiberglass. Nimble, very playful; low swing weight, progressive mount; carves surprisingly well for how loose it is; not damp but handles speed well for weight; stance-intuitive. More playful than Origin 106 (lower, more balanced swing weight, more accessible flex). Viable all-mountain-freestyle ski; lighter, better-carving alternative to Slap 104 or surfier, softer Rustler 10.
- **ZAG Slap 104** (188 tested) -- 137.2-103.3-125.9 (188); 20 m; 1997 & 2001 g; 69/4.5/47; 186.2; -8.3 / 84.8; 164-188; paulownia/poplar + phenol + rubber strip + fiberglass. Middle-path versatility; fairly light, quick, better suspension than Scope 104; tapered/rockered = loose in tight grabby snow; predictable but unexciting carving. Mount a couple cm forward for centered stance. Next ski for on-piste excitement; Black Crows Atris more precise/lively.
- **Blizzard Rustler 10** -- 136-103.5-126 (186); 18.5 m; 1956 & 1976 g; 78/3/41; 184; -7.8 / 84.2; 162-192; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Nimble, fairly playful, fun on piste; stance-versatile; more playful than More Stable skis and more forgiving than Ranger 102; quick initiation and impressive edge hold despite deep rocker; floats well, easier to slash in soft snow. Mid-pack suspension but energetic. Optic 104 or new directional Anomaly series for planted.
- **Line Optic 104** (185 tested) -- 131.6-103.7-123.2 (185); 20 m; 2230 & 2290 g; 59.5/3.5/44.5; 184.4; -7 / 85.2; 171-190; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. One of most playful AND most stable here; mass smooths rough snow; skis switch, balanced in air, loose, surfy; far from dead, more energetic than J Hotshot; stiff middle for edge hold, softer ends to bend/butter. Aggressive directional skiers may want more shovel support. Optic 96 narrower/quicker.
- **Black Crows Atris** -- 137.2-103.9-121.8 (184); 20 m; 1946 & 1962 g; 59.5/3.5/41; 182.6; -7.9 / 83.4; 172-190; poplar + fiberglass. Long-standing with strong following; low swing weight, deep rocker, lively flex = playful (mount 1-2 cm forward for freestyle) yet can be driven directionally; latest has better on-piste performance than older wider versions. Sakana / Rustler 10 better tight carving; Apostle 106 surfier/more forgiving; more equal on/off balance than Slap 104. Not smooth/planted.
- **Line Sakana** -- 149-104.4-137.2 (181); 16.3 m; 1869 & 1873 g; 56/3/18; 180.8; -10.9 / 79.5; 166,174,181; paulownia/maple + carbon/flax stringers + fiberglass. Like a lighter, more all-mountain Line Blade concept: wide soft shovels, minimal rocker, snappy flex = blast for tight hard carves; not hooky, easy to slash, floats well. Knocked around in rough snow; larger skiers may want stronger tail in bumps. Pandora 106 more versatile; Blade smoother. Cure for boredom with jack-of-all-trades skis.
- **RMU Apostle 106** (RMU flagship) -- 139-105-127 (184); 20 m; 1999 & 2012 g; 62/4/30; 182.6; mounts FR -4.9 / 86.4, TR -6.9 / 84.4; 176,184,192; poplar + beech mounting plate + fiberglass. Exceptionally playful and maneuverable with added stiffness/stability; super quick, little effort to slash; carves well for how surfy it is. Optic 104 / Hotshot far damper; Apostle far easier to flick, respectably damp for agility. Surprisingly well-rounded. Apostle 96 more precise on piste; 106 shines off trail.
- **Shaggy's Ahmeek 105** (custom available) -- 140.3-105.2-128.7 (186); 20-22.5 m; 2202 & 2209 g; 54/3/40; 185.4; -7 / 85.7; 162-186; poplar/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Fits both sections: damp, composed at speed, fairly playful; camber pockets = above-average edge hold for width; long-ish radius needs speed; excellent carver; deep rocker = maneuverable (not as loose as next ski). Heavier swing weight but balanced in air. Alternative to Optic 104 with better edge hold and stronger flex.
- **J Skis Hotshot** -- 133.5-105.2-123.1 (183); 18 m; 2295 & 2344 g; 75/2.5/43; 180.6; -7.6 / 82.7; 170-189; maple + 2 partial titanal + fiberglass. Rare breed: exceptionally damp/smooth in rough snow yet not demanding; hefty build + moderate flex mute crud; soft tips/tails, strong middle, deep rocker, progressive mount = playful, forgiving, forward or centered. Aggressive directional skiers may want stiffer; not for low swing weight. Makes challenging conditions easier at any pace.
- **Line Pandora 106** (183 tested; repeat) -- 133.5-105.5-125 (183); 18.4 m; 2025 & 2090 g; 56/4/43; 182.3; -5.2 / 86; 155-189; aspen + fiberglass. Optic 104 design with lighter, more energetic core: stance-flexible, surfy, quicker and livelier; carves as well or better despite no metal. Not wildly light, can be pushed. Optic 104 for planted.
- **K2 Mindbender 106C** -- 137.5-105.5-126.5 (183); 18.9 m; 2050 & 2084 g; 53/2.5/30; 182.9; -9 / 82.5; 169-189; aspen + "carbon spectral braid" + flax & fiberglass. More than a 108Ti without metal: similarly surfy in soft snow, gives up some high-speed stability for lower swing weight; carves really well (better than most at width incl. 108Ti); damp for weight; strong tail dislikes backseat moguls; excellent float for width; much more maneuverable in soft than firm. Pandora 106 more forgiving/freestyle.
- **Icelantic Nomad 106** (replaces Nomad 105, "face of Icelantic for decades") -- STATED 141-106-133 (188); 20 m; 2022 & 2029 g; 69/5.5/60; 185.2; mounts Progressive Center -5 / 87.6, Boot Center -7 / 85.6; 171-188; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Similar shape/rocker/flex: maneuverable, playful, accessible; much quicker and more energetic without being much less stable in soft/consistent snow; carves soft groomers well; above-average composure in soft chop for weight; freestyle-friendly. Stiffer/more stable than Nomad 100, less playful at low speeds.
- **Salomon QST 106** -- 139.3-106-126 (181); 19 m; 2068 & 2178 g; 64/3/37; 179.9; -9.1 / 80.8; 165-189; poplar + carbon & flax stringers + cork tips/tails + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. Shines in suspension and maneuverability; very easy to release/pivot/slash in any snow; doesn't eagerly initiate carves but stiff middle = good edge hold (better than Optic 104). Apostle 106 quicker; Rustler 10 more on-piste precision. Very balanced: damper than most here, more maneuverable than many. QST Blank for chop.
- **DPS Wailer 107** (no 112 in new Wailer line) -- 139.5-107-125 (184); 15 m (all lengths); 2055 & 2056 g; 88.5/6.5/30; 182.1; -9.6 / 81.4; 155-189; poplar + carbon & fiberglass. Much of iconic Wailer 112's pow performance in more versatile silhouette; dramatic taper/rocker float better than most at width; practical between storms. Quick, surfy, more planted than narrower Wailers in soft chop; knocked around in crud at high speed. Cuts clean arcs on soft slopes, not firm high angles. Great tight-tree pow ski.
- **Dynastar M-Free 108** (updated: new lengths, less environmentally harmful construction) -- 137-107.6-127.4 (192); 20 m; 2322 & 2322 g; 64/9.5/50.5; 189.6; -8.3 / 86.5; 162,170,178,185,192; poplar/PU + fiberglass. Performs very similarly: one of most stable here yet playful and maneuverable. New 185 fills big gap between old 182 and 192 (old version varied a lot by length). Bit stiffer and less forgiving. Preliminary. For high speeds in variable without being demanding in tight terrain or unwieldy in air.
- **Wagner Summit 109** (semi-custom) -- 141.7-107.9-129.1 (186); 20.4 m; 2171 & 2191 g; 70/5/33; 184.2; -7.3 / 84.8; lengths "custom"; beech/aspen + carbon fiber "torsion box" + fiberglass. Excels in soft snow but versatile all-mountain not pow specialist; minimal taper, shallow low rocker = very good initiation/edge hold for width, looser in soft snow; feels lighter than weight (swing weight like Ranger 108, slightly better suspension). Next ski surfier; Hotshot smoother in crud. Hard to fault.
- **Fischer Ranger 108** -- 142.7-108.4-133.7 (185); 18 m; 2110 & 2117 g; 68/2.5/33; 183.4; -9.6 / 82.1; 171-192; beech/poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Ranger 102 blueprint optimized for soft/deep snow; composed in soft chop, tails punish backseat but flex more forgiving/balanced; floats very well, excellent pow ski, predictable on piste; energetic for dynamic skiers but loose enough for slarvers. More well-rounded alternative to Wailer 107; Nomad 106 for centered/playful.

**Blister Spectrums -- All-Mountain More Forgiving** (best -> worst):
- Most Stable in Crud/Variable: M-Free 108, Optic 104, Hotshot, Masterblaster, Ahmeek 105, Summit 109, QST 106, Ranger 108, Nomad 106, Ranger 102, Mindbender 106C, Pandora 106, Slap 104, Apostle 106, Meier Wrangler, Sender Soul 102, Pandora 99, Rustler 10, Camox, Endurance 98, Optic 96, Optic 88, QST 98, Wailer 107, Atris, Nomad 100, Apostle 96 Ti, Completo 100, Rustler 9, Scope 104, Ahmeek 95, Slap 98, Wailer 100, QST 92, Line Blade, Wailer 90, Season Aero, Zorro 100, Mindbender 96C, Mata Ti, Ripstick 96, Line Sakana (least).
- Most Maneuverable: QST 92, Zorro 100, Wailer 90, Scope 104, Apostle 96 Ti, Apostle 106, Sakana, Wailer 100, Optic 96, Ripstick 96, Completo 100, Mata Ti, Optic 88, Endurance 98, QST 98, Mindbender 96C, Nomad 100, Wailer 107, Mindbender 106C, Rustler 10, Nomad 106, Sender Soul 102, Pandora 106, Rustler 9, Slap 98, Ranger 102, QST 106, Slap 104, Pandora 99, Summit 109, Atris, Ahmeek 95, Ranger 108, Masterblaster, Camox, Season Aero, Blade, Wrangler, Optic 104, Hotshot, Ahmeek 105, M-Free 108 (least).
- Best in Deep Snow: Ranger 108, Wailer 107, M-Free 108, Summit 109, Mindbender 106C, Nomad 106, Apostle 106, Scope 104, Slap 104, Pandora 106, Optic 104, QST 106, Hotshot, Ranger 102, Atris, Completo 100, Rustler 10, Ahmeek 105, Nomad 100, QST 98, Wailer 100, Apostle 96 Ti, Sakana, Sender Soul 102, Pandora 99, Optic 96, Endurance 98, Masterblaster, Mindbender 96C, Zorro 100, Slap 98, QST 92, Wailer 90, Ripstick 96, Camox, Optic 88, Rustler 9, Wrangler, Blade, Mata Ti, Ahmeek 95, Season Aero (worst).

### All-Mountain Chargers (p.145)
Class overview: diversified dramatically -- heavy & stiff, light & stiff, subtly or seriously rockered, very demanding, even pretty forgiving. After years of skis getting lighter, some manufacturers acknowledge weight is valuable for certain skis -- most true here. Not for everyone, but favorites of many passionate skiers: if you prioritize high-speed stability over low-speed maneuverability, "this is your section."

- **Blizzard Anomaly 102** (widest/heaviest/most stable of new Anomaly series replacing Bonafide and Cochise) -- 135.5-101.5-122.5 (188); 21 m; 2259 & 2278 g; 62/5/14; 186.6; -10 / 83.3; 176,182,188,192; poplar/beech + full-width & partial titanal + fiberglass. Quite different from Cochise 106 but solid charger; shorter lengths could be More Stable, 188 very substantial. More precise on firm than many chargers; slightly more compliant than Bonafide 97. For technically and physically strong skiers wanting solid on edge from groomers to crud.
- **Moment Commander 102** (latest Commanders quite different) -- 140.5-102-123 (182); 18 m; 2220 & 2224 g; 50.5/7/20; 180.7; -9.9 / 80.5; 176,182,188; poplar/beech + 2 titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Decidedly stiff, precise, game-on; high ceiling but ride not as smooth/plush as similarly heavy skis -- more feedback and rebound. Not for muted glued-to-snow feel. Don't be afraid/proud to size down.
- **Folsom Primary 104 (Blister Pro Edition)** (custom) -- 136.4-103.4-122.5 (186); 21 m; 2325 & 2352 g; 75/4/27; 184.7; -9.6 / 82.7; 163-189; maple/bamboo/poplar + carbon & fiberglass. Stiff, substantial, yet highly maneuverable for aggressive technical-terrain skiing; mutes rough snow very well, much easier to release/throw sideways than others on its page; less precise initiation and edge hold on firm smooth snow. For those who like planted heavy skis but hate locked-in feel in bumps/trees/steeps. Rare suspension + composure + low-speed maneuverability.
- **Volkl Mantra 108** (formerly Katana 108) -- 145.4-107.7-128.6 (184); radii 34/19/39 m; 2353 & 2360 g; 57.5/2.5/21; 183.1; -11.5 / 80; 170-191; poplar/beech + "titanal frame" + carbon tips + fiberglass. Very damp, stable, excels in variable, more precise than similarly wide skis; girthy shovels can feel heavy in tight terrain but releases more than Anomaly 102 / Commander 102; not very demanding for strong skiers, blast on piste, big stiff shovels blast through chop. Mantra 102 better for bumps/trees; Mantra 108 for open-terrain fall-line charging.
- **Prior Husume** ("Quad-Glass" tested; "XTC Carbon" lighter) -- 135.7-109-123.5 (188); 25 m; 2312 & 2386 g; 78/2/22; 187.5; -10.7 / 83; 175-193; maple + fiberglass (custom). Surfy, maneuverable yet capable at speed; very stiff with excellent suspension, less demanding than weight/flex suggest; deep rocker, tapered shovel = loose and pivotable (needs physical input); outstanding float -- great first pow turns then excels in chop. Mantra 108 more engaging on groomers; Husume nearly matches its stability in variable with superior maneuverability.
- **Folsom Giver (The Hammer Edition)** (Giver formerly Primary 110; custom) -- 138.3-109.2-129.3 (188); 25 m; 2371 & 2374 g; 63/0/42; 186.4; -7 / 86.1; 184-195; maple/bamboo/poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Very beefed-up Giver for inconsistent snow at speed; for physically strong skiers; remarkable composure in variable; forward mount + reverse camber = similarly maneuverable/loose as Primary 104 / Husume, skis more centered; stiff shovels hinder deep-snow float. Lighter/softer build = versatile daily driver (as an Alaska reviewer chose).
- **Dynastar M-Pro 108** -- 138.5-109.8-128.7 (192); 22 m; 2448 & 2499 g; 68/6/26; 191.9; -13.3 / 82.7; 172,182,192; beech/PU + partial titanal + fiberglass.
