Evokes classic big-mountain comp skis (old Dynastar Pro Rider): very heavy, exceptional suspension, for skilled powerful skiers; rearward mount, big swing weight; hard to find speed limit; eliminates harsh vibrations. Needs speed/aggression but not punishing. Top pick for directional skiers wanting top suspension/composure in open terrain.
- **Volkl V-Werks Katana** (repeat; 184) -- Light and strong; quickest, most agile charger; excellent big-mountain pow ski, big sweet spot, among most damp carbon skis tested; least stable in section but wild how hard it can be skied for weight, esp. soft-ish; for dynamic style (continues).
  - (Katana cont.) Better yet if you also tour; for firm snow or rock-smacking resort use, Mantra 108.
- **Heritage Lab FL113** (modern take on discontinued classics; small limited runs) -- STATED 133-113-122 (194); 45.3 m; 2738 & 2755 g; stated profile 65/0/10; 194; -11 / 86; 187, 194; bamboo/poplar + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. >2700 g per ski, nearly straight 45 m sidecut: supremely damp and stable in any chop; very slow to initiate carves (FL = "fall line"); surprisingly easy to pivot and slash. Similar stability-to-maneuverability ratio to Husume / Giver but "more ski" -- another league when you don't want to slow down in crud.

**Blister Spectrums -- All-Mountain Chargers** (top -> bottom):
- Least Demanding -> Most Demanding: V-Werks Katana (needs precision, less strength since light), Prior Husume (stiff/heavy but taper and deep rocker make it easier), Folsom Primary 104, Mantra 108, Folsom Giver (Hammer), Commander 102, Anomaly 102, M-Pro 108, Heritage FL113 (physically demanding but easy to pivot).
- Best in Powder -> Worst: Husume, Katana, FL113, M-Pro 108, Mantra 108, Giver, Primary 104, Anomaly 102, Commander 102.
- Easiest in Tight Terrain -> Most Work: Katana, Primary 104 (loose but stiff & heavy), Husume, Giver, Mantra 108, Commander 102, Anomaly 102, FL113 (easy to pivot, hefty swing weight), M-Pro 108.

### All-Mountain Freestyle Skis (p.151)
Class overview: vs other All-Mountain sections, more maneuverable, forgiving, suited to spinning/flipping/switch/playful approach. Generally suit (a) experienced skiers preferring playful maneuverable skis, (b) feature-hunters, (c) those taking a few park laps, (d) intermediates wanting forgiving and easy to turn. Some narrow enough for park, some wide enough for deep days. Most encourage centered upright stance (some can be driven); symmetric consistent flex aids balance. Don't write off because of "freestyle."
(Photo: skier Sander Hadley, Blister Summit 2024.)

- **Black Crows Camox** (repeat; 186.5) -- Hit with freestyle and very directional reviewers; drive shovels or balanced stance; stands out here for precision and energy; very good carver but needs focus in tight bumps/trees (not loose/skiddy); energetic flex, fairly low weight for dynamic light skiers. M-Free 99 surfier/planted; Season Kin soft/easygoing.
- **Fischer Nightstick 97** (Fischer's first freestyle collection in a while) -- 132-96.5-123 (184); 18 m; 1944 & 1952 g; 56.5/8.5/51; 182.3; -3.4 / 87.7; 163-184; poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Well-sorted all-rounder; Ranger-like ride/stability in symmetric playful design; between Camox (precise) and M-Free 99 (surfy); easy to throw sideways with solid edge hold underfoot; mid swing weight and stability; can be skied forward but directional skiers may find others more intuitive. Playful with no significant faults.
- **Dynastar M-Free 99** -- 127.6-97.2-119.4 (185); 18 m; 2024 & 2112 g; 75/5/59; 181.5; -7.6 / 83; 171,179,185; poplar/PU + titanal binding plate + fiberglass. Loose, surfy plus above-average suspension and stability; deep rocker, moderate radius; mutes rough snow better than lighter/softer skis like Revolt 104; intuitive for directional skiers on line, freestyle skiers mount closer to center. Not best firm edge hold nor easiest to butter. M-Free 108 for variable charging.
- **Season Kin** -- 139.5-97.6-135.5 (181); 15 m; 1824 & 1843 g; 61/6/62; 179.2; -2.6 / 87; 163,175,181; karuba + fiberglass. Uncommonly playful for park and piste; soft flex, deep rocker, easy to bend/slash; tight radius + impressive edge hold = blast on groomers, one of most exciting carvers here without much effort; easy in trees/bumps, predictable off piste. Can get sketchy in big fast turns; not damp. Season Nexus more stable/deeper snow.
- **Nordica Unleashed 98** (186 tested) -- 134.3-97.6-123.4 (186); 18.8 m; 1938 & 2003 g; 73/3.5/57.5; 183.6; -7.3 / 84.5; 156-186; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Broad appeal; minimal rocker/taper = excellent carver, far from loosest; edge hold and energy on firm off-piste steeps but easy to slide (vs Enforcer). On line: maneuverable directional all-mountain ski; mounted near -5 cm from center: strong lively freestyle ski. Faction Studio 2 more balanced/surfy; Kin / Reckoner 102 more engaging at slow speed.
- **K2 Reckoner 102** (tweaked 24/25; reviewer favorite) -- 133.5-101-126 (184); 19.4 m; 2040 & 2061 g; 61/7.5/56; 184.1; mounts "Team" -2.1 / 89.9, "Midsole" -4.2 / 87.8; 163-191; aspen + carbon stringers & "spectral braid" + fiberglass. Still ultra-playful and surprisingly versatile; a bit heavier and damper than original; soft flex, easy to butter, forgiving in tight terrain; surprisingly surfy for moderate rocker and fun to carve. Not stiff/supportive (K2 Omen Team in Park) nor quickest. Doesn't fall apart going faster/bigger. Reckoner 110 / Reckoner KF for softer variable snow.
- **Faction Studio 2** (Mana 2 reborn as Studio 2) -- 130.5-101.5-126.5 (183); 20 m; 1917 & 1934 g; 61.5/7/58.5; 183; mounts Newschool -3.7 / 86.7, Progressive -5.2 / 85.2, Classic -6.7 / 83.7; 166-188; poplar + carbon/rubber edge reinforcement + carbon stringers + fiberglass. For freestyle at higher speeds/aggressive style; stiff flex = tons of pop; light, balanced in air; agile, more precise than surfy (but more maneuverable than Camox / Unleashed 98); carves very well, tighter than radius suggests. Not for low-speed jibs/butters (Reckoner 102). Predictable at speed for light stiff ski; directional-friendly on rearward lines.
- **Volkl Revolt 104** -- 131.4-103.6-121.6 (188); 22.5 m; 1973 & 1997 g; 68.5/3.5/43; 186.7; -1.7 / 91.6; 172,180,188; poplar/beech + fiberglass. Like a park ski grown wider; stronger centered-stance preference than most (even mounted back); balanced and quick for freestyle backgrounds; knocked around fast in variable; tail punishing in tight bumps (188). Rare surfiness + support on landings. Next skis for shovel-drivers; Reckoner 102 / J Vacation for slow speed/small features.
- **J Skis Vacation** -- 126.3-103.7-122.3 (186); 20.1 m; 2010 & 2018 g; 70/3/53; 183.3; -3.7 / 87.9; 166,173,180,186; maple/aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Great do-everything freestyle ski; 186 balances all-mountain performance and jibby playfulness; shorter lengths have softer flex (more playful, less support at speed). 186 between soft Reckoner 102 and stiff Deathwish 104; shorter more like Reckoner 102. J Allplay narrower (Park); J Hotshot damper.
- **Moment Deathwish 104** -- 132.7-103.8-124.1 (184); 22 m; 1970 & 1993 g; 67.5/4/66.5; 181.8; -5.8 / 85.1; 164-190; aspen/beech + carbon & fiberglass. One of stiffer here but playful (symmetric, Triple Camber); deep rocker = surfy, excellent firm grip when driven; low swing weight, very poppy; strong, not super damp; Triple Camber learning curve. Stout freestyle ski with firm edge hold and soft-snow surfiness.
- **Whitedot Altum 104** -- 134.3-104.4-124.4 (187); 22 m; 2049 & 2053 g; 67/3.5/55; 185.5; -5.9 / 86.8; 171,179,187; poplar/ash + carbon/kevlar stringers + fiberglass. Easy to recommend to many directional and playful skiers; forward or neutral stance, skis switch, stiff enough to push hard; minimal taper/low rocker = excellent carving for class yet pivots easily. Not as forgiving as Vacation or next ski; high speed limit without heft in soft snow. Like Wildcat 108 -- Altum better carver, a touch less surfy/stable in chop. Cohesive combination.
- **Fischer Nightstick 104** -- 140-104.5-129.5 (188); 20 m; 2090 & 2122 g; 56.5/7.5/54; 186.1; -3.4 / 89.7; 172,180,188; poplar + partial titanal + fiberglass. Widest of new Nightstick series; generalist freestyle ski; stronger and more centered-biased than NS97; enough mass for chop composure but quicker than heaviest here; mid-spectrum carving and surfiness. ARV 106 / Altum 104 for forward stance. Middle of bell curve.
(Photo: Luke Koppa.)
- **Majesty Dirty Bear XL** -- 139.4-104.6-129.5 (186); 21 m; 1960 & 1962 g; 59/1.5/51; 184.5; -3.9 / 88.3; 162-186; poplar/ash + fiberglass. Reviewer-loved, hard to describe: impressive piste edge hold yet surprisingly easy to slash in soft snow; supportive middle but easy to butter/press. Not for loose surfy priority. For those wanting park-ski turn initiation/grip with much better soft-snow handling; very lively off lips, fairly low swing weight -> more playful than previous few. Unconventional.
- **FullSend Max K 106** (made in South Dakota; fully customizable; tested rocker/camber/rocker, "stiff" flex) -- 138.5-105-129.5 (185); 18 m; 2189 & 2236 g; 71.5/4/56.5; 184.7; -5.5 / 86.9; 185 cm; bamboo + carbon stringer + fiberglass (wood-veneer topsheets, ABS/bamboo sidewalls options). Strong, substantial for class; above-average suspension/stability, pretty surfy; not best firm carver; needs speed/effort to load; softer version more playful. Like a heavier Wildcat 108; reminiscent of out-of-production Sego Big Horn 106 (damper but more sluggish/demanding). Beautifully finished custom.
- **Season Nexus** -- 135.4-105.2-131.9 (183); 17.5 m; 2073 & 2074 g; 53/5/48; 181.5; -2.6 / 88.1; 158-189; paulownia + fiberglass. Do-it-all that isn't bland: near-symmetric ~106, blast to carve, surprisingly surfy, damp/supportive enough for fairly high speed. Similar to Line Bacon 108 (centered, engaging carver) but stronger and lower swing weight; more exciting on piste than Wildcat 108; not a soft jib stick; easy-to-bend ends. Directional skiers face stance learning curve.
- **Armada ARV 106** (ARV series updated; constructionally identical to ARW 106) -- 131.5-105.5-124 (188); 22 m; 2015 & 2170 g; 56/7.5/54.5; 186.2; mounts Freestyle -2.5 / 90.6, Factory -5 / 88.1; 164-188; poplar + ash binding insert + fiberglass. All reviewers big fans of updates; intuitive for wider variety of styles while true to freestyle roots; does many things well; safe bet if unsure what you want in a mid-fat playful ski. Old version "jack of all trades, master of none"; new pushes toward "master of most."
- **4FRNT Devastator** -- 135.5-107.2-130.6 (186); 21 m; 1947 & 2011 g; 60/0/55; 183.3; -4.7 / 86.9; 172,179,186; aspen + maple underfoot + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Not a demanding charger despite name: reverse camber + low weight = extremely nimble, surfy; surprisingly predictable on smooth firm snow and fun to carve when not bulletproof; knocked around in firm crud but composed for weight; very freestyle-friendly, super low swing weight. M-Free 108 / DPS Koala 111 heavier/damper. One of best for max maneuverability.
- **Nordica Unleashed 108** (186 tested) -- 141.5-107.5-130.5 (186); 19.7 m; 2077 & 2097 g; 77/3/59.5; 183.6; -7.7 / 84.1; 162-191; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. All-mountain capability with freestyle flair; carves impressively, better edge hold/precision on firm than most similar widths; looser in pow, wide tips plane quickly; middle ground weight/flex; directional or centered (mount a few cm forward for balance). For those between More Forgiving directional skis and freestyle-specific skis.
- **Dynastar M-Free 108** (repeat) -- Biggest change is lengths (smaller gaps, more consistent; old 182 and 192 felt very different). Initial take: a bit more precise and stable in challenging snow at cost of some forgiveness/maneuverability (notable old 182 vs new 185). Like old 192: one of most damp/stable in class yet maneuverable and freestyle-friendly. More A/B testing planned.
- **Line Bacon 108** -- 141.4-107.6-137 (184); 17.4 m; 2194 & 2208 g; 61/6/55; 184; -4 / 88; 166-190; maple/aspen + fiberglass. Mid-fat freestyle with twists: easy to bend, symmetric; fun on piste plus float/maneuverability in soft snow; swing weight lower than weight suggests; but weight detracts from playfulness and soft flex limits stability -- neither ultra-nimble nor very stable. For butters + on-piste + suspension over low swing weight. Line Chronic 101 similar, more agile, more practical on firm.
- **Moment Wildcat 108** -- 134.0-107.7-126.9 (184); 22 m; stated 2050 g; 74/5/71; 181.8; -6.1 / 84.8; 164-190; aspen/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Top playful mid-fat: balance of quickness, maneuverability, high-speed composure; drive shovels or centered; light, deep rocker = nimble/surfy; strong flex encourages aggression. Not smoothest/most planted; needs speed/aggression to load (esp. on piste). Wildcat (wider) for deep chop; Countach 110 damper/directional.
- **Atomic Bent 110** (repeat) -- One of most comprehensively playful ~110s in the guide: light, surfy, balanced, spins/butters/slashes; stiff middle + super low swing weight great for tricks in soft snow; one of least stable here in crud/dense chop -- reserve for soft/consistent conditions; amazing 50/50 or BC freestyle ski. Next ski for variable; Bent Chetler 120 for deep.
- **K2 Reckoner 110** (new; replaces Reckoner 112) -- 137.5-109.5-134.5 (184); 22 m; 2070 & 2103 g; 75.5/5.5/67.5; 183.7; -5.5 / 86.4; 170-191; aspen + carbon stringers + "spectral braid" + fiberglass. More well-rounded than 112: more versatile across conditions and styles, more predictable in variable, still one of more playful ~110s; deep splayed rocker, accessible tips/tails; respectable high-speed composure for slow-speed engagement; carves way better than it should. Sender Free 110 for very stable. No obvious weaknesses.
- **Prior Northwest 110** ("quad-glass" tested; lighter version available; custom) -- 138.5-110.0-131.2 (190); 23 m; 2188 & 2190 g; 64/4.5/59; 189.2; -3.1 / 91.5; 178,184,190; maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass. One of most stable here and customizable; extremely fun in soft variable snow -- high speed limit, great suspension, boosting off everything; not quickest or most fun at slow speed (strong flex, heavy swing weight). Damper alternative to Wildcat 108 / Professor 111, more centered-stance preference. Northwest 116 for more float.
- **Rossignol Sender Free 110** -- 140.3-110.4-132.6 (184); 20 m; 2196 & 2211 g; 69/6/50; 181.5; -3.4 / 87.4; 176,184,191; poplar + partial titanal + rubber inserts + "Air Tip" insert + carbon & fiberglass. Freestyle platform with big-mountain build -- for modern freeride (high speeds, variable, tricks off big features); one of best here for suspension and high-speed stability; centered mount, symmetric; holds edge well on firm, looser in soft. Stout and heavy; not for low-speed playfulness. Sender Free 118 surfier/more planted.
- **RMU Professor 111** (new) -- 138-110.5-131 (188); 19.8 m; 1948 & 1957 g; 70/3/38.5; 186.3; -4.8 / 88.3; 168,178,188; poplar + beech mounting plate + fiberglass. Brings Professor 121's surfy ride to versatile width (slushy park laps to pow); low weight, mid flex; more agile and practical in shallow snow; knocked around more in chop than heavier skis but much lower swing weight, more playful; more support than Bent 110 / Bacon 108. RMU Apostle 114 more directional.
- **DPS Koala 111** -- 140-111.1-127.6 (184); 18 m (all lengths); 2180 & 2195 g; (continues). Unlike most DPS skis, neither very directional nor light; freestyle-friendly with substantial build for a wide range of conditions; more composed in soft chop than most lighter skis here...
  - (Koala 111 cont.) 68/4/60; 181.8; -6.9 / 84; 168,176,184,189; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. ...than Professor 111 / Bent 110, but easier to bend and livelier at moderate speeds than stout Sender Free 110 / Northwest 110. Forward or centered; most at home in soft snow but carves well and has nice suspension. For those caught between directional and freestyle skis wanting a maneuverable, pretty stable soft-snow setup. Moment Deathwish for lower swing weight.
- **Faction Studio 3** (formerly Mana 3) -- 139.4-111.5-133.4 (184); 21 m; 2052 & 2081 g; 66/5/59; 182.1; mounts Newschool -2.5 / 88.5, Progressive -4 / 87, Classic -5.5 / 85.5; 165-190; poplar + carbon/rubber edge reinforcement + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Unconventional but cohesive: light, stiff, symmetric, minimally tapered -> for playful-yet-aggressive skiers; not engaging at slow speed nor damp in firm rough snow; can be pushed very hard in soft conditions even directionally; balanced, skis switch, energetic when bent; carves very well for width; less easy to slash than peers. Studio 2 for shallower/firmer.
- **Moment Deathwish** (mainstay for over a decade) -- 137.6-111.9-129.3 (184); 25 m; stated 2000 g; 72/4/69; 182.2; -5.1 / 86; 174-190; aspen/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Triple Camber = deep-rocker maneuverability + edge hold from multiple camber pockets; strong; driven through shovels blasts soft chop and carves firm better than weight/width suggest; centered = balanced, agile. Not particularly surfy, forgiving, or easy to bend for width, but one of more versatile ~112s. Deathwish 104 quicker/more precise for firmer.
- **K2 Reckoner KF** (Karl Fostvedt's new pro model) -- 137-113.5-133 (184); 23 m; 2176 & 2185 g; 68/3/64; 184.1; -4.2 / 87.9; 172-191; aspen + "spectral braid" + flax + carbon & fiberglass. One of most unusual designs in the guide; ultra-soft flex, radically rockered ends, extremely energetic core -- easy to bend yet snaps back with serious rebound at any speed. Once used to it (takes time), more versatile/capable at speed than nearly any similarly soft/surfy ski. Not for driving through chop. Remarkably playful yet practical. Reckoner 110 more conventional.

**Blister Spectrums -- All-Mountain Freestyle** (best -> worst):
- Most Stable in Crud: Sender Free 110 (191), M-Free 108 (192), Northwest 110, M-Free 108 (185), Wildcat 108 (190), Koala 111, Max K 106, Sender Free 110 (184), ARV 106, Altum 104, Studio 3, Wildcat 108 (184), Reckoner 110, Unleashed 108, Deathwish 104, Deathwish, Studio 2, Nexus, Professor 111, Reckoner KF, Nightstick 104, Bacon 108, Camox, M-Free 99, Nightstick 97, Unleashed 98, Dirty Bear XL, Devastator, J Vacation, Revolt 104, Reckoner 102, Bent 110, Season Kin (least).
- Most Park-Friendly: Reckoner 102, Revolt 104, Nightstick 97, Studio 2, Season Kin, J Vacation, Dirty Bear XL, Camox, Unleashed 98, Nexus, Nightstick 104, M-Free 99, ARV 106, Reckoner KF, Reckoner 110, Deathwish 104, Wildcat 108 (184), Professor 111, Devastator, Bacon 108, Bent 110, Altum 104, Max K 106, Unleashed 108, Studio 3, Deathwish, M-Free 108 (185), Sender Free 110 (184), Wildcat 108 (190), Northwest 110, Koala 111, Sender Free 110 (191), M-Free 108 (192) (least).
- Most Surfy/Loose: Devastator, Bent 110, Reckoner KF, Reckoner 110, Koala 111, Professor 111, Bacon 108, ARV 106, Max K 106, Wildcat 108 (184), Wildcat 108 (190), M-Free 108 (185), Studio 3, Deathwish, J Vacation, Deathwish 104, M-Free 99, Reckoner 102, Nightstick 104, Nexus, Altum 104, Northwest 110, M-Free 108 (192), Sender Free 110 (184), Dirty Bear XL, Nightstick 97, Kin, Studio 2, Revolt 104, Unleashed 108, Sender Free 110 (191), Unleashed 98, Camox (least).

### Park Skis (p.165)
Class overview: park skis vary a lot; traditional ultra-stiff stable comp skis are "a bit of an endangered species," while slow-speed/small-feature/creative skiers have more options than ever; more skis cover both ends; many park skis now perform well across the whole mountain.

- **Line Tom Wallisch Pro** -- 117-89.3-116.2 (178); 19 m; 1745 & 1773 g; 48/5.5/49; 177.6; -1.9 / 86.9; 157-178; maple/aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Standout for versatility: stable on sizable jumps, nimble, soft enough to butter/press; little taper/rocker = reliable firm traction but not hooky pipe ski; lightweight. Middle ground between burlier Omen 90 (big features) and softer BDOG (small). Line Chronic for softer/surfier.
- **K2 Omen 90** (new; nominally replaces K2 Sight but entirely different) -- 119-89.5-112.5 (179); 18 m; 1956 & 1978 g; 40.5/6/41; 180; -2 / 87.3; 149-185; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Traditional end: mostly cambered, strong flex, substantial core -> more precise/supportive than most here; great on big firm features; less maneuverable and more demanding at slow speed vs Joyride / TW Pro. For those finding modern park skis lacking composure going bigger/faster. Omen Team (96) more versatile.
- **J Skis Joyride** -- 117-89.5-113 (182); 21 m; 1825 & 1858 g; 59/4/47; 178.9; -4 / 85.5; 140-182; maple/aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Exceptional playfulness in narrow package; soft flex, low swing weight, easy to whip around; intuitive and forgiving; not much inherent stability (balanced stance on big features); fun in bumps/trees; good carver, tighter than radius suggests. Stands out vs stiffer comp-oriented ~90 park skis.
- **Armada BDOG** -- 120.7-93.7-116.5 (180); 15 m; 1970 & 1990 g; 58/5/56; 178.2; -0 / 89.1 (true center); 164,172,180; poplar/ash + fiberglass. Poster child for playfulness: very soft tips/tails transitioning smoothly to stiffer middle without hinge points; tapered ends reduce catching on rails/botched landings/butter starts; tight radius for quick carves. Much less stable on big jumps/very firm snow than stiffer skis (e.g., Edollo). Top pick for butters/presses/smears.
- **Head Oblivion 94** -- 128.1-93.9-117.5 (184); 22.8 m; 1846 & 1857 g; 64/7/44; 181.7; mounts SC -0 / 90.5, All-Mountain -5 / 85.5; 163-184; poplar + elastomer reinforcements + fiberglass. Somewhat old-school: minimal taper/rocker = very solid firm edge hold yet easy to release; low swing weight with stout flex hampers damping in crud but precise/supportive on big landings. Joyride better for butters; Omen 90 more planted. For support/precision over accessibility; capable all-mountain freestyle option.
- **K2 Omen Team** (replaces longstanding K2 Poacher; widest Omen) -- 125-95.5-118.5 (184); 20 m; 2100 & 2115 g; 50/8.5/47; 185; -2 / 89.7; 163-188; aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Not drastically changed from Poacher: notably damp, stiff park ski, surprisingly capable on natural features; less precise/park-specific than Omen 90 but still stiff; rewards aggressive skiing; enough rocker/taper/flex at ends for butters/slashes. J Allplay nimbler at low speed.
- **J Skis Allplay** -- 123.5-96.5-120.5 (186); 20.5 m; 2009 & 2015 g; 57/5.5/49.5; 185.5; -3.5 / 88.1; 158-186; maple/aspen + carbon stringers + fiberglass. One of best for playfulness and ease; soft, ideal for butters/presses/jibs; intuitive for many; lacks support on bigger features (land centered); respectable overall stability. Joyride for hardpack; Allplay handles soft and firm, fine in slush and boot-top pow.
- **Armada Edollo** -- 132.3-97.5-122.2 (180); 20.5 m; 2080 & 2102 g; 62/5/60; 177.4; -2.3 / 86.4; 164,172,180; poplar/ash + fiberglass. Easy recommendation: handles seriously big jumps yet playful at slow speed/small features via contrasting ends (soft rockered tips for nose butters, stiffer cambered tails for support -- better on big jumps than Reckoner 102 / Allplay). Not old comp-ski stability but more playful, versatile 1-ski park quiver. TW Pro lower swing weight.
- **Nordica Unleashed 98** (repeat) -- Viable wider park ski on precise/supportive side; strong middle, little rocker/taper = one of more stable here; mount a few cm forward for dependable all-round park ski between Omen Team and Allplay; can feel hooky/grabby in inconsistent snow before detuning; not easiest to bend; rarely out of place.
- **4FRNT Switch** -- 126.2-98.5-122.2 (184); 19 m; 1707 & 1752 g; 49/4.5/37.5; 182; -3.5 / 87.5; 163-184; aspen/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Particularly agile, lively; significantly lower swing weight than other ~100s -> effortless last-minute spins; knocked around in rough in-runs/runouts; moderate rocker/taper = reliable firm grip, fun carver, not hooky on botched rotations; mid flex. Not for very stable or very surfy; for those who dislike sluggish wide skis.
- **Prior Northwest 100** ("traditional camber" version tested; custom; "Hybrid Rocker" and softer flex options) -- 128.1-100.2-123.3 (184); 21 m; 1910 & 1919 g; 54/4.5/45; 182.8; -2.9 / 88.5; 172-190; maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Combines traditional narrow and modern wide park ski traits: impressive edge hold, excellent pop off lips, solid on large landings; costs maneuverability/easy butters; low swing weight despite width. Hybrid Rocker version more like Reckoner 102.
- **Line Chronic 101** -- 129.1-100.4-124.2 (186); stated 16.6 m (179); 2126 & 2129 g (186); 59/4/50; 186; -4.5 / 89; 165-186; aspen + fiberglass. Ideal for butters/presses/slashes yet damp and substantial; energetic flex and balance disguise mass; stands out for out-of-park capability (excellent maneuverability, good composure in rough snow). Reckoner 102 more playful at slow speed; Studio 2 more stable at speed. Bacon 108 for softer snow.
- **K2 Reckoner 102** (repeat) -- Extremely playful, cohesive; consistently soft flex -> easy butters, forgiving off piste; capable on moderately large jumps with centered landings; deep low rocker = easy sideways, carves well on non-icy snow. Revised version slightly heavier, less nimble, better suspension and burlier build (optimistic about durability).
- **Faction Studio 2** (repeat) -- Standout wider park ski for strong/lively preference; aggressive higher-speed trick skiing; strong flex = pop/amplitude, effort at slow speed; narrower skis better firm precision and lower swing weight, Studio 2 more float and surfier in slush/variable.
- **Head Oblivion 102** -- 135.3-102.2-125.3 (189); 25.1 m; 1806 & 1884 g; 65/5/57; 186.7; mounts SC -0 / 93, All-Mountain -5 / 88; 173,181,189; poplar + recycled PET stringers + elastomer layers + carbon & fiberglass. Different take: light, fairly strong flex, minimal rocker, long radius -> very low swing weight but precise/stable for weight carving and on big landings; more locked-in on firm, less easy to pivot than most ~100s; solid edge hold, high-speed composure vs light peers; rewards aggressive dynamic approach like a lighter, more precise Studio 2. Studio 2 easier to slash.

**Blister Spectrums -- Park** (best -> worst):
- Most Playful Overall: BDOG, Joyride, Reckoner 102, Allplay, Edollo, Chronic 101, Switch, TW Pro, Studio 2, Oblivion 102, Northwest 100, Omen Team, Unleashed 98, Omen 90, Oblivion 94 (least).
- Most Stable on Jumps: Omen 90, Omen Team, Edollo, TW Pro, Northwest 100, Oblivion 94, Oblivion 102, Studio 2, Switch, Unleashed 98, Reckoner 102, Chronic 101, Allplay, Joyride, BDOG (least).
- Most Versatile (big jumps + small jibs) -> Most Specialized: Edollo, TW Pro, Switch, Northwest 100, Omen Team, Allplay, Reckoner 102, Oblivion 102, Chronic 101, Studio 2, Unleashed 98, Joyride, Oblivion 94, Omen 90, BDOG.

### Powder Skis -- More Directional (p.173)
Class overview: playful vs directional pow lines increasingly blurred; not simply any fat ski with traditional mount (~-9 cm or further back). Skis here aren't necessarily heavier, more stable, or straighter than More Playful, but suit those with traditional forward stance who aren't spinning/skiing switch. Blister indicates how playful, stable, versatile each is.
(Photo: Paul Forward, Chugach Powder Guides AK.)

- **Peak 110 by Bode** (widest Peak) -- 140-109.5-129.8 (188); 25.3 m; 2072 & 2092 g; 65/6/23; 186.7; -9 / 84.7; 168,178,188; poplar/paulownia/PU + 2 partial titanal + fiberglass. One of least specialized here; blurs wide all-mountain and pow; very intuitive for directional skiers, carves clean on firm; not loose/surfy; easy in deep snow with momentum; knocked around more in crud than heavier skis but low swing weight lets you push it in low-density chop. Quick, precise, versatile.
- **Parlor McFellon Pro** (custom available) -- 149.1-112.6-140.5 (185); 19.8 m; 2181 & 2190 g; 58/0/36; 184; -9 / 83; 154-192; maple + fiberglass. Could be in either pow section: reverse camber, minimal taper, moderate flex; good for width in shallow/firm, effortless pivoting, impressive on piste; nice suspension, not punishing; beats looser/lighter skis for chop stability. Damper/more maneuverable than Peak 110, easier-going than Revolt 114; playful skiers can mount closer to center and add rocker.
- **Volkl Revolt 114** -- 146-113.8-128 (184); radii 25/19/22 m; 2288 & 2291 g; 72/4/35; 182; -8 / 83; 177,184,191; poplar/beech + fiberglass. "Black sheep" of freestyle Revolt line: strong, heavy, very stable; unlike old Confession has deep rocker and middle mount -> surprisingly easy to release/pivot/slash, can ski somewhat centered (avoid backseat). One of most stable in class, floats extremely well, maneuverable moving down fall line. Loves chop as much as pow.
(Ad: Powder Hound, Girdwood AK -- 9th year Recommended Shop; ski-in/out at Alyeska; 4 MasterFit bootfitters; 130+ brands; 50+ demo models; new Reichmann Slopemaster SK tuning; Blister+ deals.)
- **Blizzard Rustler 11** -- 142.3-114-132.6 (186); 20.5 m; 2082 & 2089 g; 81/3/49; 183.7; -7.6 / 84.2; 168-192; poplar/paulownia/beech + 1 partial titanal + fiberglass. Practical mix of stability, maneuverability, precision, liveliness; current version quicker, more directional, better edge hold, more energetic than earlier more playful versions; fairly easy, handles chop well for light-ish weight; impressive carver for width, nimble in bumps/trees. Heavier skis for chop; most others float better at moderate speed. McFellon Pro for similar versatility + float.
- **Volkl Blaze 114** (repeat) -- Big shovels and set-back mount suggest charger but one of easiest, most intuitive pow skis; shovels don't submerge even very deep; centered or forward; ... (continues).
  - (Blaze 114 cont.) Low swing weight helps at slow speeds; carves clean on groomers, OK on firmer snow. Main drawback: doesn't blow through resort chop on its own. Hard to beat for float + maneuverability under 120 mm.
- **Atomic Maverick 115 CTI** (widest of overhauled directional Maverick line) -- 140-114.5-129 (185); 18 m; 2117 & 2132 g; 61.5/6.5/29.5; 184; -9.5 / 82.5; 177,185,193; ash/poplar + titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Easygoing, forgiving like Bent cousins; lots of taper -> one of loosest here, rivals More Playful skis; excellent in tight technical terrain; soft shovels + directional mount = plenty of float even for shovel drivers. Neither most stable nor quickest but nice suspension/maneuverability mix. Mount 1-2 cm forward for centered stance.
- **Moment Wildcat** (unchanged except graphics; a reference pow ski) -- 143.1-116.4-133.6 (184); 25 m; 2163 & 2166 g; 67.5/7/66.5; 181.9; -5.6 / 85.3; 164-196; aspen/ash + carbon & fiberglass. Original "playful charger": enough mass to bust chop/crud yet easy to throw sideways, stance-versatile (deep rocker, versatile mount). Not lowest swing weight or most forgiving; loved by ex-park-rats to ex-racers for rough-snow composure + playfulness. Revolt 114 damper, more drivable; 4FRNT Renegade looser, more buoyant.
- **Head Kore 117** -- 144.7-116.8-128.4 (184); 24.6 m; 1920 & 1923 g; 73.5/4.5/25; 182.7; -6.8 / 84.5; 177,184,191; karuba/poplar + graphene + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Powerful but not sluggish; push remarkably hard for weight; for directional skiers wanting nimble + supportive; lowest swing weight in section; great for human-powered pow. Centered skiers like mount; directional consider 2-3 cm back. Not surfy/forgiving, poor firm-snow suspension. Rare quickness + stability in soft snow. Oblivion 116 for tricks.
- **Folsom Rapture** (one of Paul Forward's all-time favorites from AK heli guiding/touring; custom) -- 149.4-120.9-138.4 (192); 28 m; 2288 & 2301 g; 74/0/34.5; 190.5; -7.7 / 87.5; 182,186,192,196; poplar/bamboo + fiberglass & carbon. "Shallow Reverse Camber": rare combo of excellent stability in all conditions, very good float except deepest/lightest snow, easy rolling into drifted turns. Composure in high-speed runouts across avalanche debris as good or better than any similar-width ski. One of best big-mountain pow skis; narrower skis more versatile in resorts.
- **4FRNT Renegade** -- 136.7-121.2-130.4 (191); 30 m; 2173 & 2204 g; 89/0/35.5; 188.9; -5.3 / 89.1; 177,184,191; aspen/maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Rare: as comfortable slashing tight trees as making big open-face turns; long radius + reverse camber = calm, stable drawn-out carves, but easy to feather/release/throw sideways. Deflected more in chop than Wildcat / Chipotle Banana; massively rockered tips plane in any snow; predictable in breakable crust. Traits trickle down to narrower Hoji.
- **Moment Chipotle Banana** (limited runs due to passionate fans) -- 140-122-134 (193); 35.5 m; 2323 & 2352 g; 71/0/71; 190; -5.8 / 89.2; 178,186,193; aspen/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Unmatched: girthy waist and full rocker suggest pow tool, but weight, straight shape, stout flex suit high-speed all-conditions charging. Not impressive float for width, but plows rough inbounds conditions better than any >120 mm ski reviewed, still easy to break into quick skids. Specialized; composure + surprising maneuverability.
- **DPS Lotus 124** -- 146-124.6-134 (191); 23 m (all lengths); stated 2110 g; 76/6/35; 189; -11 / 84; 178,185,191; aspen/paulownia/ash + carbon. Pow-specific, optimized for easy maneuverability and float; not stable/supportive in big turns in deep/variable snow; very easy to slash/pivot/carve at moderate speeds; like extension of Kaizen 112 / old Wailer 112 (low swing weight, loose, slarvy) with longer radius for extended drifts and slight stability gain. Folsom Rapture for high-speed big terrain (esp. if you liked original Alchemist Lotus 124). DPS Koala 111 for stability/freestyle.
- **Faction La Machine 5** -- 147.3-125.5-138.7 (186); 26 m; 1870 & 1895 g; 64/0/31; 184.2; -13.1 / 79; 178, 186; paulownia/poplar + titanal binding reinforcement + carbon & fiberglass. Very wide, quite light, pure pow; surprisingly stable in all BC conditions incl. crusts; OK in resort unless pushed fast in chop; shines from blower to maritime goo; ample float except bottomless; easily drifted. Heritage 132 surfier; Rapture more versatile.
- **Heritage Lab Ultra Taper 132** (formerly BC132; limited runs) -- STATED 137-132-135 (200); 72 m; 2028 & 2112 g; stated 75/0/45; 201; -9.2 / 91.5; 190, 200; paulownia/poplar + carbon. Rebirth/evolution of reverse-camber, reverse-sidecut ("reverse-reverse") designs like Volant Spatula and DPS Lotus 138; narrower-feeling and more tapered than most Lotus 138s; damp yet reactive for low weight. Once used to it, unmatched fresh-snow maneuverability. Esp. for human-powered pow.
- **K2 Crescendo** (continues Pontoon legacy) -- STATED 155-132-144 (186); 22 m; 2177 & 2200 g; rocker/camber/rocker; 185.5; -12.2 / 80.5; 159-186; aspen/paulownia + carbon spectral braid + fiberglass. 200 lb AK reviewer with guide pack wished for longer length; pretty stable pow ski with excellent tip float; not as loose in drifted turns as similarly wide skis (esp. steep), but easy initiation and nearly limitless buoyancy for all abilities, esp. moderate terrain. More intuitive on firm than Pontoon; most at home in deep untracked.
- **Folsom Rotor** (aka "Dad Bod" / "Fat Forward"; custom) -- 157.5-131-147.5 (193); 25 m; 2343 & 2376 g; 73/0/58.5; 190.3; -7.2 / 88; 184-195. poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass. Born on AK heli trip (Paul Forward + Folsom CEO Mike McCabe); based on Rapture, +~10 mm width, modified shape/rocker for heli/cat conditions; dramatically more float and slightly smaller radius. 2024 heli season consensus: one of best do-everything heli skis for Alaskan terrain. Order slightly softer flex than Rapture, tail even softer than tip.
- **Folsom G-Wagon** (custom) -- 159.6-133-138.7 (192); 23 m (all lengths); 2566 & 2580 g; 87/0/64; 189.8; -7.2 / 87.7; 188,192,196; poplar/bamboo + carbon & fiberglass. For bottomless days: 160 mm shovel, 133 waist = huge float; subtle reverse camber for accessible drifts. Narrower scope than others; Rapture makes more sense for most; Rotor better in bigger steeper deep terrain; G-Wagon excels in moderate-angle terrain (giant shovels, skinnier tail plane quickly).
(Photo: Paul Forward, Chugach Powder Guides.)

**Blister Spectrums -- Powder More Directional** (best -> worst):
- Most Flotation: G-Wagon, Rotor, Crescendo, Rapture, La Machine 5, Renegade (191), Heritage 132, Lotus 124, Chipotle Banana, Wildcat (190), Blaze 114, Renegade (184), McFellon Pro, Revolt 114, Maverick 115 CTI, Kore 117, Wildcat (184), Rustler 11, Peak 110 (least).
- Best in Deep Chop: Chipotle Banana, Wildcat (190), Rapture, Revolt 114, Rotor, Wildcat (184), McFellon Pro, Maverick 115 CTI, Renegade (191), Rustler 11, Kore 117, Peak 110, Renegade (184), Blaze 114, Lotus 124, G-Wagon, Crescendo, Heritage 132, La Machine 5 (worst).
- Best in Refrozen Crap: Revolt 114, Wildcat (190), Chipotle Banana, Wildcat (184), McFellon Pro, Rapture, Peak 110, Rustler 11, Renegade (191), Renegade (184), Maverick 115 CTI, Blaze 114, Rotor, Kore 117, Lotus 124, Crescendo, G-Wagon, La Machine 5, Heritage 132 (worst).

### Powder Skis -- More Playful (p.184)
Class overview: more interesting and harder to describe each year; less about driving shovels and more about surfing, smearing, spinning, tricking with upright centered stance; not just spin/flip skis -- includes some of guide's most playful and some of most stable; many directional reviewers like them.
(Photo: skier Trevor Kennison, Blister Summit 2024.)

- **Armada ARV 112** (new; replaces ARV 116 JJ) -- 134.5-111.5-129.5 (185); 21 m; 1998 & 2026 g; 55/7/54; 183.4; mounts Freestyle -2.8 / 88.9, Factory -5.2 / 86.5; 165,175,185,192; poplar + ash binding insert + fiberglass. Resembles predecessor but notably more agile; playful end vs stable; more well-rounded than ARV 116 JJ UL or ARG II UL; for freestyle skiers prioritizing quickness/pivotability, skiable aggressively in soft snow centered. QST Blank damper; ARG II UL surfiest. Safe one-pow-ski bet for playful skiers.
- **Icelantic Nomad 112** (new widest of overhauled line; replaces Nomad 115) -- STATED 147-112-139 (188); 20 m; 2026 & 2118 g; 67.5/4/58; 185.2; mounts Progressive Center -5.2 / 87.4, Boot Center -7.2 / 85.4; 171-188; poplar + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Very well-rounded, easy to recommend; moderate flex, deep rocker, minimal taper = accessible, maneuverable, stable in cut-up chop; notably quicker/livelier than Nomad 115 without losing composure (rare). Stance-tolerant; big rockered shovels float very well. Well-executed playful generalist.
- **Salomon QST Blank** -- 137.4-112.1-126.5 (186); 17 m; 2243 & 2287 g; 70/4/35; 184.7; -7.9 / 84.4; 178,186,194; poplar + flax & carbon stringers + cork tips/tails + titanal binding reinforcement + fiberglass. One of more stable/versatile here; not light -> good suspension; deep rocker and tight radius = quick changes of direction; floats really well, fine on firm; favors slightly forward stance but can be centered (mount forward). Not for low swing weight or easy bending (tail stiff when backseat). Surfy in pow, composed elsewhere.
- **Whitedot Altum 114** -- 142.5-113-131.3 (187); 24 m; 2122 & 2151 g; 63/3/58.5; 185.5; -5.9 / 86.8; 179, 187; poplar/ash + carbon/kevlar stringers + fiberglass. Highly adaptable soft-snow tool; fairly light, moderate taper, strong flex = very good stability in forgiving conditions; deep but low-slung rocker = easy sideways yet secure edge engagement. Bacon 115 / Bent Chetler 120 surfier; Sender Free 118 / Optic 114 damper chargers. For middle-ground directional and freestyle skiers; Altum 104 for daily driver.
- **Line Optic 114** (dropped "Blade") -- 138.3-113.4-130.9 (186); 24 m; 2318 & 2322 g; 65.5/1.5/47.5; 185.2; -7.8 / 85.2; 178,186,192; aspen + partial titanal + fiberglass. Blasts through pow, chop, crud; one of best here for high-speed chop composure; easy sideways, natural in air, skis switch; far more stable than Line's other pow skis. Not very forgiving esp. on firm; drive hard in variable, loose in fresh. Bacon 115 more balanced/freestyle and engaging slow.
- **Meier Leeper** (Owen Leeper signature) -- 146-113.5-138 (185); 19 m; 2216 & 2246 g; 65/4/38; 183.7; -4.5 / 87.5; 178,185,192; poplar/maple/beetle-kill pine + carbon & fiberglass. Surprisingly approachable despite Leeper's straightline reputation; hefty, strong, good suspension, not sketchy at speed but fun slower; moderately centered mount + deep rocker = easy in tight terrain, happy with big turns. Sender Free 118 / Optic 114 better high-speed; Leeper lower swing weight, more practical on firm; directional reviewers liked it. Most like QST Blank (Blank floats better; Leeper more forgiving/agile/balanced).
- **Nordica Unleashed 114** -- 147-113.6-135.7 (186); 20.4 m; 2086 & 2088 g; 78/6/57; 183.7; -8 / 83.5; 174-191; poplar/beech + partial titanal + carbon & fiberglass. Hard to classify, broad potential; fairly light; mount/flex for many (mount closer to center for freestyle); very impressive float for 114; looser in soft, impressive carver on firm. Not super loose/soft/playful nor very damp in chop; good composure for weight in soft/consistent snow; easy to flick. Altum 114 more stable/balanced, less float.
- **RMU Apostle 114** (rebranded North Shore 114; flex tweaked for smoother transitions) -- 142.5-114-131.5 (184); 22 m; 2086 & 2125 g; 66.5/4.5/35; 182.5; mounts FR -4.7 / 86.5, TR -6.7 / 84.5; 176, 184; poplar + beech mounting plate + fiberglass. Very similar to North Shore but a bit more stable, pushable in chop; nimble, surfy, playful, energetic; not damp; pop and slash through soft chop rather than plow; centered or directional. Professor 121 more balanced/freestyle/surfy.
- **Line Bacon 115** -- 147.6-114.2-143.2 (188); 18.4 m; 2260 & 2290 g; 61/4/54; 188.2; -4.1 / 90; 168,178,188; maple/aspen + fiberglass. Like Line's early pow skis with own personality: minimal taper, tighter radius, centered mount, soft flex = playful, freestyle-focused; hefty -> better suspension in crud than ARV 112 / Bent Chetler 120. Not a charger. Rare combo of balanced forgiving freestyle design + smooth damp ride. Optic 114 for stability.
- **Black Crows Anima** (longstanding big-mountain ski) -- 146.2-114.8-135.5 (189); 19 m; 2259 & 2279 g; 64/6/55; 187.4; -6.3 / 87.4; 176.6-194.4; poplar + carbon & kevlar stringers + fiberglass. Middle of bell curve: fairly heavy, composed in chop, easy to get on edge, not very punishing (more forgiving than QST Blank); balanced in air, lands switch in all but deepest snow; moderate rocker = carves/handles firm well, looser in deep. One of more well-rounded here. Black Crows Nocta for deep-snow specialist.
- **Head Oblivion 116** -- 141.5-116-131.5 (189); 30.8 m; 1998 & 2024 g; 66/7/55; 187.2; mounts SC -0 / 93.6, All-Mountain -5 / 88.6; 181, 189; poplar + recycled PET stringers + elastomer + carbon & fiberglass. Unconventional: very stiff, light, long radius -- uniquely fun; easy to slash in fresh, calm and predictable in big fast turns despite low swing weight; poor suspension -> soft-snow-specific tool. Mount options suit varied stances; excellent for slashing and airing feature-filled pow lines. Northwest 116 / CBC for crud.
- **Salomon QST X** (repeat; specs as in 50/50) -- Loosest, most playful, most pow-oriented QST; vs QST Blank considerably lighter, surfier, more centered; extremely maneuverable in soft snow; not composed in dense chop; one of best here for human-powered pow; neutral stance in deep, can drive shovels in shallower; not very freestyle-focused (fairly stiff, no truly turned-up tail).
(Photo: Luke Koppa.)
- **4FRNT Inthayne** (Thayne Rich pro model) -- 137.3-116.3-128.6 (188); 25 m (all lengths); 2043 & 2046 g; 79.5/0/75; 185.4; -5.8 / 86.9; 181, 188; aspen + maple underfoot + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Fairly straight shape, strong flex, low weight, progressive mount; reverse camber but far from loosest (surfier than Oblivion 116) -> drive hard on shallower/firmer snow. Heavier skis better suspension; impressively stable for weight; light enough to tour; strong for landings; catch-free ride.
- **Moment Wildcat** (repeat in playful section) -- Still all-time favorite of several reviewers; stiffer/heavier than many here -> one of best for mobbing soft chop; versatile after storms; works for very directional and very playful; returns energy when flexed (needs effort); not very playful skied conservatively slow, comes alive pushed hard without being sluggish/demanding... (continues).
  - (Wildcat cont.) Impressive blend of contradictory traits while cohesive. Sender Free 118 for all-out suspension/stability (heavier, less agile).
- **Prior CBC** ("Quad-Glass" tested; lighter carbon version exists; custom) -- 139.6-116.5-137.5 (184); 16.5 m; 2147 & 2286 g; 64/3/61; 182.3; -2.1 / 89; 178-193; maple + fiberglass. Narrower focus, very good at it: very balanced freestyle ski with stout flex and damp construction for aggressive style; centered stance, at home in air; more composed in variable than lighter skis; extremely confidence-inspiring landing platform; quicker and surfier than expected (taper/rocker) but less chop-stable than Wildcat / Sender Free 118. Few better for fast, big skiing in steep techy terrain. Northwest 116 more versatile.
- **Prior Northwest 116** ("Quad-Glass"; carbon version quicker/uphill-friendly) -- 141.8-116.7-133.8 (190); 23 m; 2318 & 2377 g; 62/3/51; 188.9; -2.9 / 91.5; 178,184,190; maple + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Vs CBC: less tapered, longer radius -> floats better, blasts chop better, heavier swing weight, less surfy. For freestyle-background skiers going very fast in fresh and chop. Directional skiers -> Wildcat / Optic 114.
- **Rossignol Sender Free 118** (replaces all-time favorite Blackops 118; basically the same ski + "AirTip" insert and new name/graphic) -- 145.5-117.5-140.5 (186); 25 m; 2457 & 2533 g; 64/7/60; 184.2; -2.7 / 89.3; 176, 186; poplar + AirTip + fiberglass. Absurdly stable yet fairly playful; one of best skis ever for going really fast in soft chop/pow while balanced, easy sideways, forgiving of loose on-the-limit skiing. Not quick, doesn't float great for size, tiring; directional skiers mount a couple cm back. Few things more fun for those who click.
- **Atomic Bent Chetler 120** (repeat) -- Minor footprint-reduction tweaks; still very playful, quick, touring-friendly; less damp/stable in chop than most here but really surfy, nimble; capable for weight in soft snow; suspect new version a bit heavier/stiffer (not A/B tested yet). Revolt 121 similarly loose with damper build. Top pick for playfulness.
- **RMU Professor 121** -- 146.3-120.3-139.6 (188); 21 m; 2127 & 2161 g; 72/3/41; 186.5; -5 / 88.5; 178, 188; poplar + beech underfoot + fiberglass. Apostle 114 ingredients on wider freestyle platform for deep snow; soft deeply rockered ends -> surfy, forgiving; strong middle and wide footprint support imperfect landings (more than Bacon 115); versatile across terrain in deep snow; middle weight balances agility/suspension. Next ski more stable in rough; Apostle 114 / Professor 111 quicker/more versatile. Air time and slashes even tracked out.
- **Volkl Revolt 121** -- 142.5-120.4-134.2 (184); 19.2 m; 2240 & 2250 g; 87/4.5/53; 182.2; -2.8 / 88.3; 177,184,191; poplar/beech + fiberglass. Surfy, easy to slash but with suspension/support after snow is cut up; very tapered, deep rocker = extremely easy to pivot/slarve; strong middle = ski hard in chop. Few do both as well (Bent Chetler 120 looser, Sender Free 118 more stable). More versatile than width suggests; reasonable swing weight. Revolt 114 / Wildcat for directional.
- **Whitedot Ragnarok ASYM** -- 144.8-120.9-132.9 (190); 30 m; 2062 & 2080 g; 72/3/37; 187.8; -7.4 / 86.5; 190 only; poplar/ash + carbon stringers + fiberglass. More easygoing and intuitive than odd look suggests; "ASYM" = more tip taper on outside edge, less inside -> balanced weight distribution, easy skids/drifts. Dedicated pow tool, fun slarvy ride in blower or spring slush; "skis short," not easy to carve firm; nice suspension for low weight; compelling 50/50; stance-friendly, not best for switch landings in deep snow.
- **Black Crows Nocta** -- 147-121-136.5 (190.6); 19 m (all lengths); 2118 & 2124 g; 64/3.4/45; 187.5; -5.5 / 88; 177.6,185.5,190.6; paulownia/poplar + fiberglass. Black Crows' best for max deep-snow maneuverability; easy slarves/drifts without very deep rocker; prefers centered stance but drivable more than Revolt 121; fairly light, not a charger but above its weight in chop/crud/crust; predictable and fun in AK on steep faces and low angles; OK on skin track. Anima for more chop.
- **Heritage Lab HB122** (reincarnation of cult K2 Hellbent; near-identical shape) -- 153.5-122.5-147 (189); 26 m; 2420 & 2576 g; 82.5/0.5/71; 192.5; mounts Progressive -2.2 / 94, Standard -4.2 / 92, Traditional -6.2 / 90; 179, 189; bamboo/poplar + full-width VDS rubber + fiberglass. ~2500 g, extremely smooth ride; at ~-5 cm incredibly fun in almost all soft snow (slush to pow). Soft shovels can over-flex in very deep snow -> ~200 lb reviewer had OTB crashes; neutral or even backseat stance in deep solves it. Tail very supportive yet forgiving and loose -- unlike any other pow ski. AK reviewer's favorite for Alyeska inbounds.
- **K2 Reckoner 124** (replaces Reckoner 122; quite different) -- 149-123.5-147 (189); 25 m; 2423 & 2446 g; 85/3/68; 188.3; -5.3 / 88.8; 159-189; aspen + "spectral braid" + carbon stringers + fiberglass. Considerably more stable and versatile; very deep splayed rocker = very loose/surfy; substantial weight (189) adds suspension in chop; needs more effort at slow speed than 122 (momentum helps in tight terrain). Excels untracked (tricks or fall-line charging). Sender Free 118 better for post-storm chop.
- **Line Pescado** -- 157.5-124-144.5 (180); 19 m; 1808 & 1809 g; 66/5/22; 178.9; -9.8 / 79.6; 180 only; paulownia/maple + fiberglass. Outlier: very directional, light swallow-tail fish encouraging playful style; blast in soft snow esp. moderate terrain, tight trees, mellow slopes (big soft shovels, low weight); fun in slush, carves soft groomers surprisingly, pops off features, very quick. Rearward mount for forward stance; not balanced in air or for switch. Excellent pow touring ski in low-angle/avalanche-conservative terrain.
- **Armada ARG II UL** (repeat) -- One of very few skis so focused on deep untouched pow; among least versatile in guide but in its conditions an entirely different experience, ludicrously easy to pivot/slash/surf; niche, not intuitive on firm; best for quiver owners; truly special for untracked laps by lift/cat/heli/foot.
(Photo: Luke Koppa, Crested Butte.)

**Blister Spectrums -- Powder More Playful** (top -> bottom):
- Most Surfy/Loose: ARG II UL, Ragnarok ASYM, Revolt 121, QST X, Bent Chetler 120, Nocta, HB122, CBC, Reckoner 124, Professor 121, ARV 112, Apostle 114, Anima, QST Blank, Optic 114, Wildcat (184), Bacon 115, Nomad 112, Leeper, Altum 114, Unleashed 114, Northwest 116, Inthayne, Oblivion 116, Pescado, Sender Free 118, Wildcat (190) (least).
- Lightest in the Air -> Heaviest: Pescado, QST X, Bent Chetler 120, Oblivion 116, ARV 112, Apostle 114, Nocta, ARG II UL, Unleashed 114, CBC, Ragnarok ASYM, Altum 114, Professor 121, Nomad 112, Wildcat (184), Revolt 121, Leeper, Inthayne, Bacon 115, QST Blank, Optic 114, Anima, Wildcat (190), Northwest 116, Sender Free 118, Reckoner 124, HB122.
- Most Stable in Chop: Wildcat (190), Sender Free 118, HB122, Reckoner 124, Optic 114, Anima, Northwest 116, Wildcat (184), Revolt 121, QST Blank, Leeper, Altum 114, Nomad 112, Inthayne, Nocta, CBC, Bacon 115, Professor 121, Apostle 114, Oblivion 116, ARV 112, Unleashed 114, Bent Chetler 120, QST X, Ragnarok ASYM, ARG II UL, Pescado (least).

## 3. Ski-Quiver Selections (article, p.198)
"Blister Ski-Quiver Menu": suggestions for one- or two-ski quivers (and building larger ones); can't cover every scenario (hence Blister+ 1-on-1). Reviewers' personal quivers on the website.
- **One-ski quiver (resort)**, narrowest to widest, with ability ranges: Blizzard Black Pearl 88 (low-intermediate to advanced); Folsom Spar 88 (int-expert); Renoun Endurance 88 (int-expert); Salomon QST 92 (beginner-expert); Nordica Santa Ana 92 (adv-expert); Black Crows Serpo (high-int-expert); Blizzard Black Pearl 94 (adv-expert); Volkl M7 Mantra & Secret 96 (high-int-expert); Elan Ripstick 96 & Ripstick 94 W (low-int-expert); Faction Dancer 2 (high-int-expert); Wagner Summit 97 (int-expert); Fischer Nightstick 97 (beginner-expert); Peak 98 by Bode (int-expert); 4FRNT MSP 99 & MSP CC (high-int-expert); J Skis Masterblaster (beginner-expert); K2 Mindbender 99Ti & 99Ti W (int-expert); Romp Zorro 100 (beginner-advanced); Dynastar M-Pro 100 Ti & M-Pro 98 W (high-int-expert); Atomic Bent 100 (low-int-expert); Rossignol Sender Soul 102 (beginner-expert); Parlor Cardinal Pro 102 (high-int-expert); Armada Declivity X 102 Ti (high-int-expert); Salomon Stance 102 (high-int-expert); Line Optic 104 (low-int-expert); Nordica Enforcer 104 & Santa Ana 102 (int-expert); Whitedot Altum 104 (int-expert); Shaggy's Ahmeek 105 (low-int-expert); Icelantic Nomad 106 & Maiden 102 (low-int-expert); RMU Apostle 106 (beginner-expert); Salomon QST 106 & QST Stella 106 (low-int-expert); Season Nexus (low-int-expert); K2 Reckoner 110 (int-expert); Folsom Giver (int-expert); Moment Deathwish (int-expert).
- **Two-ski quiver (resort), lower-snow areas** (narrow ski -> wide partner options): The Curv -> Enforcer 94 / Mindbender 99Ti / Stormrider 102; Wingman 86 CTi -> M-Pro 100 Ti / Nomad 106 / QST 106; Mirus Cor -> Optic 96 / Apostle 106 / Reckoner 110; Spar 88 -> Stance 102 / Ahmeek 105 / Folsom Giver; Anomaly 88 -> Mantra 102 / Enforcer 104 / Summit 107; Maven 93 C -> Pandora 99 / Maven 103 CTI / Cash 106; Enforcer 94 -> Harfang 106 / Summit 107 / Mantra 108; Black Pearl 94 -> Santa Ana 102 / Summit 106 / Sheeva 11; Stance 96 -> Oso 105 / J Hotshot / Countach 110; M7 Mantra -> Enforcer 104 / Summit 107 / MSP 107; Camox Birdie -> QST Stella 106 / ARW 106 / Valhalla 107; Pandora 99 -> Nomad 106 / Mindbender 106C / Meier Leeper; Studio 2 -> Sender Free 110 / Northwest 110 / Deathwish.
- **Two-ski (resort), higher-snow areas**: Anomaly 88 -> Enforcer 104 / Mantra 108 / Revolt 114; Mindbender 89Ti -> Ranger 108 / Rustler 11 / Unleashed 114; Santa Ana 92 -> Summit 106 / Sheeva 11 / Mindbender 116C; Stormrider 95 -> McFellon Pro / Maverick 115 CTI / Anima; Optic 96 -> Nomad 112 / Optic 114 / Wildcat; M7 Mantra -> Rustler 11 / Revolt 114 / Kore 117; Mindbender 99Ti W -> Sheeva 11 / Rafiki / MB116C W; Mindbender 99Ti -> QST Blank / Nomad 112 / Folsom Rapture; Sheeva 10 -> Sheeva 11 / Rafiki / MB116C W; Stance 102 -> Countach 110 / Anima / Wildcat; Altum 104 -> Apostle 114 / Anima / Sender Free 118; Apostle 106 -> Reckoner KF / Professor 121 / Revolt 121; QST 106 -> Maverick 115 CTI / Anima / Wildcat.
- **Two-ski (1 resort + 1 touring)**: Spar 88 -> Superwolf / Peak 98 by Dav / QST Echo 106; Serpo -> Vital 98 / Ripstick Tour 104 / Vision 104; Optic 96 -> Weston Summit Carbon / Bent 110 / Vision 114; M-Pro 98 W -> Wagner Summit 106 / Intention 108 / Backland 108 W; Mindbender 99Ti -> Wagner Summit 105 / QST Echo 106 / Backland 109; MSP 99 -> Agent 3 / Radical 107 / Intention 108; Mantra 102 -> Ronin 108 / La Machine 3 / Ragnarok Carbonlite; Altum 104 -> Vision 104 / Wildcat Tour / Bent Chetler 120; Enforcer 104 -> 4FRNT Raven / QST Echo 106 / Norse Freeride.
- **One ski for everything (resort + backcountry)**: QST 92; Serpo; Ripstick 94 W & Ripstick 96; Pandora 99; Zorro 100; Bent 100; Sheeva 10; Liberty Scope 104; Altum 104; Black Crows Atris; Head Kore 105; DPS Kaizen 105; Apostle 106; Wagner Summit 106; Cardiff Goat 108 Enduro; WNDR Intention 108; J Skis Slacker; Majesty Havoc 110 Carbon; Norse Freeride; Moment Deathwish.
(Ad: Bluebird Mountain Sports (formerly Ski Tech), Santa Fe NM -- bootfitter Charlie Bradley, 30+ years, Masterfit instructor 20+ years; demo fees credited.)

### Ski Boots (p.202)
Section overview: best way to find boots is an experienced bootfitter -- feet differ; fit + ability + foot biomechanics matter; also how/where you ski. Three classes: (1) Alpine (fixed cuff, no walk mode); (2) Alpine/Touring "AT" (alpine boots with walk mode -- some good for touring, some just ease walking to lift); (3) Touring (lighter, better range of motion, all tech-binding compatible, usually less ideal for frequent resort laps due to downhill performance/durability). Manufacturers making lighter boots in all categories; trade-offs as with skis. Flex terms: "progressive" = gets stiffer as you flex forward; "linear" = resistance stays similar. Mostly reviewed stiffest versions (e.g., 130); notes generally apply to softer (120) versions. Specs given: stated flex, stated last width, sole type, measured weight per boot.
(Photo: skier Drew Petersen, Blister Summit 2024. Ad: Skiboot RX, Billings MT -- only Masterfit bootfitter in area.)

**Alpine boots**
- **Head Raptor WCR 140S** (unchanged) -- flex 140-150 adjustable; 96 mm; Alpine sole; 2360 g (26.5). Top pick for smooth suspension, precision, powerful flex; current (since 22/23) has extended toe box, adjusted pivots, slightly more forgiving spine; low-volume, roomier than Redster CS 130 and esp. Dobermann 5. For experts; Fischer RC4 Pro if fit doesn't work.
- **Atomic Redster CS 130** -- 130; 96 mm; Alpine; 2209 g. Race-derived but more accessible fit/flex; one of lowest-volume here, but anatomical last, heat-moldable shell, Mimic liner (and aftermarket "Professional" liner) make fit easier than true race boot; very damp, laterally stiff. Hawx series lighter/softer. Max-performance daily resort boot.
- **Nordica Dobermann 5 S** (Dobermanns updated last season: slimmer lasts, bi-injected cuffs, shorter soles) -- 120-140 adjustable; 96 mm; Alpine; 2304 g. Stiffest of 96mm-last "recreational race" line; race-boot feel, more accommodating than plug but much lower volume than 98 boots; comparable support/suspension/precision to Redster CS 130 / Raptor WCR 140 (soften by removing spine bolts); lower volume esp. instep and toe box -- one of most precise-fitting here.
- **Fischer RC4 PRO LV** -- 140; 98 mm; GripWalk; 2025 g. Top of non-race line: higher-end plastics, carbon-reinforced cuffs, big power straps, ZipFit cork liners (moldable/add/remove for years). Legit 140; not quite as smooth as heavier boots nor as low-volume; pricey but liner outlasts most stock liners by seasons.
- **Fischer RC4 PRO MV BOA** -- 140; 100 mm; GripWalk; 2170 g. Same as LV but medium/high-volume with BOA; notably roomy in midfoot/toe box; add cork to take up ankle volume; quite stiff, damper than lighter alternatives; BOA gives fine-tunable, uniform pressure. Among best for roomy fit with real support/customizability.
- **Salomon S/Pro Race 140** (new) -- 140; 96 mm; Alpine; 1933 g. S/Pro Alpha design with stronger flex and lower-volume last; fit less precise than Redster CS / Dobermann 5; try if low-volume but high instep; "strong 130 or soft 140"; light for flex, not as damp as heavier boots but not outlier.
- **Fischer RC4 Pro MV DYN BOA ZF** -- 130; 100 mm; GripWalk + tech toe inserts; 2219 g (27.5). Same as RC4 Pro MV but tech toe inserts only, moldable "Vacuum" plastic, lighter parts -- skin uphill in hybrid bindings (Marker Duke PT, Salomon Shift, CAST Freetour) with fixed-cuff downhill performance; ZipFit liner; at least legit 130, much stiffer than next boot.
- **K2 Recon 140 BOA** -- 140; 97-100 mm; GripWalk + tech toe; 1653 g (25.5). Fixed-cuff with tech toe for pin-uphill bindings (Duke PT, Shift, CAST); one of lightest fixed-cuff boots; softer than any other 140 tested and several stiff 130s, but slight upgrade over Recon 130 (BOA liner, cuff "power plate"); adaptable low-medium fit.
- **Kastle K130P LV** (Kastle entered boots in 22/23; GEAR:30 Ep.250) -- 130; 97 mm; Alpine; 1996 g. Race-inspired, one of lowest-volume here, powerful; flex/energy like RC4 Pro LV; Redster CS / Raptor better suspension; fit more precise than some 96 boots; also K130P 100 mm and race "R" models. Give up a bit of damping for lighter setup.
- **Tecnica Mach1 LV 130 TD** -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1983 g. Stiffest/lowest-volume Mach1; suspension below Raptor / Redster but above Promachine 130 / Recon 140; forward support like Promachine with stiffer initial flex. For those between light and heavy boots, or those finding 98 boots roomy but can't fit 93-96. MV/HV versions.
- **Lange Shadow 130 LV** -- 130; 97 mm; GripWalk; 1977 g. Go-to alpine boot for two reviewers over multiple seasons; unique pivot layout transfers energy differently -- noticeably easier to bend skis (tested one foot vs traditional boot); takes adjustment, can be addictive; very nice suspension without heavy weight; considerably higher volume than most "LV" boots; MV also offered.
- **Head Formula 130 LV** -- 130-140 adjustable (extra spine bolt -> 140); 98 mm; GripWalk; 2052 g. Raptor-like performance with more accommodating lasts, easier customization, lighter builds; easier to flex than Raptor, easier initial flex than Mach1 LV then ramps progressively; plush liner + moderately heavy shell = smooth/damp; legit low-volume with slightly longer toe box.
- **Rossignol Hi-Speed Elite 130 Carbon LV** -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 2053 g. Nicely progressive, easy to initiate, ramps smoothly; softer off top than Mach1 but stiffens faster than Recon 130 / Speedmachine 130; roomier at instep and toe box than most LV; liner customization at ankle, 1st/5th metatarsals, instep; better vibration muting than lighter boots. MV/HV variants.
- **Nordica Promachine 130** -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1890 g. Lighter, low-volume, supportive, precise; flexes like Mach1 LV 130 but lighter; less smooth/progressive than Raptor/Redster/Hi-Speed; more supportive than Recon 130. Alternative if Mach1 LV doesn't fit; consider Promachine 120 if unsure about 130; Speedmachine for higher volume.
- **Salomon S/Pro Alpha 130** -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1812 g. Roomier instep (vs old S/Max) with heel hold via revised buckle layout, low elsewhere; nicely progressive; Mach1 LV / Promachine slightly stiffer and smoother; highly customizable (adjustable tongue, heat-moldable liner and shell). S/Pro Race 140 more support/precise fit.
- **Tecnica Mach1 MV 130 TD** -- 130; 100 mm; GripWalk; 2037 g. Hundreds of days (managing editor's feet); minor stiffness drop vs LV; one of stiffest MV boots, esp. initial flex; balance of damping vs weight; durability issues with shell after 100+ days (see full review); otherwise excellent daily driver. Mach1 MV Concept has carbon cuff.
- **Atomic Hawx Ultra 130 S** -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1786 g. Atomic started lightweight-alpine trend; current is best yet; less supportive/damp than Redster CS 130 but smoother, more progressive than similarly light boots; distinctive rebound (loved by some), less polarizing than original; highly customizable (heat-moldable shell, Mimic liner; GEAR:30 #88 & #132); one of lowest-volume 98 boots pre-customization. XTD version for touring.
- **Atomic Hawx Prime 130 S BOA** (updated; buckle or BOA; reinforced shell) -- 130; 100 mm; GripWalk; stated 1887 g. Medium-volume Hawx aligned with Ultra performance; preliminary: fairly light, nicely progressive, stiffer than Recon 130 / Speedmachine 3 130; average MV last; customizable shell/liner + BOA.
- **K2 Recon 130 BOA** -- 130; 97-100 mm; GripWalk; 1847 g. Lightweight low-medium volume; BOA lower closure uniform and micro-adjustable; flex softer, more linear, ramps less than most 130s; Recon 140 stiffer; Mindbender boots same shell + walk mode.
- **Nordica Speedmachine 3 130 S** -- 130; 100 mm; GripWalk; 1976 g. Generous MV fit; one of softer 130s, very easy to initiate; comfortable, warm liner; better vibration muting than Recon 130; much easier flex and roomier (esp. midfoot) than Promachine. New Speedmachine BOA series.
- **Rossignol Vizion 4B Elite 130 LV GW** (new) -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk; 2053 g. Addresses on/off difficulty: lever unlocks cuff from shell -- step in/out hands-free; notably softer than most 130s, linear then ramps late. For softer-flex fans who dread donning boots; Hi-Speed for strong flex.
- **Phaenom FS 01 120** (new brand; also walk-mode FR 01 130) -- 120; 102 mm; GripWalk; 2055 g. Hybrid shell blending 2-piece overlap and 3-piece cabrio; very progressive, smooth; stiffer than soft 130s above, more forgiving initial flex than legit 2-piece 130s; wide last but low-instep reviewers got secure heel hold via simple 2-buckle layout; big elastic power strap; replaceable parts.
- **Rossignol Pure Elite 120** (women's) -- 120; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1707 g (24.5). Among stiffest women-specific boots; notably progressive; damp; low-volume overall but roomier instep/toe box than Promachine / Mach1 LV; similar fit to Lange Shadow 115 LV (which ramps earlier; Pure Elite stiffer deep); removable spine bolts soften it -- suits progressing intermediates to experts.
- **Nordica Promachine 115 W** -- 115; 98 mm; GripWalk; stated 1690 g. Particularly low-volume; supportive, precise, energetic; snugger at ankle/instep than Shadow LV, K2 Anthem BOA, Dalbello Cabrio LV; stiffer than K2 Anthem 115 and Fischer RC4 105. For aggressive skiers avoiding race plugs who find unisex 130s too stiff.
- **Tecnica Mach1 LV 115 W** -- 115; 98 mm; GripWalk; 1727 g. Stiffest, narrowest non-race women's Tecnica; very supportive, above-average suspension; legit low-volume (instep, midfoot, heel); like Hawx Ultra 115 W / Promachine 115 W fit but stiffer initial flex and better harsh-snow suspension. Mach1 MV 115 W for wider; Cochise 115 W for touring.
- **Lange Shadow 115 W LV** -- 115; 97 mm; GripWalk; 1769 g. Same unique shell/pivot as men's, more accessible flex; exceptional power transmission without harshness; fit like Lange RS LV/old RX LV with snugger heel; not as low-volume as Mach1 LV / Promachine / Hawx Ultra (esp. instep), similar to Pure Elite. Efficient power, smooth suspension, snappy rebound.
- **Atomic Hawx Ultra 115 W S** -- 115; 98 mm; GripWalk; stated 1621 g. Low-volume, strong; Mimic liner and heat-moldable shell; cuff shorter than men's but taller than many women's boots (more leverage); one of lowest-volume, like Mach1 LV 115 / Promachine 115 (heat-molding opens midfoot); flex slightly softer, progressive, light.
- **Dalbello Cabrio LV 115 W 3DWrap** (Dalbello revamped 3-piece boots last season) -- 115; 99 mm; GripWalk; 1727 g. Stiffest women's Cabrio; fairly low-volume except more accommodating instep; heat-molded 3DWrap liner cushions and removes shin slop; pivoting tongue eases entry; linear 3-piece flex (stiffer off top, less stiff deep than 2-piece).
- **K2 Anthem 115 W BOA** (women's Recon counterpart) -- 115; 97-100 mm; GripWalk; 1730 g. BOA + wrapping shell let last range 97-100; low-volume reviewer got snug fit; works for medium volume too (heat-moldable). Predictable, smooth flex, not stiffest 115 but close. Anthem 125 BOA for more support.
- **Nordica Speedmachine 3 115 W** -- 115; 100 mm; GripWalk; stated 1700 g. Roomier women's boot; more forgiving flex, less effort to initiate (forgiving in bumps/crud); higher volume than Promachine/Hawx Ultra/Mach1 LV but nice ankle pocket and not-high instep. For wide feet needing more than ~100 flex support.
- **Fischer RC4 105 MV BOA** -- 105; 100 mm; GripWalk; 1686 g (24.5). Very customizable "Vacuum" plastic, still supportive; BOA let ~98-last reviewers get secure hold despite MV last/roomy toe; roomier than LV boots, less than next; flex feels like Promachine 115 W / Mach1 LV 115 W -- stiffest of MV women's alpine boots.
- **Salomon S/Pro Supra BOA 105 W** -- 105; 100-106 mm; GripWalk; 1772 g (25.5). Generous fit, fairly light, highly adjustable lower shell -- best chance here for high-volume feet; BOA gives even snugness; decent suspension; heat-moldable shell/liner; adjustable spine "direct" vs "progressive." S/Pro Alpha 130 available down to 22.5 for stiff low-volume.
(Photo: Eric "Hoji" Hjorleifson testing boots at Blister Summit 2024.)

**Alpine / Touring (AT) boots**
- **Scarpa 4-Quattro Pro** (newest, stiffest Scarpa) -- 130+; 100 mm (feels low-volume); GripWalk w/ tech inserts; 1531 g. Downhill-focused 4-Quattro XT; one of stiffest walk-mode boots tested; still walks very well (bit less ROM than XT), lighter than many AT boots; less smooth/plush in firm rough snow than heavier boots.
- **Dalbello Cabrio LV Free 130 3DWrap** -- 130; 99 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1885 g. Latest Dalbello "free touring" 3-piece (Lupo successor); more solid cuff-shell connection in ski mode; dual-density tongue adds ROM without removal; skis like fixed-cuff 3-piece; linear flex absorbs mid-travel forces, ramps less than 2-piece; wrap liner limits ROM but snug cuff fit. Roxa R3 lighter. Resort boot viable for short tours.
- **Tecnica Cochise 130** -- 130; 99 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1832 g. Original helped establish category; latest most well-rounded; generous ROM (stated 50 deg) yet feels like lightweight fixed-cuff alpine boot inbounds (PU shell, burly liner). Zero G Tour Pro lighter/walks better. One of best 1-boot quivers for aggressive skiers; now MV and HV lasts.
- **Lange XT3 Free 130 LV** -- 130; 97 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1777 g. Downhill-priority AT; light/mobile enough for fairly long tours; skis much better than light touring boots (PU shell, thick liner, tall cuff); fit like Lange RS LV/Shadow LV; MV version. XT3 Tour 2.0 similar downhill with more uphill focus; Hawx Ultra XTD if Lange LV too roomy.
- **Atomic Hawx Ultra XTD 130 BOA** -- 130; 98 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1791 g. Extremely compelling 1-boot option; heavier than earlier XTDs, big downhill and suspension gains; shockingly small difference from fixed-cuff Hawx Ultra 130; stated 54 deg ROM; less ideal for extended tours than Backland XTD. Adaptable fit, strong flex, smooth ride.
- **Salomon Shift Alpha BOA 130** (new) -- 130; 98-104 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1799 g. Lower-volume last than Shift Pro AT but BOA + heat-moldable shell for medium feet; new walk mechanism and instep strap like S/Lab MTN Summit -> impressive heel hold and respectable ROM; a bit softer off top, quicker rebound, slightly less damp than previous three (subtle); drives big stiff skis; strong 1-boot quiver.
- **Dynafit Tigard 130** -- 130; 101 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1702 g (27.5). Dynafit's most downhill boot; lighter than most rivals; drives big skis fast, a bit softer/less progressive than Cochise 130; "Hoji-Lock" loosens whole upper cuff in one motion (reviewers still adjusted buckles depending on leg shape); excellent fore-aft ROM for 4-buckle AT; one of more comfortable to skin in.
- **Roxa R3 130 T.I.** -- 130; 99-101 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1580 g. Hard to categorize: lighter than some touring boots, stiffer than most; 3-piece with linear strong flex and upright stance; remove tongue for >1-hour climbs for big ROM; stiff setting and less forward lean polarizing; tongue- or wrap-style Intuition liner (wrap better for resort), heat moldable. Uncommonly versatile; Cabrio LV Free smoother.
- **Fischer Ranger Pro 130** -- 130; 99 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1772 g. Middle-of-road fit/weight/stiffness; internal walk mechanism with respectable ROM; a bit softer and more linear than most 130 2-piece boots; muted slow rebound preferred by aggressive reviewers for BC over resort; lighter alternative to next boot.
- **K2 Mindbender 130 BOA** -- 130; 97-100 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1961 g. Recon/Anthem with walk mode; low-volume out of box but BOA Fit System and heat-moldable shell accommodate medium feet; flex nearly identical to Recon 130, more linear than Cochise 130 / Hawx Ultra XTD; average ROM; thick alpine liner + PU shell less harsh. Resort-oriented AT.
- **Atomic Hawx Ultra XTD 115 BOA W** -- 115; 98 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1616 g. Stiffest/narrowest women's Atomic touring boot; skis like fixed-cuff 115s; respectable ROM; less harsh than light touring boots; low-volume but easy to customize. Cochise 115 W or Hawx Prime XTD 115 BOA W for roomier.
- **Tecnica Cochise 115 W** (unchanged; reviewer Kara Williard's long-time boot) -- 115; 99 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1643 g. Excellent 1-boot quiver for hard inbounds skiers who tour some; more medium volume at instep/midfoot/toe than Mindbender / Hawx XTD, slim ankle (HV version too); stiffer than several fixed-cuff women's alpine boots. Zero G Tour Pro W for more touring.
- **Scarpa 4-Quattro XT W** -- 115; 100 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1323 g (24.5). Very capable uphill (like touring boots) with AT-like forward stiffness, slightly poorer suspension; light, high ROM; for low-volume feet. Scarpa Gea RS for more volume and mobility.
- **Salomon Shift Alpha BOA 115 W** (redesigned from ground up: last, BOA, walk mode) -- 115; 98-104 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1665 g. More versatile fit and walks better than predecessor; slightly less supportive/damp than Cochise 115 W / Ranger 115 but close to 115 alpine boots; walks a bit better than those; heat-moldable. Similar to Mindbender 115 BOA W but lighter, ramps quicker. Shift Alpha 130 available to 22.5.
- **Fischer Ranger 115** -- 115; 99 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1641 g. Fixed-cuff-like ride at competitive weight; walk lever under upper buckle; walks like Cochise 115 / Mindbender 115; stiffer than Mindbender 115, comparable to Cochise 115 W; more uniformly low-volume than Cochise. Transalp series for more touring.
- **K2 Mindbender 115 W BOA** -- 115; 97-100 mm; GripWalk w/ tech; 1858 g. Most versatile-fitting version: BOA on shell plus BOA on liner -> snug heel/ankle and less movement on ascent (adds weight); robust heat-moldable liner and shell; slightly less damp/softer than Cochise 115 W / Ranger 115; similar uphill; more plush than 4-Quattro XT W. K2 Dispatch LT W for touring.

**Touring boots**
- **La Sportiva Vanguard** -- 130; 102.5 mm; touring sole w/ tech; 1484 g. Downhill-oriented touring boot balancing weight/stiffness/mobility; hybrid 3-piece hinged shell (like Scarpa Maestrale RS) gives ROM without removable tongue; one of lighter 130s; performs near its rating; roomier fit -- alternative to next two lower-volume boots.
- **Lange XT3 Tour 2.0 130** -- 130; 99 mm; touring sole w/ tech; stated 1450 g. XT3 Free design in lighter materials; walk mechanism nearly identical; ROM below Zero G / Maestrale but pretty good, with excellent rearward support; standout predictable progressive flex rebounding like heavier non-Grilamid boots; full-rubber Vibram sole for rock scrambling. Familiar feel for 2-piece alpine boot users.
- **Tecnica Zero G Tour Pro** (second iteration for 24/25) -- 130; 99 mm; touring w/ tech; 1354 g (27.5). Class leader in "freeride touring"; slightly stiffer, a bit more fore/aft ROM, slightly more instep and toe room while keeping heel hold; otherwise very similar. One of most impressive downhill-to-weight boots tested.
- **K2 Dispatch Pro** -- 130; (continues).
  - (Dispatch Pro cont.) 100 mm; touring w/ tech; 1568 g (27.5). Stiffest Dispatch; well-rounded touring boot with strong flex for those not needing lightest; light breathable BOA liner adjustable on the go; Pebax shell/cuff -> progressive, respectably stiff (softer than previous few, much more supportive than <1300 g boots). No class-leading trait but no shortcomings; alpine-like flex without AT weight. Zero G Tour Pro for lower volume/more support.
- **Scarpa Maestrale RS** -- 125; 102 mm; touring w/ tech; 1380 g. Easy to recommend: light, walks well, skis aggressively; not quite as stiff as Zero G Tour Pro / Vanguard but drives big skis in variable; progressive flex and lateral support feel familiar vs uphill boots like F1 LT; roomier fit though 102 last feels smaller (ankle/instep); slightly above-average uphill for freetouring class.
- **Dynafit Radical Pro** (effectively succeeds Hoji Pro Tour) -- 120; 103.5 mm; touring w/ tech; 1483 g. More supportive, progressive than many <1300 g boots, better-than-average ROM for 120-130 touring boots; "Hoji Lock" can speed transitions (biomechanics-dependent). Forward flex below previous few but laterally stiffer than next two. Flexes like 120 alpine boot yet comfortable skinning.
- **Atomic Backland XTD Carbon 120** -- 120; 100 mm; touring w/ tech; 1372 g. Bridges uphill Backlands and downhill Hawx XTD; "120" accurate forward; notably less laterally rigid/precise than other 120-130s here; nearly endless ROM feels much nicer uphill; "goldilocks" for those worried ~1000 g boots need too much adjustment.
- **Dalbello Quantum Free Pro** -- 130; 100 mm; touring w/ tech; 1367 g (27.5). Positioned closer to light high-ROM boots; forward flex notably softer, very linear until deep, considerably less powerful than similar weights (Zero G Tour Pro, Ridge Pro, even S/Lab MTN Summit); respectable lateral power; plenty of ROM. Fit: extremely low instep, moderately wide heel/toe. Ridge Pro / MTN Summit for better downhill.
- **Dynafit Ridge Pro** (new; preliminary -- late-spring testing) -- 120; 101 mm; touring w/ tech; 1376 g (27.5). Lower volume than Tigard esp. instep; excellent fore-aft ROM competitive with <1300 g boots; latest Hoji Lock (still some buckle adjustment); 200+ lb reviewer drove 117 mm ski in spring snow. Likely competitive between stiff/low-ROM and light/soft/laterally weak boots.
- **Tecnica Zero G Tour Pro W** (new women's Pro; 125 flex) -- 125; 99 mm; touring w/ tech; 1165 g (24.5). Step up from Zero G Tour Scout (115); revised last, ~10% more ROM, improved lateral stiffness; a tad roomier toe/instep; as good or better than original. No other women's touring boot this supportive at this weight.
- **Scarpa Gea RS** -- 120; 102 mm; touring w/ tech; 1270 g (25.0). Comfortable on skin tracks (stated 61 deg ROM, smooth) with downhill not far from alpine boots; uphill like Zero G Tour Pro W but softer forward flex; slightly wider last; ratchet ankle strap, minimal lower buckle cable; heat-moldable Intuition liner.
- **K2 Dispatch LT W** -- 115; 100 mm; touring w/ tech; 1351 g (24.5). Safe bet downhill-oriented women's touring boot; stated 60 deg ROM; fairly supportive; middle-of-curve mix; medium volume throughout (much roomier than Zero G Tour Pro W); heat-moldable shell/liner incl. higher volume feet. Zero G Tour Pro W / Gea RS stiffer and lighter.
- **Salomon S/Lab MTN Summit** -- 120; 99 mm; touring w/ tech; 1166 g. Blurs categories: almost limitless 75 deg ROM, sub-1200 g, walks incredibly well yet skis quite well; fairly progressive; reviewers skeptical of "120" but agree it's close. Best in smooth consistent snow. Medium volume. More supportive flex than nearly any boot with similar ascent mobility. Ridge Pro / Backland XTD 120 softer off top, plusher liner.
- **Tecnica Zero G Peak Carbon** -- flex not listed; 99 mm; tech-binding-only sole; 1077 g (27.5). ~1 kg class: efficient, low-friction stride; stiff enough to drive fat skis in soft snow; not drastically better than Scarpa F1 LT but a bit lighter and competitive downhill. MTN Summit slightly stiffer with wider toe; Zero G Tour line for alpine-like skiing.
(Photo: Blister Summit 2024 attendees.)
- **Atomic Backland Carbon** -- 110; 98 mm; tech only; 1112 g (28). Between skimo and alpine-feel boots; quick closure/walk mode, streamlined, walks very, very well; enough power from neutral stance; surprisingly progressive; open-throat lower gives up forward/lateral support; fully heat-moldable shell and liner -> one of most fit-versatile. Backland XTD Carbon 120 for harder skiing.
